## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-18 (user-attached ingest) — **THE PRICE WAR GETS A STATED PURPOSE AND A STATED BILL FROM THE SELLER'S OWN PLAN: [[OPENAI]] *"SLASHED PRICES AS IT SEEKS TO WIN BUSINESS FROM US RIVAL [[ANTHROPIC]] AND SEE OFF THE THREAT FROM CHEAPER, 'OPEN-WEIGHT' MODELS FROM CHINA"* — INSIDE A PLAN OF $36bn → $350bn REVENUE AND −$278bn OF CUMULATIVE FCF 2026–30**

(Financial Times · George Hammond / James Fontanella-Khan, *"OpenAI expects to burn $280bn by 2030"*, 2026-09-18, relaying an OpenAI internal investor presentation. ⚠️ **Journalism relaying a company plan, not a disclosure; no per-token price, margin or volume figure in the piece.** [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260918-FT-Hammond-OpenAI-expects-to-burn-280bn-by-2030.html) · reconciliation: [`../_meta/reconciliation-2026-09-18.md`](../_meta/reconciliation-2026-09-18.md))

- 🔴🔴 **WHAT IT ADDS TO THIS PAGE'S CENTRAL EQUATION:** the 09-08/09 block has Hock Tan splitting **~$200bn/yr of industry spend against ~$150bn of model revenue**; the 09-08 block has OpenAI's CFO measuring a **10x usage response to the ~80% Luna cut** and asking to be judged on cost per task. **The FT supplies the seller's own five-year P&L shape behind those two facts: revenue tenfold to $350bn, ~$856bn of compute + infrastructure spend, −$278bn of FCF — and a plan that IMPROVED by ~$27bn between May (−$305bn) and now while the July run-rate grew ~20% m/m.** ➤ **Price cuts that expand volume faster than they compress revenue is the elasticity result this page logged on 09-08; the FT's framing shows the company planning on it for five years.**
- 🔴 **THE OPEN-WEIGHT LEG, NAMED BY THE COMPANY'S PLAN:** the cuts are also aimed at *"cheaper, 'open-weight' models from China"* — the same axis as the GLM-5.3 Flash parity mark on [[OPENAI]] (Artificial Analysis score 57 = GPT-5.6 Terra, at $0.15 / $0.50 per 1m tokens) and the 09-16 DeepSeek architecture block on this page. ⚠️ **Whether the −$278bn is burn to *win* share or burn to *defend* it is the question the FT cannot answer and this page should not guess.**
- 🔭 **HOUSE INFERENCE, NOT IN THE SOURCE:** $350bn of OpenAI revenue in 2030 would be ~38% of the house AI Labs Revenue MAP's ~$920bn model-layer pool; with Anthropic's own trajectory the two frontier labs would exceed half of it. **Either the pool is too small or the labs' plans embed share the MAP does not grant — a scenario line to add to the MAP, not a contradiction to resolve here.** Full numbers and the two superseded marks on [[OPENAI]].

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-18 (user-attached ingest) — **TOKEN RESALE FINALLY GETS MEASURED: A DATA PLATFORM GROWING 80% IS GROWING ~65% EX-TOKEN-RESALE, SO ~15 POINTS OF ITS GROWTH IS BOUGHT-AND-RESOLD FRONTIER TOKENS — *"VOLUME SET BY MACHINES RATHER THAN BY PEOPLE."* AND MORGAN STANLEY PUTS THE FIRST FULL PER-QUERY MONETISATION MODEL ON A CONSUMER AGENT: 100mn MUSE USERS ≈ $1.3bn OF REVENUE AND 1% OF '28 EPS.**

_(**Morgan Stanley · Sanjit K Singh / Adam Wood / Jamie Reynolds** — *"Databricks: First Data, Today AI, Next the Agentic Enterprise"*, **Foundation**, 2026-09-18, 40pp ([source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/DATABRICKS_20260918_0401.html)) ⚠️⚠️ **DATABRICKS IS PRIVATE — no rating, no price target, no coverage; MS states the note *"does not constitute a solicitation or imply future research coverage"* and is *"based on unaudited information."* Nothing here belongs to any covered name.** · **Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak / Matthew Cost / Nathan Feather / Erik W Woodring** — *"The Morgan Stanley Agentic Playbook"*, Internet / North America, **2026-09-18**, 15pp ([source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260918_Morgan_Stanley_META_Internet-_The_Morgan_Stanley_Agentic_Playbook.html)); ⚠️ **a RESTATEMENT note — MS says explicitly it wants *"to remind investors of 7 key points about our agentic frameworks"* — so most of it is already on this page from 09-08/09 and from the 2025-11-17 ancestor. Only the net-new arithmetic is logged below.** ✅ **NOTHING ON THIS PAGE IS SUPERSEDED: the $30tn consumer agentic TAM, the $50-60tn Guidebook TAM, the Muse token tiers, the Muse Spark $1.58/mn blended anchor, the 2,000-3,500 tokens/sec/GPU workload band and the revenue-per-GW ladder all stand unchanged. [[META]] ratings and PTs are untouched.**)_

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE TOKEN-RESALE NUMBER, AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME THIS PAGE CAN SIZE PASS-THROUGH TOKENS INSIDE A REAL P&L.** MS: Databricks *"disclosed a revenue run-rate above $7 billion as of July 2026, growing more than 80% Y/Y. **Excluding the LLM token-resale business, management indicated that core revenue grew roughly 65%; the difference reflects faster growth in a lower-margin pass-through business.**"* ➤ **~15 percentage points of an 80% growth rate — roughly a fifth of the growth — is tokens BOUGHT from frontier labs and RESOLD.** And MS names the mechanic precisely: *"the agent layer… Databricks is largely **buying tokens from frontier model providers, and reselling them inside its own governed perimeter**. This is a **pass-through business with a thinner spread than software**, and one where **the volume is set by machines rather than by people**."* ✅✅ **THIS QUANTIFIES, FROM A SECOND HOUSE, THE CLAIM UBS's KEIRSTEAD MADE ON 09-03 AND THIS PAGE HAS CARRIED UNMEASURED SINCE: that Databricks is "chasing" the low-gross-margin token pass-through revenue [[SNOW]] declines. It is no longer a characterisation — it is ~15pts of growth and an identified margin drag.** ⚠️⚠️ **BASIS GUARD: "run-rate", "core revenue" and "token resale" are all MANAGEMENT-INDICATED and unaudited, with no dollar split published. The ~15pt figure is the difference of two rounded growth rates ("more than 80%" vs "roughly 65%") and is therefore a RANGE, not a point. Do not convert it into a token-resale revenue number.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE STRUCTURAL CLAIM UNDERNEATH IT, WHICH IS THIS PAGE'S THESIS STATED BY A SOFTWARE ANALYST RATHER THAN A SEMI ONE: *"For both Databricks and Snowflake, **agents can generate additional consumption through analytical queries, model calls and application workflows, including read-only use cases**… **Usage on either platform could therefore grow WITHOUT A CORRESPONDING INCREASE IN HUMAN USERS.**"*** MS gives the dual-metering worked example: *"[[SNOW]]'s **Cortex Agents… incur token-based charges, while the SQL queries they execute can also generate warehouse-compute charges**."* And the first-party corroboration MS quotes is the CEO already on this page from 09-17: Ghodsi at the June Summit — *"**the agents are generating way more queries… it just increases the consumption of everything all around**."* ➤➤ **Two meters firing off one agent action — a token meter at the model call and a compute meter at the query it triggers — is the cleanest published statement on this page of why agentic adoption is a MULTIPLIER on infrastructure consumption rather than a substitution within it. It is also the mechanism that makes "seats" the wrong denominator for this whole layer.** ⚠️ **MS attaches its own caveat and it should be carried: *"This does not establish a structural disadvantage for per-seat software vendors, which can also offer consumption-based or hybrid pricing."* An option, not a verdict.**
- 🔴 **AND THE MARGIN MECHANIC THAT DECIDES WHETHER RESELLING TOKENS IS A GOOD TRADE — MS separates it from the serverless shift, which this page had been conflating.** Two distinct forces on gross margin: **(a) classic → serverless**, where under classic *"the DBU charge sits on top of the customer's own cloud bill"* (two bills, non-GAAP subscription gross margin *"above 80%"*) and under serverless *"the hyperscaler's cost moves onto Databricks' own income statement"*; **(b) the agent layer**, i.e. the token pass-through above. MS's defence of (a) — *"gross profit dollars per workload can rise even as the percentage falls, because Databricks is now earning a margin on a bill it previously never touched at all"* — **does NOT apply to (b), where the platform is reselling someone else's commodity at a thin spread.** ➤ **For this page's standing question of who captures the token dollar: the data platform captures a spread on it and a second, fatter meter alongside it. The frontier lab still captures the token itself.**
- 🔴🔴 **MORGAN STANLEY PUTS A FULL PER-QUERY MONETISATION MODEL ON A CONSUMER AGENT — THE FIRST ON THIS PAGE, AND IT IS DELIBERATELY CONSERVATIVE.** The Muse build, as printed: **100mn MAUs × 5.0 queries/MAU/day (the same rate MS uses for [[GOOG]]) = 182,500mn annual queries × a 10% commercial-query rate (vs 20% for GOOGL) × $0.07 monetisation per commercial query (20% of GOOGL's US $0.36) = ~$1.3bn of revenue, ~$0.35 of incremental EPS, ~1% upside to '28 EPS**, at assumed *"80% incremental margins on new Muse-based revenue"*. The sensitivity: holding the 10% commercial rate and $0.07 constant, **3,000mn MAUs at 2.5 commercial queries/day = ~$20bn of '28 revenue and ~15% upside to '28 EPS**. MS's framing: *"While Muse could monetize with subscription revenue… the larger opportunity is around monetizable behavior and transactions. We expect META to eventually charge a commission for Muse enabled transactions… search CPCs are effectively real-time advertiser bid-based commissions."* ➤➤ **WHY IT BELONGS ON A TOKEN PAGE AND NOT ONLY ON [[META]]: this is the first published attempt anywhere in the corpus to convert AGENTIC QUERY VOLUME INTO REVENUE PER QUERY. Every number on this page above the application layer — $/mn tokens, tokens/sec/GPU, revenue/GW — is a supply-side unit. $0.07 per commercial query is the DEMAND-side unit those all have to reconcile to, and it is 20% of what the incumbent earns.** ⚠️ **Every input is MS's assumption, not a disclosure: MAUs, queries/day, commercial rate, monetisation rate and the 80% incremental margin are all assumed. It is a scalability illustration, explicitly labelled as such.**
- 🔴 **THE TOKENS-PER-QUERY BOUND THAT FALLS OUT OF THE SAME NOTE, AND THIS PAGE HAS NEVER HAD ONE FOR A CONSUMER AGENT.** MS on Muse's free tier: *"is high utility and free, with **100mn free weekly tokens (which could likely support 100+ queries per day)**."* ➤ **100mn tokens ÷ 7 days ÷ 100 queries ≈** **up to ~140k tokens per agentic consumer query** — ⚠️ *this page's arithmetic on MS's two printed figures, and an UPPER bound only, since MS writes "100+" queries.* **Set it against the API-side anchors already on this page — MS's own illustrative workload is 8K input / 1K output — and an agentic consumer query is being budgeted at something on the order of a hundred times a single chat turn, because it is a multi-step browsing-and-transacting session, not a completion. That ratio, not the price per token, is what makes consumer agents a capex story.** ⚠️ **MS gives no per-query token measurement; the free-tier allowance is a PRODUCT LIMIT, and users may not exhaust it. Do not model token cost off this.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE TAKE-RATE ARITHMETIC — MS finally publishes the offset, and this page flagged the un-offset version as "STILL the open question" as recently as the 2025-vintage ancestor note below.** As printed in the text (⚠️ *the accompanying Exhibit 6 bars are CHART VALUES and are deliberately not quoted here, per this wiki's standing rule on chart-deck label/value mispairing*): **current search per-transaction *"effective take rates" (ad spend as a percent of GMV) are ~5-10X higher than early agentic take rate offerings from earlier this year.*** The offset: ***"80%+ of retailer online traffic is still unpaid/direct… a 500bp shift in unpaid to paid traffic can offset a 1,400bp decline in effective take rates. However, it would require a 3,800bp shift from unpaid to paid channels for GOOGL to break even at a 5% take rate."*** ➤➤ **The 500bp/1,400bp ratio is the sentence to keep: roughly 2.8 points of take-rate decline can be absorbed per point of traffic converted from free to paid. It converts the agentic-disruption debate from a MULTIPLE question into a MEASURABLE FUNNEL question — and MS's own answer is that at a 5% take rate the required shift (3,800bp) is nearly half of all retailer direct traffic, i.e. implausible. The monetisation gap does not close on volume alone.** ⚠️ **MS's caveat, carried: *"We acknowledge take rates may change."* And the whole frame is MS's model of GOOGL's economics, not a disclosure.**
- 🔭 **TWO SMALLER ADOPTION MARKS, BOTH SOFT, BOTH DATED.** *"Muse… is garnering adoption (**9 straight days in the top 5 downloaded apps**)"* — ⚠️ downloads, not users, not engagement, not retention, and a launch spike; it neither confirms nor extends the Citi *"900K+ App Downloads in One Week"* exhibit already on this page (different metric, different window). And the agentic-commerce datapoint MS calls out on the startup side: ***"Instinct reportedly running $1,300/month in purchases per user"***, with MS's own immediate discount — *"admittedly biased given early tech adopters tend to be higher income consumers."* ➤ **$1,300/month of agent-executed purchases per user is the highest per-user commerce figure in this corpus by a wide margin, and it is reported, unsourced and self-selected. Logged as a directional sighting, NOT as a run-rate, and it must not be multiplied by any user base.** ⚠️ **"Instinct" remains, as this page already notes, a startup (Spear Street Technology) with no wiki page and no funding or product detail carried.**

## 🆕🔴 2026-09-17 (ingest 09-18 — user-pasted podcast transcript, FIRST-PARTY CEO OF A PRIVATE DATA-PLATFORM VENDOR, NOT A BROKER) — **THE LARGEST ENTERPRISE DATA-PLATFORM VENDOR PUTS A *DECADE* ON AI DIFFUSION, AND THE EVIDENCE IS A MEASURED CASE FROM HIS OWN ENGINEERING ORG: THE AI-WRITTEN CODE WAS TWO DAYS OF A NINE-MONTH CYCLE, THE TEAM'S FIRST OFFER WAS A 17% COMPRESSION, AND AFTER FORCED PROCESS RE-ENGINEERING PER-PERSON THROUGHPUT WENT ~3x — NOT 100x.**

**Source: Ali Ghodsi (Databricks CEO), *Long Strange Trip* podcast with Brian Halligan, 2026-09-17** — [archive](../../_equity_calls/Internet/2026-09-17_LongStrangeTrip_Databricks-Ghodsi-CEO-interview.md) · [raw](../../_equity_calls/Internet/2026-09-17_LongStrangeTrip_Databricks-Ghodsi-CEO-interview_raw.md). ⚠️ **Spoken, first-hand, unaudited; a vendor whose revenue depends on enterprise AI adoption; two-speaker YouTube auto-transcript. The arithmetic below is this page's, from his spoken numbers.** Second first-party Ghodsi source in nine days (the 09-08 GS Communacopia items sit on [ai-data-infra-observability](ai-data-infra-observability.md) §2.3).

- 🔴🔴 **THE CONNECTOR CASE, STEP BY STEP (53:18–56:48).** (a) November 2025: Ghodsi built a data connector himself with AI in **two days**; his teams were taking **"three quarters… for one person"** per connector. (b) The team: "your thing is not production ready. It's not secure… basically it doesn't really work" — then conceded AI was under-used and offered **"seven and a half months, one person"** (**−17%**). (c) One FTE inserted "not going to stand all this BS"; **three quarters later: "seven connectors in one quarter"** with seven people. (d) The "real inside scoop": **the code was never the bottleneck.** One quarter went to customer feedback and the PRD ("we write very good PRDs… that quarter, the whole pipeline is stalled") → now "one week… in an MD file, and if it's not great, it's okay"; **setting up the target systems (Salesforce, Workday, NetSuite)** — "we hate that part of the work… error-prone" → consultants; **one quarter of testing at the end** → moved up front, "end to end with AI." ➤ **Arithmetic: 1 connector / 3 person-quarters (0.33) → 1 connector / person-quarter (1.0) = ~3x per-person throughput; the AI-written core was ~2 days of a ~9-month cycle (~2%).** ⚠️ Team size before is not stated, so only per-person throughput is derivable. ✅ **Consistent with his 09-08 line ("I don't think that we're seeing any 100x velocity improvements… it is faster") — now with a number behind it.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE CONCLUSION, DATED TO END-2025: "it's going to take humanity a decade at least to absorb and diffuse AI, because of what I just experienced in a tech company with super smart people who want AI… you have to basically [do] the process re-engineering of all organizations on the planet. So that's going to take a long while."** ➤ **This is the demand-side counterweight to the two lab agent counts in the 09-17 block below (OpenAI's 10,000 burst; Anthropic's ~30,000 standing population): labs have agent populations; enterprises, per the largest data-platform vendor's own poll, have chatbots and coding agents.** It also dates the GS "70–90% experimenting, <25% scaling" survey line (05-05, below) from the supply side: the vendor selling the platform says the gap is process, not capability.
- 🔴 **THE ADOPTION-STAGE POLL, WITH THE VENUE NAMED — and one new question.** "How many of you think that the AI is smarter than most of the people around you most of the time? …I get like 90% of the hands up. Well, I define that as AGI… I asked that at **Moscone Center to 32,000 people**" (Data + AI Summit, June 2026 — REPEAT of 09-08). Inside enterprises: "**they're just using them as stupid chatbots**… And then they're using them for coding… this is what's happening inside the enterprise today." **New: "Do you have hundreds of agentic co-workers collaborating with each other, providing proposals to you…? Nobody raises their hand. I've never had a single person raise their hand on that."** What is missing is "the enterprise context… We call this ontology" — and the vendor concedes his product alone does not supply it: "**you have to also build this ontology**… our customers… use the same product but they haven't built up the ontology the same way we have."
- **THE ORG-DESIGN COROLLARY (a check on the "AI-native flat org" narrative this page has carried from Dorsey/Armstrong-style claims):** separate the human org chart from the information graph — AI on the company's own ontology now carries the latter ("Genie pretty much answers any question I have in any meeting"); but on 25-report fan-outs with player-coach managers who ship code: "**I think it's BS. I think things will break down.** You're going to get… unhappy employees… the manager is busy vibe coding most of the day." (12,000 employees; 3,500 engineers; staff meets three times a week.)
- **ONE MORE FIRST-PARTY LINE FOR THIS PAGE'S "WHO IS PAYING" LEDGER — not a token number, but a scale marker for the data layer:** "our… 1 million to **7 billion ARR** engine"; "**free cash flow break even**"; IPO "not too far into the future… a year or so." Spoken, basis undefined; detail and guardrails on [ai-data-infra-observability](ai-data-infra-observability.md) §2.3 and [SNOW](../SNOW.md).

## 🆕⚠️🗄️ 2026-08-11 (ingested 2026-09-17 /run-inbox — **5 WEEKS STALE, OPINION ESSAY, NO NUMBERS**) — **AN ECONOMIST PUTS A NAME AND A MECHANISM ON THIS PAGE'S OWN WORD: *"TOKENMAXXING"* IS WHAT CREATED THE ENTERPRISE DEMAND FOR OPEN WEIGHTS, AND HIS ARGUMENT IS THAT COST WAS ONLY THE FIRST REASON — THE DURABLE ONE IS CONTROL.**

**Christian Catalini (MIT; guest post on Alex Danco's Substack), *"Some Simple Economics of Open versus Closed AI — If the weights are free, who pays for the next run, and who will keep us safe?"*, 2026-08-11.** ⚠️⚠️ **THIS IS AN OPINION ESSAY BY AN ACADEMIC, NOT RESEARCH: no estimate, no rating, no price target, no TAM, no proprietary data, and nothing below is written to the assumptions ledger. It is carried for the FRAMEWORK and for one first-order claim about enterprise buying behaviour — not as evidence.** [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Some_Simple_Economics_of_Open_versus_Closed_AI.html)

- 🔴 **THE CLAIM THAT LANDS DIRECTLY ON THIS PAGE, AND IT USES THIS PAGE'S OWN TERM UNPROMPTED:** *"The initial enterprise interest in open-weights models was mostly cost-driven, and came as a natural correction to months of employees **'tokenmaxxing'** their way to greater productivity. By switching prompts to lower-cost, open-weights models, enterprises can shift a meaningful share of their token demand away from more expensive frontier models. AI labs have started fighting this by progressively slashing prices for entry-level models, and by training their models to perform best with their products."* ➤ **That is a two-sided description of the exact price war this page has been tracking as a series of unilateral cuts: the volume-tier discounts are read here as a DEFENCE against open-weight substitution, not as a land-grab. Same facts, opposite causal arrow — worth holding as an alternative reading of the 09-07 Astra barbell and the entry-tier cuts.**
- 🔴 **AND THE FORECAST THAT MATTERS MORE: cost is the wrong reason to care.** *"In the long term, cost is not the reason enterprises should care about openness: **control** is the critical dimension… For most market leaders, open-weights are therefore not just a cost escape hatch, but **existential**. Over the next few years, every firm will need a way to train, optimize, and own the models that support its most strategic operations."* Mechanism: interactions leak information even where labs promise not to train on them; customisation deepens lock-in; and *"many customers are increasingly feeling that they are one step away from having to compete with their AI provider"* — he names the **[[ANTHROPIC]]–Figma** relationship as the worked example, and **Thinking Machines / Bridgewater** (a custom model trained as an extension of the lab's base model) as the counter-model where *"it only wins if its customers do too."* ⚠️ **Assertion, not evidence — no survey, no spend data, no disclosed contract.**
- **THE CENTRAL ECONOMIC ARGUMENT, WHICH IS A LEVEL-VS-DIRECTION CLAIM:** from Petra Moser's dataset of ~15,000 inventions across the 1851 Great Exhibition and its 1876 American sequel, patent systems *"had no effect on the level of innovation, only on where inventors placed their attention."* Applied forward: **open weights are unlikely to change the LEVEL of AI investment, only its DIRECTION** — what gets built, by whom, and who captures the returns. ➤ **If right, the distillation-kills-the-next-training-run argument is about allocation, not about funding, and the *"appropriability regime"* the labs are asking Congress for *"never existed"* — outputs are not copyrightable, labs assign output ownership to customers, and distillation looks like legitimate customer traffic scattered across farmed accounts.**
- **THE MARKET-STRUCTURE PREDICTION, AND IT IS THIS PAGE'S UNIT ECONOMICS RESTATED AS A CURVE:** the relevant variable is *"the payoff to an additional unit of model capability."* Across most of the economy it **diminishes quickly** — *"once a model is good enough, further gains matter less than price, reliability, and ease of deployment"* — so intelligence gets **priced "at the meter," like electricity**, served by open weights or closed models run at cost. **Convex domains — cyber, finance, frontier R&D (including AI development itself) and patentable matter — sustain "temporary frontier rents" because buyers pay up for the best available model rather than the second-best.** ➤ **This is a cleaner statement of the value-added-token argument this page runs on, and it says where the mark-up survives: *"companies turn into token factories… they will only be able to charge a mark-up if they have access to better ground truth, data, and talent."***
- ⚠️ **THE TAIL CLAIM, FLAGGED AS THE AUTHOR'S OWN SPECULATION:** *"In the most convex domains, the marginal buyer is not a user or a company, it's a country… the steeper the curve, the higher the likelihood that if a lab were to achieve something truly unprecedented, it would be tightly managed or even nationalized. Ironically, for any AI lab to retain its freedom, it needs to be successful, but not too successful."* He cites *"the Fable and Mythos bans"* as the preview. **No source is given for that characterisation and this wiki has not verified it — logged as a thing the essay says, NOT as a fact.**
- **ON REGULATION AS A COMPLEMENTARY ASSET (relevant to every safety-and-capture debate on this page and on [ai-cybersecurity](ai-cybersecurity.md)):** *"if, in the name of safety, they can significantly raise the bar for model evaluation and approval, they can make it much harder for others that do not have… well-staffed, all-star policy, regulatory, compliance and safety teams to compete"* — with GDPR as the precedent that *"had a chilling effect on everyone else."* ✅ **His own honest hedge, which is why this is worth keeping rather than dismissing: *"The labs' claims could be self-serving and correct at the same time."***
- ➤ **CROSS-READ INTO [ai-cybersecurity](ai-cybersecurity.md), AND IT CUTS AGAINST THE 09-15 MOAT ARGUMENT:** he argues the openness-vs-safety balance is **domain-specific**, and that **cyber is the domain where diffusion favours DEFENDERS** — *"defense is distributed across a very large number of organizations and devices. Open weights lower the cost of auditing code, patching vulnerabilities, and upgrading legacy infrastructure, while sophisticated attackers may be capable of circumventing model-level controls"*, so restricting access *"would tax a wide population of defenders without effectively excluding the most capable adversaries."* **Biology is the opposite** — irreversible harm, and the chokepoint is physical (synthesisers, reagents): *"when the bottleneck is made of atoms, it offers a far more effective chokepoint against bad actors than software guardrails do."* 🔴 **Note what this does to the incumbent-cyber-moat case: if open weights strengthen distributed defence, the thing that erodes is the VENDOR's pricing power, not the defender's position. Nobody on the cyber page has argued that yet.**

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-17 (ingest — Anthropic document dated August 2026) — **BOTH FRONTIER LABS PUT AN AGENT COUNT ON THE RECORD WITHIN 24 HOURS, AND THE TWO NUMBERS ARE NOT THE SAME KIND OF THING: OPENAI'S 10,000 WAS A *BURST* ON ONE PROBLEM (HOST-ASSERTED); ANTHROPIC'S **~30,000 IS A STANDING POPULATION** (FIRST-PARTY, MEASURED, WITH **OVER A BILLION MONITORED DECISIONS IN ONE MONTH**). STACKING THEM IS THE TRAP — AND THE OBVIOUS CROSS-MULTIPLICATION IS PROVABLY CIRCULAR.**

(**Anthropic**, *"Measurements for understanding the pace of AI development inside frontier labs"*, anthropic.com/institute, snapshot **August 2026**, retrieved 2026-09-17. Favaro & Wright; research direction Jack Clark. Archived w/ full text + figure: [`20260800_Anthropic_Institute_…html`](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260800_Anthropic_Institute_Measuring_pace_of_AI_development_inside_frontier_labs.html). ⚠️ **COMPANY SELF-REPORT ABOUT ITSELF — first-party primary, NOT research; no rating, no PT, no model. Anthropic states it is *"using our own models to evaluate our systems."*** Full treatment on [[ANTHROPIC]].)

### 🔴🔴🔴 (1) THE TWO AGENT COUNTS, SIDE BY SIDE — AND WHY THEY MUST NOT BE ADDED, AVERAGED OR CHAINED

| | **OpenAI** (09-17 block above) | **Anthropic** (this entry) |
|---|---|---|
| Agents | **10,000** | **~30,000** |
| Nature | **Burst** — one 88-hour run on a single problem | **Standing population**, *"at any one time"*, ordinary R&D work |
| Metered unit | 130bn **tokens** | >1bn **decisions** (monitored agent actions) |
| Period | 88 hours | **August 2026** (a full month) |
| Provenance | **Host-asserted on a podcast**, researcher did not dispute | **First-party published**, company's own measurement |
| Status on this wiki | ⚠️ **NOT ADOPTED** | ✅ HARD as a disclosure — ⚠️ self-reported, unaudited |

➤ **The temptation is to read "10,000 → 30,000" as a lab-size ranking. It is not one.** A standing 30,000 is a capacity statement; a 10,000-agent burst is a workload statement. **Neither bounds the other, and the wiki should quote them only with their basis attached.**

### ⛔ (2) THE CROSS-MULTIPLICATION THAT LOOKS LIKE CORROBORATION AND IS ACTUALLY AN IDENTITY — DONE, CHECKED, AND REFUSED

This page derived **~41 tokens/sec per agent** from the OpenAI figures (130bn ÷ 88h ÷ 10,000). Applying it to Anthropic's 30,000 gives **1.23M tok/s ≈ 3.3 trillion tokens in August**, implying **~3,294 tokens per decision** — a number that looks entirely plausible, which is exactly the danger.

**The check that kills it:** run it the other way. Take Anthropic's own decision rate (1bn ÷ 30,000 agents ÷ 31 days) and apply it to the OpenAI run → **3,297 tokens/decision**. ➤ **The same number, to 0.1%, because it is one equation rearranged rather than two independent measurements meeting.** There is **one equation and two unknowns** (tokens-per-decision, and the relative duty cycle of a burst run vs a standing population); **neither is identified.** Add the basis mismatch — burst-at-full-tilt vs standing-with-unknown-idle — and the chain fails twice.

⚠️ **NO INTERNAL TOKEN-BURN FIGURE IS ENTERED FOR EITHER LAB. Neither document discloses enough to bound one.** Logged here because the arithmetic is seductive and someone will re-derive it; this is the pre-emptive record of why it does not hold. _(Cf. [[anchor-estimates-to-reality]] discipline: a check that reuses the calibrated output proves nothing.)_

### 🔴🔴🔴 (3) WHAT *IS* DERIVABLE — AND IT INTRODUCES A NEW UNIT TO THIS PAGE

From two disclosed integers alone (1bn decisions, 30,000 agents, 31 days), no external chaining:

| ⚠️ **ALL FLOORS AT 1sf** (*"over a billion"*, *"approximately 30,000"*) | |
|---|--:|
| Monitored decisions per **concurrent agent-slot**, August | **~33,000** (≥) |
| Per agent-slot per day | **~1,000** (≥) |
| Aggregate | **~370 decisions/sec** (≥) |
| One decision per agent-slot every… | **~80s** or faster |
| Blocked actions, August (0.002% at 1sf ⇒ 0.0015–0.0025%) | **~15,000–25,000** |

⚠️⚠️ **"~30,000 at any one time" is a CONCURRENCY STOCK, not a headcount — so this is per agent-SLOT, not per agent; scope is one platform (*"These measurements cover this platform only"*); and the source says *"decisions"* while defining coverage over *"actions"*, never equating them. ⛔ An earlier draft of this block cited *"0.002%"* and *"1 in 47,000"* as two statements agreeing within 6% — WITHDRAWN: 1/47,000 = 0.0021277%, which rounds to 0.002%. One measurement at two precisions, zero corroborative content. It was the same circularity §(2) rejects.**

🔴🔴 **AND HERE IS THE STRUCTURAL POINT FOR THIS PAGE, WHOSE UNIT HAS ALWAYS BEEN THE TOKEN: ANTHROPIC DOES NOT METER ITS INTERNAL AGENT ESTATE IN TOKENS. IT METERS IT IN *DECISIONS* — discrete agent actions passing an oversight monitor.** ➤ **That is the second independent signal in ten days that the unit of account is migrating off the token.** This page already carries **MiniMax's CEO saying billing is moving *"from tokens to tasks"*** (09-08..10 Communacopia-week Notion notes) and the 09-15 buyer-side hierarchy of **cost per token → per workflow → per *completed* workflow → per decision supported**. **Anthropic's internal telemetry is built on the fourth rung of that ladder.** ⚠️ **A vendor's internal oversight unit is not a billing unit and this is not evidence of a pricing change — but three independent parties now describe the same drift, and the pricing question is downstream of it.**

### 🔴🔴 (4) THE RSI NUMBER ITSELF — MEASURED, MONTHLY, AND THE SLOPE IS A STEP FUNCTION

Anthropic's **R&D Automation Index v2026.07** (Epoch AI's AL0–AL5 scale), share of its own model-R&D tasks at **AL4 "AI leads"** — ⚠️ **this monthly series exists only inside the figure image, not in the body prose; transcribed from the chart:**

| 2026 | Feb | Mar | Apr | May | Jun | Jul | **Aug** |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| AL4 "AI leads" | ~0% | 1% | 3% | **12%** | 14% | **22%** | **26%** |

**Two step-changes, not a smooth curve: Apr→May +9pp (4x in a month) and Jun→Jul +8pp, with +2pp plateaus between.** ⚠️ **The release-timing explanation is MY inference — Anthropic names no model version against any month.** Alongside: *"the share of work at or above 'AI collaborates' is above 90%"*, and **AL5 is zero throughout** — *"Claude is not operating fully autonomously for any measured subset of AI R&D work."*

⚠️⚠️ **THE GOVERNING CAVEAT, FROM ANTHROPIC'S OWN METHODOLOGY: *"model-versus-human exact agreement was 59%, human-versus-human was 35%"*, within one level **97%** of the time. Humans agree with each other on the exact level only ~a third of the time — and the AL3/AL4 boundary is exactly where "26% leads" is cut. Treat the LEVEL as soft and the SLOPE as the signal.** Full methodology, the frozen-basket question (which Anthropic tested and largely answered) and the person-time weighting artefact are on [[ANTHROPIC]].

PLUS THE FINDING THAT THE PAGE’S OWN 09-09 COST BRACKET WAS NEVER A THROUGHPUT DISPUTE — IT WAS A **$/GPU-HR** DISPUTE THIS WIKI COULD ALREADY HAVE SETTLED FROM ITS OWN MARKS.**

### 🔴🔴🔴 (1) THE DENOMINATOR — **130bn TOKENS FOR ONE MILLENNIUM-PRIZE RESULT**, AND WHAT IT DOES TO THIS PAGE’S COST-PER-DISCOVERY ITEM

- 🔴🔴 **[PATEL→BROWN uncontested]** *“10,000 different AI agents that spent **130 billion tokens over 88 hours**”*. Brown engages with the figure throughout and never disputes it. ⚠ **NOT ADOPTED** — host-asserted, researcher-uncontested; the wiki already holds this story as *“proof unverified”*. It is the best available figure, not a fact.
- ➤ **This page has carried, since 09-09, WSJ’s *“millions of dollars”* against @zephyr_z9’s *“$500k-$1M in inference compute (1,500-3,000 GB300s, $4/hr)”* as *“an order apart”*, *“neither adopted”*, bracketing one discovery at *“high six figures to low millions.”* @zephyr_z9 named his own gap: *“I don’t know what the effective throughput per GPU was.”* **The token count is exactly that missing variable. Worked through, it says three things — and the third is the one that matters.**
- 🟢 **(a) THE DISCLOSED FIGURES ARE MUTUALLY CONSISTENT.** 130bn ÷ 88h ÷ 3,600 = **410,354 tokens/sec aggregate**; ÷ 10,000 agents = **41.0 tok/s per agent** — **1.2x off this page’s own nearest serving mark** (SemiAnalysis: DeepSeek V4 at **50 tok/s/user**, 8k in / 1k out). Had it implied 2 or 500 tok/s per agent, one of the three numbers would be wrong. ⚠ *Assumes a 100% duty cycle; concurrency is sourced, full utilisation is not.*
- 🔴 **(b) THE TWO PUBLISHED ESTIMATES NEVER TOUCHED — IT IS A DEAD BAND, NOT A BRACKET.** At $4/GPU-hr, @zephyr_z9’s range implies **137-274 tok/s/GPU**; WSJ’s *“millions”* (read as ≥$2M) implies **≤72 tok/s/GPU**. **Between them — a run costing $1.06M to $2.00M — neither published estimate is correct.** This page has been quoting them as a range; they are separated by a ~1.9x gap that neither claims.
- 🔴🔴 **(c) THE CONTESTED INPUT WAS NEVER THE THROUGHPUT — IT WAS THE RENTAL RATE, AND THIS PAGE ALREADY HELD THE MARK.** @zephyr_z9 assumed **$4/GPU-hr**. This page carries **GB300 spot ~$7-8/GPU-hr** (CoreWeave/Cantor expert call, 2026-06-18) and **$7.00-$10.00/hr** (MS · Nowak Ex.3, 2026-08-12) — roughly **double**. Re-price **his own GPU counts** at those rates:

| $/GPU-hr | 1,500 GPUs | 3,000 GPUs |
|---|---|---|
| **$4** (@zephyr_z9’s assumption) | $0.53M | $1.06M |
| **$7** (CoreWeave spot) | $0.92M | $1.85M |
| **$8** (CoreWeave spot) | $1.06M | $2.11M |

  ➤ **At the rental rates this wiki already carries, the sceptic’s own assumptions produce WSJ’s answer. The *“order apart”* disagreement may never have been about throughput at all — and it was resolvable from data already on disk, without the podcast contributing anything.** ⚠ **Cuts the other way and must travel with it: $7-8/hr is a *merchant rental* rate carrying neocloud margin (CoreWeave targets ~25% EBITDA); OpenAI on owned or contracted capacity sits below it. The honest statement is that the true cost basis lies between $4 and $8 and nobody outside OpenAI knows where.**
- 🟡 **A ROUTE THAT NEEDS NO THROUGHPUT ASSUMPTION AT ALL, AND IT LANDS IN THE DEAD BAND.** 130bn tokens = 130,000M. At **GPT-6 Astra’s published card ($10/M in, $50/M out)**: all-output → **$6.5M retail-equivalent**, all-input → **$1.3M** (the card’s 5:1 ratio means those endpoints already span the input/output-mix question). Back out this page’s **75-85% inference GM** → **~$0.98M-$1.63M of cost**. ⚠ Directional only: that GM mark is not a frontier-OpenAI number, and the model actually used is *“significantly more capable than GPT-6 Astra”* and **unpriced**, so $50/M is a **floor** on retail-equivalent value.
- 🔴 **AND THE METHODOLOGICAL CORRECTION THIS PAGE SHOULD CARRY FORWARD: there is no such thing as *the* tokens/sec/GPU constant.** It is a chosen operating point on a latency/throughput frontier, and **this page carries a ~50x spread in it** — MS · Nowak anchors **2,000-3,500 tok/s/GPU** for DeepSeek-V4-Pro 1.6T on GB300, while SemiAnalysis’s premium-tier $/Mtok figures imply **~32-82 tok/s/GPU** at $4/hr. Both sit in this file, two sections apart. **Any $/token or $/GPU-hr conversion on this page must name batch size, interactivity target and model size, or it is not comparable to the next one.**

### 🔴🔴🔴 (2) THE MULTIPLIER — **A SHIPPED PRODUCT WHOSE DEFAULT IS FOUR AGENTS**, WITH THE EXCHANGE RATE STATED BY THE VENDOR

- 🔴🔴 **[BROWN]: 5.6’s *Ultra Mode* — *“The default is four agents, but you can set that higher”*, with published scaling plots for 1, 4 and 16 agents. On some benchmarks four agents finish *“twice as fast… you’re paying 2x more to get an answer twice as quickly”*; at 16 agents *“a little less efficient”*; overall *“slightly sublinear.”*** ➤ **4x the agents, 2x the tokens, 2x the speed.**
- 🔴🔴 **[BROWN] names the trade explicitly: *“multi-agent is a way of scaling test-time compute IN PARALLEL instead of purely serially. It is LESS EFFICIENT, because it’s not like a single agent has all the context to itself. But it is a very effective way of scaling test-time compute if it’s done well.”*** ➤ **This is the cleanest first-party statement in the corpus that the token bill is a DELIBERATE PURCHASE OF WALL-CLOCK, not a by-product of inefficiency — and that the exchange rate is knowingly unfavourable in compute.**
- ➤ **Workload-shaped, per Brown:** math *“quite parallelizable”*; Deep-Research-style web search *“extremely parallelizable”*; writing a novel *“very unparallelizable”*. **Token-demand upside concentrates in research / search / math / code — exactly the enterprise workloads this page tracks — and is absent in generative long-form.**
- ⚠ **THE BUILDER DEFLATES HIS OWN PRODUCT, AND IT BELONGS NEXT TO EVERY AGENT-SWARM READ-THROUGH: *“I wouldn’t even attribute 10% of the credit to multi-agent… Things like multi-agent are flashy and new, and that probably gets DISPROPORTIONATE CREDIT for that reason. But the core reason is this is just a very powerful model.”*** Also: *“we don’t have very good science on multi-agent scaling up to this kind of scale… We don’t actually have good measurements saying ‘this 10,000 agents led to a 2x speedup over 2,000 agents.’”* **The published science stops at 16 agents.**

**Bull:** a shipped frontier product now consumes ≥2x the tokens of its single-agent equivalent **by default**, user-dialable higher, aimed at the highest-parallelism workloads. Parallel test-time compute is a product feature, not a research artefact.

**Bear:** the vendor concedes the parallel path is *“less efficient”* per unit of result and sublinear beyond 4; the multiplier is **discretionary spend that can be dialled back to one** if latency stops binding or serial reasoning gets cheaper faster.

### ✅ (3) TWO CORROBORATIONS OF THINGS THIS PAGE ALREADY HELD

- ✅ **SAFETY IS COMPUTE-ADDITIVE — NOW WITH THE MECHANISM NAMED.** The 09-16 block on this page argued that making models *safe* is itself compute-additive. Brown supplies the mechanism: after the Hugging Face incident, chain-of-thought monitoring now runs *“during evaluations, during DEPLOYMENT, during training for any frontier model”* — **a standing monitoring overhead on every served token**, which is the concrete version of the Morgan Stanley desk’s *“AI Safety is another Compute workload… perhaps taking some Compute away from more profitable Inference workloads.”*
- ✅ **PUBLIC FRONTIER ≠ INTERNAL FRONTIER, NOW STATED AS FACT FROM INSIDE.** Held since 08-17 as a SemiAnalysis inference from hearsay about unreleased checkpoints. Brown: *“we have a very powerful model internally that is currently NOT AVAILABLE TO THE OUTSIDE WORLD… It’s not just Millennium Prize Problems. There are MANY SOLUTIONS TO UNSOLVED PROBLEMS that people have been able to get out of this model… that is an UNFAIR ADVANTAGE.”* ➤ **BASIS WARNING FOR EVERY TOKEN-SHARE AND CAPABILITY SERIES ON THIS PAGE — OpenRouter volumes, Artificial Analysis rankings, benchmark tables, the price card: all of them measure the DEPLOYED frontier, which the lab says is a lagging and possibly widening-lag indicator of the TRAINED one.** ⚠ Symmetric and equally important: **an undeployed capability generates no revenue.** The gap cuts against near-term monetisation exactly as much as it flatters long-term capability.

### 🔭 (4) VARIANT PERCEPTION — **HOUSE INFERENCE, NOT IN THE SOURCE: THE CONCURRENCY NUMBER IS A MEMORY STORY, NOT ONLY A COMPUTE STORY**

- 🔭 **Brown describes the architecture as minimally scaffolded — agents get a primitive tool to *“message another agent, and when it messages another agent, IT IS INSERTED INTO THE CONTEXT”*, plus context **forking** for sub-agents in Astra and 5.6 Sol. ➤ **10,000 concurrent agents, each carrying a growing context that is continuously appended to by peer messages, is a KV-cache footprint that scales with concurrency × context length — i.e. an HBM-capacity and memory-bandwidth problem before it is a FLOPs problem.** This is **Capstone inference and is NOT stated anywhere in the transcript** — Brown never mentions memory. But it is the direct counterpart to the 09-16 item on this page in which **DeepSeek cut HBM-resident KV cache per token to ~¼ and SSD-resident to ~⅛ at better performance**: agent-swarm concurrency pushes the same variable the other way. ➤ **If the swarm pattern generalises from one showcase run to a shipped default — and Ultra Mode says it already has — then per-query HBM demand scales with the agent count, and the KV-cache efficiency race becomes the binding constraint on how wide a swarm can economically go. Flagged for the [[MU]] / HBM debate; NOT adopted, and it needs a real sizing before it is quoted.**

THAT IS THE CLEANEST PUBLISHED INSTANCE ON DISK OF AN AGENTIC THESIS WHOSE OWN NUMBERS SHOW THE OPERATING LINE ABSORBING THE ENTIRE INCREMENTAL REVENUE DOLLAR FOR A YEAR.**

The MS · Nowak "Muse Has Arrived" note of 2026-09-09 already on this page (META OW, PT $775, $30tn consumer agentic TAM) STANDS; the two notes below are a second and third house on the same event, and the third is a direct extension of the 09-14 [[BKNG]]/Airbnb counterweight further down.**_

### 🔴🔴🔴 (1) THE ONE THAT MATTERS FOR *THIS* PAGE — GOLDMAN'S PUBLISHED META MODEL, PRINTED IN THE SAME DOCUMENT AS THE BUY

- **The numbers, all from the GS forecast table in the note (Goldman Sachs · Eric Sheridan, 2026-09-13), 12/26E → 12/27E:** **Revenue $255,408.6mn → $310,507.3mn (+21.6%)**; **EBIT $86,938.3mn → $89,950.2mn (+3.5%)**; **EBIT margin 34.0% → 29.0%**; **EPS (diluted) $31.65 → $31.39 (−0.8%)**; **FCF yield 2.7% (12/25) → 0.8% (12/26E) → (1.6)% (12/27E)**; **free cash flow $46,109mn → $13,905mn → *minus* $26,071mn**. The mechanism is in the same table and is not hidden: **capex $69,691mn (12/25) → $138,369mn (12/26E) → $213,608mn (12/27E)**, and **D&A $18,616mn → $36,815mn → $66,741mn** — i.e. **D&A alone grows ~$29.9bn in 2027E against ~$55.1bn of incremental revenue**, and EBITDA still grows **+26.6%** while EBIT grows **+3.5%**.
- 🔴🔴 **WHY THIS IS A TOKENMAXXING DATAPOINT AND NOT A META DATAPOINT.** This page has spent months measuring the compute bill of the agent era in prices, indices, $/MW ladders, lab P&Ls and vendor anecdotes. **Here it is in a covering analyst's own published GAAP forecast for the largest consumer distribution platform on earth: one full year in which +21.6% revenue growth converts to +3.5% operating-profit growth, EPS goes backwards, and free cash flow goes negative — while the same document rates the equity Buy at $725 explicitly on the agentic opportunity.** ➤➤ **That is the "operating line absorbs the entire incremental revenue dollar" case, published, dated and attributable, rather than inferred. It is the demand-side twin of every supply-side $/MW and cost-per-token series on this page: the tokens get paid for out of the 2027 P&L.**
- ⚠️ **HANDLE WITH THE CAVEATS, ALL OF WHICH ARE ALSO IN THE NOTE.** (i) **This is a GS forecast, not a company guide** — Meta has not guided 2027 EBIT and nothing here is a Meta disclosure. (ii) **The compression is DEPRECIATION-LED, not token-cost-led** — GS does not attribute it to inference cost, and the D&A step is the arithmetic driver; it is the *capitalised* compute bill hitting the P&L, which is the same dollar arriving one accounting layer later. (iii) **GS's valuation does not run through 2027 EBIT** — the $725 PT is *"an equal blend of (1) 26.0x EV/GAAP EBIT applied to our NTM + 1 year estimates and (2) a modified DCF using 45.0x EV/FCF-SBC multiple applied to our NTM + 4 years estimates discounted back 3 years"*, so the house is explicitly looking past the trough year. **That is the debate, stated fairly: the bull case is not that 2027 looks good, it is that 2027 does not matter.** (iv) GS's own listed risk (b) is *"large investments in long-tailed initiatives depress operating margins for longer than our forecasts"* — the house names the failure mode itself. (v) ⚠️ **Note the Citi note three days later carries a DIFFERENT 2026E EPS ($39.45E vs GS $31.65) — the two houses are not on the same EPS definition; do not compare the two EPS lines, and do not mix either with the VA consensus $34.28 Citi prints alongside its own.**

### 🔴🔴 (2) THE THEME-LEVEL ARGUMENT BOTH HOUSES ARE MAKING — **PASSIVE CHATBOT → PROACTIVE AGENT THAT TRANSACTS**, AND THE CLAIM THAT DISTRIBUTION, NOT MODEL QUALITY, DECIDES IT

- **Goldman's framing of the shift, verbatim: *"With the introduction of several action-based consumer agents in the last few weeks (Spear Street Technology's Instinct; Meta's Muse AI; etc.), the consumer AI landscape has begun the process of undergoing a fundamental paradigm shift, transitioning rapidly from passive, conversational chatbots to proactive, autonomous agents capable of executing real-world tasks"* — moving *"from simply answering questions to completing complex, high-value personal workflows such as travel booking, bill negotiation, calendar management, shopping, and administrative tasks"*** (GS · Sheridan, 2026-09-13). ✅ **SMALL RESOLUTION FOR THIS PAGE: the 09-09 block further down names "Instinct" as a startup with no detail and no parent. GS supplies the owner — Spear Street Technology. Still plain text: no wiki page, no funding, no product detail, and none is being created.**
- 🔴 **THE CONSOLIDATION CALL, WHICH IS THE PART TO ARGUE WITH: *"competition within consumer AI is increasingly becoming a matter of platform/ecosystem integrations, distribution and capabilities rather than purely on model quality alone. Much like the consolidation of mobile operating systems around Apple (iOS) and Google (Android), we expect consumer agents will consolidate around platforms with the strongest distribution, trust, and commerce integrations. Within our coverage, we'd highlight META & GOOGL as having the existing distribution, intelligence, compute capacity and user base to build & scale such consumer agentic AI efforts"*** (GS · Sheridan, 2026-09-13). ⚠️ **GOOGL is the share class, not a separate wiki page — read as [[GOOG]]; note the standing wiki rule that BBG dispersion and share-count fields describe a LISTING, not a company.** ➤ **If GS is right, the marginal agentic token is bought by whoever already owns the distribution, which means the token bill lands on the four or five balance sheets this wiki already tracks rather than being spread across a long tail of startups — and that is a bullish read for the compute layer and a margin-compression read for the platforms. Item (1) above is the first published quantification of the second half.**
- **GS's own list of what is still unresolved, worth keeping as the debate's agenda:** *"Access to compute is likely to be a critical variable in the ability to launch and scale such broad-based initiatives"*; *"a friction point remains how 'agent ready' the broader web/app ecosystem sits in its current form"*; and **the architecture question — *"whether browser-mimicking agents represent the long-term architecture, or if API-driven data access will ultimately prove faster, more reliable, and more scalable."*** ➤ **That last one is a direct token-volume fork: a browser-mimicking agent burns far more tokens per completed task than an API call. This page has no measurement of the ratio. Open item.**

### 🔴🔴 (3) CITI, THREE DAYS LATER — **"THE FRONT DOORS OF THE INTERNET"**, A MODEL CADENCE THAT COMPRESSED ~13 WEEKS → ~4 WEEKS, AND A DOWNLOAD DATAPOINT

The figure TITLES are text and are what is cited. Also note the scope: US, first week, downloads — not users, not engagement, not retention, and a launch spike is not a run-rate.** ⚠️ **Monetisation remains UNKNOWN on this page: GS reports launch tiers of Free (100mn weekly tokens) / Power ($20/mo, 500mn weekly tokens) / Maximum ($100/mo, 3bn weekly tokens) plus a Stripe/Link integration for agentic commerce, and Citi adds Meta One Core ($7.99/mo) and Premium ($19.99/mo); the page's standing 2026-08-25 "$199.99/month" tier is still NEITHER confirmed NOR withdrawn. 🔬 **Worth flagging for this page specifically: the GS-reported tiers are denominated in WEEKLY TOKENS, not in messages or seats — a consumer subscription priced explicitly in tokens is the retail end of the unit this whole dossier is built on.** Do not model a blended price.**

### ⚠️🔴 (4) THE NAMED COUNTERPARTY TO THE DISINTERMEDIATION CASE — A COVERING HOUSE SAYS THE AGENT IS A **CHANNEL**, NOT A SUBSTITUTE

- **Morgan Stanley, assuming coverage of online travel the same day as the Citi note: *"Horizontal AI Looks More Like a New Acquisition Channel Than a Disintermediation Threat, While Travel-Specific AI Creates a New Opportunity for the OTAs"* — *"We increasingly expect horizontal AI to resemble a **new search layer**, creating richer intent and high value traffic while **preserving the OTAs' role in inventory, checkout, service, and transaction data**"*** (MS · Matthew Cost / Brian Nowak, 2026-09-16). **The mechanism, stated plainly: *"Early agentic products are increasingly routing users to OTA apps and websites for purchase, while sharing data in exchange for access to critical inventory. We therefore see the industry structure staying closer to paid search than initially feared"* — with OTAs expected to *"integrate supply into agents, compete for high intent traffic, **remain the merchants of record** and then work to convert users into direct/repeat customers."***
- 🔴 **THE DETAIL THAT MAKES IT CONCRETE: MS's own AI-strategy exhibit lists the top-of-funnel/discovery layer for [[BKNG]] and for Expedia as *"Paid search, ChatGPT app, **Google agentic** hotel booking, brand advertising"* — the agent sits in the SAME ROW as paid search, as one acquisition channel among several, not as a replacement for the funnel.** ⚠️ Expedia and Airbnb have no wiki pages and none is being created; both are referenced in plain text only. MS names *"direct mix"* as the metric to watch and flags *"unpriced risk to ABNB's 90% direct mix, if horizontal AI"* scales — i.e. the house is not claiming immunity, it is claiming a different failure mode.
- ➤➤ **WHY IT BELONGS ON THIS PAGE AS A NAMED COUNTERPARTY AND NOT AS A CONCLUSION.** Items (2) and (3) above are two houses arguing that agents become *"the Front Doors of the Internet"* and consolidate around whoever owns distribution. Item (4) is a third house, covering the companies that would be disintermediated, arguing the opposite structural outcome from the same observed behaviour. **Both readings are consistent with the evidence already on this page — the 09-14 [[BKNG]]/Airbnb block records LLM traffic *"significantly below 1%"* of Booking's traffic and AI-agent bookings *"under 1%"* of bookings, with Airbnb management putting the interface shift 18-24 months out. MS is the first covering house to turn that observation into a published structural call.** ✅ **The debate is therefore now DATED AND TWO-SIDED on this page: GS/Citi (platform agents capture the front door) vs MS (the agent is an acquisition channel and the incumbent stays merchant of record). NOT RESOLVED, and nothing here is adopted.** ⚠️ **Basis caution before anyone pairs the two: GS/Citi are arguing about the CONSUMER-PLATFORM layer and MS about the TRANSACTION layer in one vertical (travel) — they can both be right, and the falsifiable variable that separates them is the one MS names: direct mix.**

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-16 (/wiki-ingest 21h) — **BOTH SIDES OF THIS PAGE'S CENTRAL EQUATION MOVED IN ONE DAY: DEEPSEEK PUBLISHED AN ARCHITECTURE THAT CUTS HBM-RESIDENT KV CACHE PER TOKEN TO ~¼ AND SSD-RESIDENT TO ~⅛ AT BETTER PERFORMANCE — WHILE OPENAI ARGUED THAT MAKING MODELS *SAFE* IS ITSELF COMPUTE-ADDITIVE**

_(**Jefferies Asia TMT sales · Conor O'Mara** carrying analyst "Edison" + the DeepSeek V4.1-Flash paper, 2026-09-16 · **Sachin Katti (OpenAI VP Compute Strategy)** and **Sarah Friar (CFO)**, public remarks 09-15 via @GavinSBaker / @MF_Camillus / @firstadopter · **BofA · Vivek Arya**, AI Infra Summit Day 1 · **[[CRM]] Dreamforce keynote** via JPM · Samik Chatterjee)_

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE COST-PER-TOKEN SIDE. DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash (2026-09-10): **552B-parameter MoE backbone, 1m-token context, 16B params active per token on DECODE but only 8B on PREFILL** via a Causal Encoder-Decoder design. **Compressed Sparse Attention 2 + FP4 KV caching cut the global (HBM-resident) KV cache to 890 bytes per token ≈ ¼ of V4-Flash; "SWA Bounded Replay" cuts the persistent (SSD/host-memory) cache to ≈ ⅛**, trading a little prefill recomputation for storage. **Blended API price US$0.2/token as the note writes it (⚠️ read as per-MILLION tokens — the note's own unit), 74% below the prior version.** *"Despite its much smaller KV cache footprint, the model delivers substantially better performance than the baseline."***
- 🔴🔴 **WHY IT BELONGS HERE RATHER THAN ONLY IN [themes/hbm-memory](hbm-memory.md): this page's whole thesis is that **token volume × compute-per-token** is the demand function, and that falling cost-per-token has so far expanded volume faster than it has cut consumption. **DeepSeek just moved the second term by ~75% on the memory dimension in a single release, published the method, and priced the result 74% lower.** The Jevons question is no longer rhetorical — it is now measurable on a dated release. **THE TEST, WRITTEN DOWN: does Chinese inference bit-demand decelerate in 1H27 despite rising token volume?
- 🔴🔴 **THE COUNTERVAILING FORCE, AND IT IS NEW IN KIND: the safety debate is being answered with COMPUTE. OpenAI's **Sachin Katti: *"The way we will make sure frontier models are safe and aligned is by spending compute. So the counterintuitive point is that we'll need even MORE compute to make sure future models are more safe and aligned."*** **CFO Sarah Friar, same cycle: *"there is so much opportunity to drive growth that I am still highly focused on getting more compute to keep that flywheel going."*** **@firstadopter sizes the overhead at *"roughly 20% of the inference compute being monitored, though the cost varies substantially across training and evaluation workloads"* ⚠️ independent estimate, not a disclosure.** **BofA's European semicap team reaches the same conclusion from the equipment side — AI governance is *"neutral to potentially positive"* because monitoring adds compute demand. If safety is implemented as evaluation-and-monitoring rather than as a pause, the regulatory push is a DEMAND ITEM on this page, not a risk item.
- 🔴 **AND A MIX DATAPOINT THAT MATTERS FOR WHO CAPTURES THE TOKENS: at [[CRM]]'s Dreamforce keynote, **NVIDIA stated that open-model share of tokens rose from ~30% to ~70% over the past year.** ⚠️ **Source is a vendor keynote relayed by JPM, and "share of tokens" is undefined (which population? which workloads?). Logged as a directional claim with a caveat, NOT adopted — but if it is even approximately right, the value migrates from model licences to inference infrastructure and orchestration, which is the argument [themes/ai-data-infra-observability](ai-data-infra-observability.md) is built on.
- 🔭 **Also from the infra summit, on the shape of inference demand: it is **disaggregating into prefill-optimised and decode-optimised silicon** while buyers insist on **fungibility** because *"agentic workloads are not only highly diverse (with the prefill/decode mix varying widely) but also evolving very quickly"* — BofA's resulting market split is **~70-75% general-purpose GPU / the rest specialised ASIC-XPU**. **DeepSeek's 16B-decode / 8B-prefill asymmetry is the same phenomenon expressed in a model rather than in silicon.

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-15 (/run-inbox) — **THE DEMAND SIDE SPEAKS, AND IT SPEAKS IN THIS PAGE'S OWN UNIT: A BANK'S BUYER-SIDE FRAMEWORK PUTS THE HIERARCHY AT COST PER TOKEN → PER WORKFLOW → PER *COMPLETED* WORKFLOW → PER DECISION SUPPORTED, PRICES ONE ANALYST REQUEST AT "10-20+ CALLS", AND SAYS A NAIVE "BEST MODEL FOR EVERYTHING" DEFAULT CAN BE AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE DEARER AT THE DECISION LEVEL THAN A ROUTED DESIGN.**

_⚠️⚠️ **READ THE PROVENANCE BEFORE THE CONTENT. THIS IS NOT A BARCLAYS EQUITY VIEW AND MUST NEVER BE QUOTED AS ONE.** The note states on its own cover: *"This is a Special Report that is not an equity or a debt research report under U.S. FINRA Rules 2241-2242,"* and the author *"is a member of the Fixed Income, Currencies and Commodities Research department and is not an equity or debt research analyst."* **There are NO ratings, NO price targets, NO estimates and NO proprietary company forecasts anywhere in it** — every company fact it contains is a recitation of already-public information. It is a **framework / thought-leadership piece written for financial-institution BUYERS of AI compute**. ✅ **That is precisely why it earns a place here: this page is thick with vendor and sell-side SUPPLY-side views, and a structured BUYER'S decision framework — a large financial institution describing how it actually decides where workloads run — is the scarce input. Weight it as demand-side evidence, not as a call.** (Barclays · Sahana Athreya, CFA — "AI Frontiers: The AI Compute Portfolio", Special Report, 2026-09-15)_

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE CENTRAL CLAIM, AND IT IS THIS PAGE'S OWN ARGUMENT PUSHED ONE RUNG FURTHER THAN THE WIKI HAS TAKEN IT:** *"The key metric is cost per decision, not cost per token."* The full hierarchy, verbatim: *"Cost per token → cost per workflow → cost per completed workflow → cost per decision supported"* — with each rung defined: *"Cost per token is an infrastructure measure. Cost per workflow is an operating measure. Cost per completed workflow adds the retries and failures that token prices hide. Cost per decision supported is the only business measure."* ➤ **The wiki already stands at rung three (cost per TASK). The net-new rung is the fourth — *decision supported* prices in the HUMAN REVIEW leg: Barclays' own worked example lists "Analyst minutes / Fully-loaded labor" as a line item inside the compute-cost stack. No seller on this page has ever put the buyer's own labour inside the unit; the buyer does.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE WORKED EXAMPLE, WHICH IS THE MOST USEFUL SINGLE EXHIBIT IN THE NOTE (their Figure 4).** One analyst request — *"Summarize today's earnings call and identify the three biggest risks"* — decomposes into: **embedding + retrieval calls** (cost driver: volume of context retrieved) · **1 reasoning-model call to plan** (premium model tokens) · **2-4 model calls to summarise and extract risks** (input + output tokens) · **several tool invocations** (per-call + data egress) · **1-3 eval + retry calls** (*"retries multiply cost"*) · **human review** (fully-loaded labour). Barclays' own summary row: *"What looked like one answer is a dozen-plus compute events"* — **10-20+ calls**, with *"Retries + agent loops dominate"* named as THE cost driver. ➤ **This is the first explicit call-count decomposition on this page, and it is the mechanism behind every "cheap tokens, expensive task" result the page already carries: if one request is 10-20+ calls and retries dominate, the per-token price is a minority of the bill — and a model that fails less can be cheaper at a higher list price.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE ROUTING PRINCIPLE, VERBATIM, AND IT IS THE CLEANEST STATEMENT OF THE POLICY THIS PAGE HAS BEEN ARGUING ABOUT SINCE AUGUST:** *"Use the lowest-cost combination of model and compute that satisfies the workflow's measured quality, latency and risk requirements—then escalate only when evaluation thresholds are not met."* And the cost of NOT doing it — the sharpest claim in the note, though an order-of-magnitude assertion rather than a measurement: a naive *"best model for everything"* default *"can be an order of magnitude more expensive at the decision level than a routed design"*, because *"the premium model is billed on every retrieval, plan, retry and low-value classification, not just the reasoning step that actually needs it."* ➤ **Note what this does to the page's open downtiering dispute: the Aug-31 CFO who downtiered, hit a quality wall and went back to the expensive model, and the [[ANTHROPIC]] customer who UPGRADED to Opus 5 and cut cost per task 66%, are not counter-examples to one another — they are both the ESCALATION half of a single routing rule. On Barclays' framing the policy is "route low, escalate on measured eval gates" and the error is "pick one model", in EITHER direction.**
- ✅🔴🔴 **CROSS-CHECK AGAINST WHAT THIS PAGE ALREADY CARRIES — THIS IS THE BUYER-SIDE FORMALISATION OF TWO SELLER-SIDE MARKS ALREADY HERE, AND IT BELONGS WITH THEM RATHER THAN FILED APART.** ① [[ANTHROPIC]]'s own GTM (BTG session, 2026-09-10, block above): a customer *"spending like $1.6 per completed task"* on Sonnet went to *"0.65"* on Opus 5, *"reducing the cost in 66%"*, with the instruction *"start measuring cost per task, not cost per token."* **Barclays' hierarchy is that same instruction with the mechanism attached: if one request is 10-20+ calls and retries dominate, a more capable model that completes first-pass removes RETRY CALLS, not merely output tokens.** ② MiniMax's CEO (GS Communacopia, 2026-09-09, block above) on billing moving *"from token-based business model to end-to-end task delivery business model"*. ➤ **Two sellers independently moved the unit of account; a buyer has now published the full hierarchy and the arithmetic underneath it. That is corroboration from the only side that actually pays the bill.**
- 🔴 **THE HARD NUMBERS — ADOPTION AND BUDGET. THE THIRD-PARTY ATTRIBUTION MATTERS, BECAUSE BARCLAYS IS RECITING SURVEYS HERE, NOT ESTIMATING.** Moving from isolated pilots to enterprise-wide deployment **increases AI spend roughly FOURFOLD**, and **93% of organizations report EXCEEDING their AI budgets**, with a majority expecting spend to rise **at least a further 25% over the next year**. ⚠️ **Barclays does not footnote those three figures inline; the only cost-management study in its reference list is McKinsey & Company, "The cost of intelligence: How CIOs can manage AI demand at scale" (July 2026) — treat that link as probable, not confirmed.** Next: **82% of leaders say they are concerned about token costs but only 64% actively monitor usage** or maintain clear budget guardrails (EY US AI Pulse Survey 2026, as reported by Barclays via ISMG, 28 August 2026). And the one that lands closest to this wiki's own users: **in financial services specifically, 61% of firms say AI has ALREADY FALLEN SHORT of the return they expected** for the cost and effort invested (Coastal / Oxford Economics, *"Financial Services AI Trends 2026: Survey Findings From 150 Organizations"*, as reported by Barclays). Barclays' framing: the industry is coining *"tokenomics"* — **a FinOps discipline for AI** — and **cost is named *"the dominant barrier to production AI"*, explicitly *"no longer theoretical."***
- ⚠️🔴 **THE TENSION THIS PAGE SHOULD HOLD OPEN RATHER THAN SMOOTH, BECAUSE BOTH SIDES OF IT ARE NOW ON THE PAGE.** [[ANTHROPIC]]'s GTM says the frontier tier CUT cost per task by two-thirds *"across the board"*; a 150-organisation financial-services survey says **61% of firms have already failed to get the return they expected**. ➤ **The reconciliation Barclays itself implies is that the upgrade case is measured at the TASK level while the disappointment is measured at the DECISION level — the gap being precisely the retries, tool calls, human review and failure modes that rung three does not price. But that is an explanation, not evidence. Until this page has a buyer publishing BOTH numbers on the SAME workflow, the two marks stay side by side, unresolved.**
- **THE OPERATIONAL TEST BARCLAYS PUTS BEHIND THE ROUTING RULE — worth carrying, because it is the buyer's verdict on every vendor benchmark on this page:** *"Vendor benchmarks are a starting point, never a decision."* Their method: **① build a golden data set per workflow** from real institutional cases (*"real earnings calls, real surveillance alerts, real credit files — not synthetic prompts"*) · **② define explicit pass/fail gates** on accuracy, grounding/citation quality, hallucination rate, **tail latency (not just average)** and cost per completed workflow · **③ run every candidate route through the same harness**, holding data, retrieval and evaluation constant and varying only model, provider and processing environment · **④ score RISK-ADJUSTED cost**, including retries, failure behaviour, stressed-capacity latency and fallback performance — *"the costs that token prices hide"* · **⑤ set escalation thresholds in advance** · **⑥ re-benchmark on a cadence**, since *"models improve, prices move and regulation evolves; placement decisions should be reviewed, not frozen."* ➤ **Every price card, $/MW ladder and tokens-per-dollar mark on this page is a vendor or third-party benchmark. This is the buyer saying, in writing, that none of them decides anything.**
- ➤ **Cross-filed [custom-asic-tpu](custom-asic-tpu.md) (the three-models-of-provision taxonomy, the value-capture argument and the "provider count overstates diversification" dependency iceberg) and [secure-sovereign-infrastructure](secure-sovereign-infrastructure.md) (Moody's concentration warning, the DORA / EU AI Act timeline, the confidential-compute mechanism list and the IEA power gating constraint). ⚠️ NO COMPANY PAGE WAS PATCHED FROM THIS SOURCE, DELIBERATELY — it carries no rating, PT, estimate or proprietary forecast on any name, so there is nothing a company page could legitimately take from it.**
