### 🔴🔴🔴 (1) SEMIANALYSIS — THE OPEN-SOURCE $/MW CEILING MOVED, AND IT MOVED IN THE SAME HOUSE'S OWN NUMBERS (independent research, 2026-09-08)
- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE NEW MARK:** asked whether Amazon can earn similar margins selling open-source and closed models on Bedrock — *"we actually wrote a new one on this exact topic a couple weeks ago… we take all the most popular [open-]source models, and we put them on all the most popular chips… after recording all of SemiAnalysis' own [agentic] traces for many months, we replayed all those traces on [InferenceX]… **you can currently generate extremely high numbers of dollars of revenue selling open source models on the newest chips. Specifically, we found that $70 to $80 million [per] megawatt is totally reasonable**"* (⚠ *"for 10K3"* — garble, most likely Kimi K3). *"To be clear, this is a recent phenomenon… a recent trend with the new generation of open source models… I actually talked to a lot of the big open source providers… they essentially confirmed, yes."* The margin conclusion: with *"lower, if not zero, [rev] share"* on open-source, *"it's just totally possible that the actual [margins for] Amazon are comparable."* **THE ASTERISK, IN THE SAME BREATH:** *"the demand for Anthropic and OpenAI models is still just way higher than [open-]source today. [If] Amazon wanted to divert a gigawatt with capacity to serve open source models, there's probably not enough demand for that. Whereas there is enough demand for a gigawatt [of Anthropic]."*
- 🔭 **WHY THIS IS A VARIANT AND NOT A CONFIRMATION — READ IT AGAINST THREE MARKS ALREADY ON THIS WIKI:** (a) **SemiAnalysis's OWN published note of 2026-08-21** ("Are Open Models Catching Up?", carried on [../ANTHROPIC.md](../ANTHROPIC.md) and [../OPENAI.md](../OPENAI.md)) has frontier tokens earning *"as much as $100M per MW per year"* against ***"sub-$30M for open-source TaaS / colo / legacy cloud"*** — the dinner's $70-80M/MW is **2.3-2.7x that published open-source ceiling, from the same house, 18 days later**, and the speaker himself dates the change to *"the new generation of open source models"*; (b) the **09-05 @zephyr_z9 ladder** on this page (Zhipu **$8-10M/MW** realised on $2bn ARR / 200-250MW; [[ANTHROPIC]]/[[OPENAI]] **$40-50M/MW**); (c) SemiAnalysis's own **08-07 SpaceX podcast** (equity-calls archive): frontier revenue per MW by chip generation **GB200 $73.4M/MW/yr → GB300 $99.7M/MW/yr**. ⚠️⚠️ **BASIS GUARD, BINDING: $70-80M/MW is a THROUGHPUT-DERIVED REVENUE *CAPACITY* (tokens/s per GPU on replayed agentic traces × list price), i.e. what a fully-utilised MW COULD bill at today's open-source list prices — it is NOT realised revenue (Zhipu's $8-10M/MW is) and NOT a margin. The frontier-vs-open gap that mattered on 08-21 was ~3x+ on the same capacity basis; on these numbers it is ~1.3x. That narrowing is the datapoint: it says the compute-concentration OFFSET this wiki carries (frontier labs out-bid open-weight labs for incremental GW because they earn more per MW) is WEAKER than SemiAnalysis's August note implied — unless the demand asterisk binds, which the speaker says it does today.** ⚠️ **A second $/MW figure in the same dinner is garbled beyond adoption:** on why Google bought SpaceX compute — *"they realize that you can monetize Anthropic tokens at like **100 [?] a [mega]watt**, and so it makes total sense to spend 50"* — consistent with the $100M/MW frontier mark but the units are not audible. **Not adopted.**

### 🔴🔴🔴 (2) SEMIANALYSIS — "COMPUTE CONSTRAINED" IS THE *ONLY* STATE WHERE LABS AND HYPERSCALERS BOTH WIN, AND THE REV-SHARE IS WHY (independent research, 2026-09-08)
- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE CONDITION, VERBATIM, after an attendee argued Bedrock/Vertex cannot coexist with a two-lab world:** *"**As long as we stay compute constrained, both of them will do very well.**"* — *"So that's the only reason you think Bedrock, [Vertex], and Azure Foundry is going to be as good as they get. It's only until when we are compute constrained."* — *"Yes, the main reason… in all the worlds where the AI labs do really well, I think it follows that you are always compute constrained… **If we are no longer compute constrained, I think everyone will have a very bad time. Not just the clouds. It means… there's less demand for AI than [the labs planned for].**"* Jevons: *"for us to stay compute constrained, [you need] the Jevons paradox holds for the long term"* — *"I think so. Yeah."* ➤ **✅ CONFIRMS and SHARPENS the MS Nowak 09-08 item already on this page (*"a compute constrained environment… holding back the pace at which… mega cap tech companies ship new agentic products"*): Nowak treats the constraint as a headwind to product timelines; SemiAnalysis treats it as the *precondition* for the whole stack's economics. Same fact, opposite sign, both dated the same day.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE MECHANICS OF THE CHANNEL, WHICH THIS PAGE HAD ONLY FROM AN EX-BEDROCK PM (08-28):** labs *"pay some sort of fixed infrastructure and service fee. And then they get an additional revenue percentage on top of that, which goes to Bedrock"*; on a pure compute-rental basis it is *"comparable to slightly lower"* than an enterprise contract, *"But if you add in the rev share, your actual **monetization per gigawatt is way better**."* Plus the eight-month trend: *"it is extremely good to be exposed to changes in spot compute prices… compute has become more scarce than we would have originally predicted. And so all the [buyers] who signed five-year fixed price contracts are really sad. Anyone who is signing short-term contracts or selling token as a service, i.e. Bedrock, is very happy."* ➤ **The allocation logic that keeps the channel alive, in an explicitly *"made-up number"* illustration: a lab with 20GW of demand that can stand up 15 itself, needing 8 for inference, would rather *"acquire an additional five gigawatts from a Bedrock or Vertex and only allocate like three of their own"* — preserving 12GW for training. So the hyperscaler channel is a TRAINING-COMPUTE preservation device for the labs, not a distribution preference — and *"the more compute [the labs are] operating themselves, the less compute-constrained they are, the less leverage the hyperscalers have."***
- 🔴 **THE SCENARIO IN WHICH ENTERPRISES ABANDON THE CLOUD CHANNEL — and it has a dated precedent:** *"let's say in '27, you have a true drop-in remote worker… And Anthropic said… this is our most capable, most dangerous model, we're not comfortable provisioning it to all the cloud providers… I think lots of enterprises would overcome the inertia"* — *"You already saw this to a much smaller degree with **OpenAI Ultra Fast**… the 750 tokens per second, super fast [5.6 Sol]. You cannot get that through Microsoft or Amazon."* ➤ **Read against the 08-28 ex-Bedrock PM's "enterprises route 90/10 through the clouds": SemiAnalysis's break condition is a capability the lab withholds from the channel, and the fast tier has already been that once.**

### 🔴🔴 (3) SEMIANALYSIS — THE LAB RACE AND THE PRICE CARD, AS SEEN FROM INSIDE A TOKENOMICS TEAM (independent research, 2026-09-08)
- 🔴🔴 **FABLE 5.1 vs ASTRA, DECOMPOSED:** *"with Astra, they actually have **the most expensive model in the world** on a per-[token] basis. For the input and output tokens, it's the same price as Fable, but Anthropic notably did a **75% price [cut] for the cached input tokens**, which translates to a **roughly 45% price [reduction] overall**… **Fable 5.1 is actually just a cheaper model than GPT-6 Astro.**"* ✅ **This DECOMPOSES, rather than replaces, the Bernstein 09-02 mark on [../ANTHROPIC.md](../ANTHROPIC.md) (Fable 5.1 *"25% cheaper for typical workloads and 45% cheaper for agentic"*): the 45% is the cached-input cut hitting cache-dominated agentic traffic — consistent with this page's AgentX spec (median input 140K tokens, cache-hit roofline 99.2%). The pricing power the 09-03/04 block logged for the frontier tier (+2.5x) is therefore being exercised on UNCACHED tokens while the cached tier is where the price war runs.** The earlier OpenAI cuts on the 5.6 line are read as a share grab from a weaker position: *"they knew the model was still slightly worse than the Fable."*
- 🔴 **THE RACE:** GPT-5.5 (*"around May"*) → *"the first inflection in OpenAI ARR"*; GPT-5.6 Sol → *"32% growth in July"* (✅ matches Friar's *"enterprise +32% m/m"* on [../OPENAI.md](../OPENAI.md)) and powered Codex; *"now finally we have Astra, which most people believe… is actually a better model than Fable 5.1."* Both labs *"adding still like **over $10 billion in the new ARR per month each**"* ⚠️ **this wiki carries NNARR $10B+/month for [[ANTHROPIC]] (SemiAnalysis 07-08) — the "each" extends it to [[OPENAI]] with no support and is NOT adopted.** IPO order: Anthropic *"in the next couple months"*, OpenAI *"probably in the first half of next year"*; *"we think it's likely they actually delayed their S1 IPO because of this strength of OpenAI"* ⚠️ **speculation, not adopted — the four S-1 timetable marks on [../ANTHROPIC.md](../ANTHROPIC.md) stand.** Twelve months out: *"very high conviction… OpenAI and Anthropic are still… the top [two]… roughly the same. I don't think Anthropic is going to widen the gap"*; Meta and SpaceX *"the only two… that [have] any shot"* at parity, *"not my base case."* No winner-take-all — but *"OpenAI and Anthropic… by far the largest companies in the world"* is entertained.
- 🔴 **THE FIVE WHO CAN TRAIN A FRONTIER MODEL — and the intangible:** research talent, compute, data → *"only five companies… OpenAI, Anthropic, SpaceX, Meta, and Google"*; Chinese labs excluded (*"don't have enough compute"*), NVIDIA (*"doesn't have the researchers"*); the fourth requirement is being *"AGI-pilled"* — otherwise *"you also will not be able to muster the [nerve] to buy the sufficient amount of compute."* ⚠️ **The Google view (*"the clear laggard… 0.1% chance"*, TPUs sold to rivals as proof of no conviction, RL-team departures) is an OPINION about [[GOOG]] and is routed to that page's owner — noted here only because it CONTRADICTS the framing of SemiAnalysis's own published 09-07 TPU note on [../GOOG.md](../GOOG.md), which treats TPU externalisation as a commercial positive. Same facts, opposite valuation, same house.**

### 🔴🔴 (4) SEMIANALYSIS — RSI, HARNESS AND DISTILLATION AS MOATS; THE OPEN-CLOSED GAP MAY STOP BEING CYCLICAL (independent research, 2026-09-08)
- 🔴🔴 **THE GAP:** *"if you just look back at the open versus closed source model gap over time, it's always been cyclical"* — R1 vs o1, Llama 405B vs GPT-4 — open source catches up *"over a period of, say, six to eight months"*, then the frontier steps forward; *"I think we're on the precipice of this new unlock."* And the regime change: *"the trend will no longer be cyclical… the gap keeps widening forever. Just because OpenAI and Anthropic have not actually brought on that much compute yet. And they are on the cusp of bringing on way, way, way more compute in the end of '26, and especially '27 and '28."* ➤ **Note the tension with the same house's 08-21 measurement on this wiki (open catch-up time HALVING each era, 4.8 months for K2.6 past Opus 4.5): the published note measures the gap closing faster; the dinner argues the compute step-up breaks the cycle. Both are SemiAnalysis — the dinner is the forward view, the note the backward measurement.**
- 🔴 **DISTILLATION:** *"definitely a factor today. But it's also definitely not a durable option for the Chinese labs… **DeepSeek has decided to stop doing distillation** because they know it ruins any long-term future"* ⚠️ *"I don't know if… public"* — hearsay, not adopted. The structural asymmetry: *"OpenAI and Anthropic can just distill their own models better than anyone else can. Because they have access to reasoning traces that are hidden to everyone else. And… the individual logits."* Chinese architectural contributions (MLA etc.) acknowledged, but *"largely, the things that they publish are things that OpenAI and Anthropic have already considered."*
- 🔴🔴 **RSI AND HARNESS:** *"RSI and the harness are two very different types of moats… but they're both very real."* RSI — *"the models are speeding up AI research at both OpenAI and Anthropic… look at how fast the new model release cadence is"*, and *"if you're… a Google or Meta or SpaceX or a Chinese lab, it is technically illegal for you to use the OpenAI [or] Anthropic models to do the research."* Harness — *"the person who creates the model also has the right to make the best harness for that particular model"*, and *"all of the wrapper companies have realized [this] over the past six to eight months. Cursor, Cognition, Harvey… they've all since realized that was sort of a fool's dream… Cognition is also training their own models now. Harvey is training their own models now."* ✅ **CONFIRMS from the independent side the 08-31 "model neutrality is dead" block and the 08-17 DB/Cursor row (Composer 2.5) — and extends the list of wrappers-turned-model-trainers to Cognition and Harvey.** ✗ **CONTESTED two days later by MiniMax's CEO — section (6) — and by [[MSFT]] on 09-11 (block below).**

### 🔴🔴 (5) SEMIANALYSIS — DIFFUSION MARKS, THE '27/'28 FORK, AND A FIRST-PERSON CASE OF THIS PAGE'S CORE MECHANISM (independent research, 2026-09-08)
- 🔴🔴 **THE SPEND-PER-HEAD LADDER, none of it previously on this page:** OpenAI *"P50 [usage] per researcher is like **20k a month** today. Carried as ~$20k / ~$200k per month, unit inferred**; **HRT** (quant fund): P50 *"probably higher than that"*, P90 lower; **Ramp** data: *"the top 1% of firms are already using over **$7,000 a month per person**. The average is much lower at like a few hundred dollars a month"*, implying *"a non-trivial portion of those employees are already spending like $3,000 to $4,000 a year on AI"*; SemiAnalysis's investor clients' top users *"3 to 4K a month"*; engineers at *"non-AI-pilled"* companies (*"the DoorDashes and Plaid and Ubers"*) *"one K a month, maybe on average, with [power] users… closer to 10K a month."* *"Is it possible that the P50 people today, one year from now, look more like the P90 people of 2026? It's like **$25 per million in[put] tokens and $125 per million output**… like five x the price… I think the fact that spend has actually been about the same… it just means… the tokens are cheaper on a per unit basis."* ⚠️ **The ~$10M base is ambiguous in the transcript — earlier the same figure is given as SemiAnalysis's *"annual run rate"*; whether it is the firm's revenue or its AI bill is not audible. The MECHANISM is what is adopted, not the base.** ➤ **This is the same shape as the [[UBER]] CFO datapoint on this page (09-07: cost per token down, adoption up, spend broadly stable) — now from a firm whose analysts *"will drop 20 or 30k a day"* on models before a deadline. The speaker's own gloss: *"no one is doing a rigorous job measuring ROI today"*, but a drop-in remote worker *"as smart as your IMO gold medalist… willing to do any tedious task… can spin up 10,000 copies"* being low-ROI *"would probably be an indictment of your business."***

Short term, no"* — the Datadog analogy: the labs are *"10x-ing the Datadog bill to hundreds of millions of dollars a year"* rather than building it ⚠ figure unverified; displacing Cadence/Synopsys at TSMC *"not one of the top priorities"*), **robotics** (*"OpenAI and Anthropic are explicitly trying to build robots so they can automate building data centers"*; diffusion *"slightly slower"*). ### 🔴🔴 (7) ANTHROPIC GTM — THE ANTI-DOWNTIERING WORKED EXAMPLE, THE 10x-PLANNED / ~80x-ACTUAL TOKEN MISS, AND "NOT ANYMORE" ON CAPACITY (company GTM, BTG session, 2026-09-10 — full block on [../ANTHROPIC.md](../ANTHROPIC.md))
- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE WORKED EXAMPLE, AND IT RUNS THE OPPOSITE WAY TO EVERY DOWNTIERING CASE ON THIS PAGE:** *"this customer, they were running a use case with **Sonnet**… spending like **$1.6 per completed task**… they were testing the same on **Opus 5**, much better model in terms of intelligence, and the cost went to **0.65**"* — *"reducing the cost in 66%… And we have seen that across the board."* The instruction: *"the importance of start measuring **cost per task, not cost per token**. Because if the token is very, very cheap, but you have a need of a lot of them to complete a task, it's going to be expensive."* ⚠️ **One customer, one eval, from memory ($1.6 also rendered "1.5"); no token counts.** ➤ **Set beside the [[UBER]] CFO (09-07, moved tasks to cheaper/open-weight models) and the Rippling routing case (GLM 5.2, token spend 40%→15% of R&D headcount): the same "cost per task" unit produces OPPOSITE model choices depending on whether the workload is token-light-and-hard or token-heavy-and-easy. Neither direction is the rule; the unit is.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE DEMAND MISS BEHIND 2026's CAPACITY SQUEEZE:** *"[Dario] mentioned that we had a plan that would grow like **10 times** our demand… from 2025 to 2026… we're going to have like a 10x growth in token consumption, **we had like 80, not 10**."* ⚠️ **Quoted from memory by a GTM lead; the wiki's 2026-05-07 morning briefing carries an "Anthropic 80x growth / new compute" echo, so the figure circulated before — still UNVERIFIED as a series point.** And the present tense: asked whether a buyer of *"a million dollars of API"* is refused — *"No, no… **Not anymore.**"* ➤ **If both are right, the binding constraint in 1H26 was a planning error against an 8x forecast miss, and it has cleared on the serving side — which is the same "compute constrained" state SemiAnalysis (section 2) says is the ONLY one in which the labs' economics work. The two sources point different ways on whether that state persists into 2027; this page holds both.**
- 🔴 **NEW MODELS AS A CAPACITY LEVER, AND CHANNEL-NEUTRAL PRICING:** *"the new models… are becoming not only more… intelligent, but… more efficient when it comes to capacity. So they can do more things using less."* On channel: *"we are channel neutral. So in our commercial policies, if you want to consume Claude via Google Cloud or AWS, **we make no pricing difference**."* ➤ **Relevant to the rev-share arithmetic in section 2: if list price is identical across channels and Bedrock/Vertex take a fixed fee plus a revenue percentage, the lab's NET per token is lower through the cloud channel by construction — which is why GTM's *"first party is growing faster than the third party — Yeah"* (on [../ANTHROPIC.md](../ANTHROPIC.md)) is a margin statement as well as a distribution one.**
- **THE OPEN-SOURCE PITCH FROM THE FRONTIER SELLER ('Enio', AI-natives):** *"three things. Intelligence, cost, and [behavior]… there's a Pareto curve… but… **very few companies have a mature eval practice** to go and… understand the total cost of hosting an open source [model]… **we're not against open-weight models**… there's a lot of overhead that goes into hosting."* The bet stated as a bet: *"the marginal return from highest intelligence models, that market is bigger"*; a humanoid-robotics customer where *"99% of their usage is Fable 5.1"* — *"they want to ship."* ➤ **Same buyer-side logic as [[AMD]]'s "increased token cost but much greater productivity gain" (09-11 block below): where the buyer measures output, the frontier tier wins; where it measures cost per employee, it does not — Enio's own framing: *"If you're only stuck here, like reducing cost per employee, you'll probably be eaten by your competition."***

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-11 (/run-inbox, 23:00 window) — **THREE INDEPENDENT SAME-DAY READS ON THE ECONOMICS OF SERVING TOKENS, AND THEY CONVERGE ON ONE THING THIS PAGE HAS ONLY HAD ANECDOTALLY: THE UNIT THAT MATTERS IS *WHICH MODEL RUNS THE WORKLOAD*, NOT THE PRICE PER TOKEN. PLUS THE FIRST NAMED MARGIN CONSEQUENCE OF MODEL MIX INSIDE A SHIPPING PRODUCT — [[MSFT]] SAYS AN OPENAI MODEL IN COPILOT IS "ECONOMICALLY BETTER… BECAUSE WE DON'T PAY RENTS THERE".**

_Sources: **(1) Hardware expert call #28 — Fran Cardells, Chief Scientist at Quantoid, ex-Senior Director Microsoft, 2026-09-11** ⚠️ **ONE EXPERT'S OPINION; HOST BROKER NOT IDENTIFIABLE FROM THE TRANSCRIPT; MACHINE-TRANSCRIBED; NO ESTIMATES, RATINGS OR PTs — mechanism only, nothing to adopt.** **(2) [[MSFT]] IR/management fireside hosted by Jefferies (London), 2026-09-11 — a MANAGEMENT source, NOT Jefferies research: no rating, no PT, no estimates** ⚠️ **machine-transcribed and the ASR garbles product names; quoted as heard and marked.** **(3) [[AMD]] management — Dan McNamara (SVP/GM Compute & Enterprise AI) and Matt Ramsay (CVP Financial Strategy & IR) — fireside at the Goldman Sachs Communacopia + Technology Conference, 2026-09-11 — a MANAGEMENT source, NOT GS research.** ⚠️ **NOTHING ON THIS PAGE IS SUPERSEDED: no price card, token-share series, TAM, ARR mark, rating or PT already carried is replaced.**_

### 🔴🔴 (1) THE EXPERT'S FRAME FOR WHAT THE LABS ARE ACTUALLY COMPETING ON — **COST OF ENERGY, NOT PRICE PER TOKEN** (expert opinion, 2026-09-11)

- **The objective he assigns to the labs is unambiguous: *"that's precisely the objective that both ANTHROPIC AND OPENAI, or Frontier Labs have, which is TO REDUCE THE COST PER TOKEN"*, and the strategy is the industrial-policy analogue: *"the way to win this race is the same way that… COUNTRIES HAVE WON THE INDUSTRIAL PRODUCTIVITY RACE, which is, I'm trying to LOWER THE COST OF ENERGY… if I'm in the lowest possible threshold, I can get A LOT OF INDUSTRY TO FLOURISH ON TOP, because they are not going to be living somewhere else."* Applied to tokens: *"if you think about the Frontier Labs trying to become like A SOVEREIGN LAND WHERE THEY CAN OFFER THE BEST POSSIBLE COST PER TOKEN, then EVERYTHING EVERYBODY'S GOING TO BE TRYING TO BUILD ON TOP OF THAT."*** ➤ **This is the cleanest statement of the deflation spine this page runs on, and it makes the price cuts a LAND-GRAB rather than a margin event: the cut is the instrument that keeps the application layer resident. Read it directly against the page's own counter-examples — the 09-03/04 frontier tier repricing UP 2.5x, and Zhipu's 40x volume WITH price up 101% — which are the cases where the sovereign-land logic did NOT bind.**
- 🔴 **AND A PRACTICAL THRESHOLD THIS PAGE DID NOT HAVE, FOR WHEN SELF-HOSTING OPEN WEIGHTS IS EVEN WORTH EVALUATING: *"if you're buying, probably on the NORTH OF A 10 BILLION TOKENS PER YEAR, you're probably going to get like A SPECIFIC COMMERCIALS that make you more palatable the thing"*, and only *"if you have a VERY HUGE VOLUME, is when it's the point where you can at least TRY TO LOOK INTO IT, or make the exercise."*** ⚠️ **One expert's rule of thumb, no workload basis and no arithmetic behind it — do NOT adopt it as a breakeven. Carry it as a screening threshold: below ~10bn tokens/yr the self-hosting debate is, in his view, not worth the engineering.** ➤ **His warning to the enterprises cutting API spend is the same mechanism inverted: they are cutting against a declining price curve — *"people are not realising that THE CURVE IS DECLINING. That the same way has happened WITH COMPUTE, has always been on a DECREASING CURVE."***
- 🔬 **AND THE MECHANISM LINE WORTH KEEPING, BECAUSE IT EXPLAINS WHY CONTEXT SPEND IS NOT DISCRETIONARY: *"if you remember a couple of years ago, three years ago, WE WERE TALKING ABOUT HALLUCINATIONS. WE DON'T SPEAK ABOUT THAT ANY LONGER. WHY? BECAUSE THE AGENTS HAVE CONTEXT"* — the enterprise data loaded into the cache is what replaced the hallucination problem.** ➤ **The token bill and the quality of the output are therefore the same line item, which is the structural argument against the "just downtier everything" version of tokenmaxxing this page has logged failing at named accounts (08-31).** ⚠️ **The same call's HBM / memory-hierarchy content is filed on [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md), not restated here.**

### 🔴🔴🔴 (2) [[MSFT]] MANAGEMENT PUTS A MARGIN CONSEQUENCE ON MODEL MIX — THE MOST CONCRETE ECONOMIC DATAPOINT OF THE THREE (management, 2026-09-11)

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE ADMISSION, VERBATIM, AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME THIS PAGE HAS A PLATFORM OWNER RANKING ITS OWN SUPPLIERS BY MARGIN INSIDE A SHIPPING PRODUCT: *"if you're in CO-PILOT for example and you're using an OPEN AI MODEL, that's ECONOMICALLY BETTER THAN IF THEY'RE USING AN ANTHROPIC MODEL BECAUSE WE DON'T PAY RENTS THERE, but we obviously ultimately want the customer to get the great experience."*** The structure behind it: *"we DON'T PAY THEM A REV SHARE… THEY STILL DO PAY US A REV SHARE. But we also DON'T PAY RENTS ON THE MODELS given WE OWN THE IP. It does give us A STRUCTURAL COST ADVANTAGE"*, plus *"we get ALL OF THEIR IP FOR FREE UNTIL THE END OF 2032."* ➤ **THE READ-THROUGH THIS PAGE SHOULD CARRY: [[MSFT]]'s gross margin on agentic products is a function of MODEL MIX, and the mix has a house favourite. Every routing/"auto mode" claim from Microsoft now has a disclosed economic gradient behind it — the router is not neutral, and management said so on the record. It is also the mirror image of the token-absorption trade this page logged on 09-09: Salesforce absorbs [[ANTHROPIC]] token cost on Slackbot; Microsoft pays no rent on the models it routes to. Same product category, opposite input cost.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE STRATEGIC BET IS *AGAINST* THE INTEGRATED MODEL+HARNESS, WHICH IS THE STRUCTURAL DEBATE ON THIS PAGE: *"there's OVER 11,000 MODELS IN FOUNDRY"* and *"we believe customers are going to want to go where there's AN ENTERPRISE GRADE HARNESS THAT IS RICH WITH IQ AND DATA THAT ALLOWS YOU TO TOGGLE BETWEEN MODEL A, MODEL B, MODEL C, all the way to the 11,000 plus models… AS OPPOSED TO saying, I'm all in on this notion that YOU NEED A HARNESS AND A MODEL THAT'S INTEGRATED. That's NEVER the world we thought would exist."* The alternative offered is routing: *"Or you put on AUTO MODE… Let us ROUTE YOU TO THE MODEL THAT MAKES THE MOST SENSE"*, available *"in foundry… in GitHub Co-Pilot… in Entra 65 Co-Pilot"* (⚠️ **"Entra 65" is an ASR garble of the Entra/Agent-365 product naming — quoted as heard, not corrected**). And the sentence that states the whole theme: *"DOES THAT MEAN THAT EVERY WORKLOAD NEEDS A FRONTIER MODEL? WE DON'T THINK SO."*** ➤ **The largest enterprise software vendor is underwriting the downtiering thesis as PRODUCT STRATEGY, not as cost control — and it says the rise of open models *"plays into where Microsoft can really differentiate."* Read against the 08-31 block on this page declaring model neutrality dead as a business model after [[OPENAI]] cut Cursor off: Microsoft's answer is that neutrality is viable if you own the harness AND hold free IP rights to one frontier model.**
- ⚠️🔴 **THE DATED CAUTION ON CONSUMPTIVE MONETISATION, AND IT IS AN EXPLICIT WARNING AGAINST THE ANALOGY EVERY MODEL IS USING: *"the CONSUMPTIVE PIECES WILL TAKE SOME TIME to become big drivers. So DON'T USE THE PARALLEL OF GITHUB, which had SIX MONTHS OF BASICALLY UNMETERED USAGE that then suddenly we turn on the consumptive billing ON JUNE 1ST. There's ALL THIS BACKED UP USAGE that we suddenly start monetizing. THAT'S NOT TRUE IN THE M365 CONSUMPTIVE WORLD. IT'S MORE OF A STANDING START."*** ➤ **A named, dated mechanism for why the agentic consumption ramp at the largest seat base will look SLOWER than the GitHub precedent that anchors most estimates — the GitHub step-up monetised a stock of backed-up usage; M365 has no such backlog. Management explicitly declines to size it (*"we haven't sized them specifically"*), and it pairs with the per-seat-to-consumptive ARPU push/pull they describe. ⚠️ No number given; do NOT infer a ramp shape from it, only a direction.**

### 🔴🔴 (3) [[AMD]] MANAGEMENT — ROUTING AS THE ENTERPRISE'S DEFAULT, AND THE FIRST VENDOR STATEMENT OF THE TRADE AS A *NET* (management, 2026-09-11)

- 🔴🔴 **THE FRAMING OF THE WHOLE THEME, FROM THE SILICON SIDE: *"HOW TO APPLY THE RIGHT TOKENS TO THE RIGHT PROBLEM RELATIVE TO THE COST OF THE TOKEN VERSUS THE RETURN OF THE TOKEN. THAT'S A VERY LARGE CONTINUUM"* (Ramsay), with the stated design goal *"to provide very compelling TOKENS PER DOLLAR AND TCO"* regardless of *"whether it's open-weight models, frontier models"*.** And the product evidence: at Advancing AI Day AMD showed **policy-based intelligent routing** across **frontier / open-weight / SLMs** — *"you have a POLICY-BASED ROUTER depending on the task. You are looking at PERFORMANCE, LATENCY… obviously, SECURITY… if it is highly secure, IT STAYS ON-PREM"* — because *"ENTERPRISE ARE INFINITELY HYBRID, and we believe that will continue."* ➤ **Third independent vendor this week describing routing as the default enterprise architecture (with [[MSFT]]'s "auto mode" above and JPM's Debate-3 framing on 09-10). ⚠️ What none of them will state is the MIX — how much of the token volume actually lands on the cheap tier. Until one does, the margin-transfer argument on this page stays directional.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE FIRST TIME A VENDOR STATES ITS OWN ADOPTION AS A NET RATHER THAN A SAVING — which is exactly the expert's compression argument arriving from a buyer: AMD is running [[OPENAI]] and [[ANTHROPIC]] tools across its engineering organisation and benchmarks itself *"on the bleeding edge of AI adoption internally"*: *"IT'S COME WITH AN INCREASED TOKEN COST, BUT IT'S COME WITH AN EVEN A MUCH GREATER PRODUCTIVITY GAIN across the organization"*, with the payoff framed as time-to-market rather than cost — *"one's for efficiency but WHEN YOU CAN DRIVE A FASTER TIME TO MARKET, THAT'S WHERE THE REAL RUBBER HITS THE ROAD."*** ➤ **Add it to this page's small set of first-party buyer marks: the token bill went UP and the buyer is happy. That is the same direction as Airbnb's "I don't care how many tokens" (09-09) and the opposite of the CFO downtiering case that failed on 08-31 — the deciding variable in all three is whether the buyer measures cost or output.**

### 🔴🔴🔴 (4) [[AMD]] — THE AGENTIC COMPUTE-MIX ARGUMENT, AND IT IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN A RATIO (management, 2026-09-11)

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE ARCHITECTURE CLAIM, WHICH IS THE BEST VERSION OF THIS ARGUMENT THE PAGE HAS: agentic inference is not the 2022-2025 SaaS datacentre. *"If you think about the world of ChatGPT from November 2022 to probably into last year. VERY LINEAR… PROMPT RESPONSE… You know the ratios. Everyone can calculate that very easily."* Against that: *"with agentic, it's AN ENTIRELY CONTINUOUS FLOW. It's a COMPLETELY DIFFERENT COMPUTE PARADIGM. It's 24/7 CHURNING, WITHIN A SANDBOX, SPAWNING NUMBERS OF DIFFERENT AGENTS."* The consequence is a NEW class of compute, not a bigger GPU bill: *"you pull in A WHOLE NEW CLASS OF COMPUTE, which is for agentic. CONTROL PLANE, API CALLS, DATABASE QUERIES, TOOL EXECUTION, AND THAT IS PURE CPU-BASED… So the GPU SERVERS GROW ALSO. But… those GENERAL PURPOSE SERVERS GET UPLIFTED TOO, because more and more calls to those."*** ➤ **For a token page the implication is that cost per token understates the cost of an agentic workload: the control plane, the tool calls and the database queries are billed compute that never appears in a $/M-token comparison. Every routing economics argument on this page is priced on the model call alone.**
- 🔴 **AND MANAGEMENT SAYS IT UNDER-CALLED IT: *"At our Advancing AI Day in November, I said that, 'Look, there's MULTIPLE AREAS OF GROWTH FOR THE CPU.' We called it, but WE CALLED IT TOO LOW. So WE'VE UPPED IT NOW."*** The segmentation offered: the GPU head node (*"IPC and high frequency… keeping the GPUs busy"*), the **agentic sandbox CPU** (*"threads per watt with the right level of per core performance"*, Venice at 256 cores) and the traditional general-purpose fleet.
- ⚠️⚠️ **AND THE RATIO — MANAGEMENT REFUSES TO GIVE ONE, AND THE ONE NUMBER OFFERED COMES WITH THE HEDGE ATTACHED: asked for CPU content per gigawatt, *"we HAVEN'T BEEN SUPER SPECIFIC about what that ratio is… because IT DOES LOOK DIFFERENT DEPENDING ON WHAT CUSTOMER IT IS"*, and McNamara: *"It DEPENDS… I would say that IT'S GROWING. If you think about it TODAY, WE'RE SAYING 1:1 SORT OF RATIO, AND IT'S GOING TO CONTINUE TO GROW. But it's just VERY HARD TO PINPOINT, PLUG THIS INTO A MODEL and you'll get what you're looking for."*** ⚠️ **EXPLICITLY NOT A MODELLING INPUT, by the speaker. Do not build a CPU-per-GW series on it.** ✅ **CORROBORATION, NOT NEW: this page already carries the CPU:XPU coefficient moving 1:4 → 1:2 → ~1:1 (08-13) and BofA's series running THROUGH 1:1 to ~1.5 CPUs per XPU by 2030. AMD management independently placing "today" at ~1:1 and rising is the vendor-side confirmation of that series' current level — and its refusal to publish a ratio is itself the finding, because every model on this page uses one.** ➤ **It also extends, rather than repeats, the [[AMD]] item already on this page from Citi Global TMT (09-08, "chatbot inference to agentic inference… opportunities for both inferencing silicon and for the CPUs to run the agents"): that call asserted the CPU pull, this one names the workload classes that create it and says the November guide was too low.**

- ➤ **Cross-filed [[MSFT]], [[AMD]], [[OPENAI]], [[ANTHROPIC]], [[CRM]], [[NVDA]], [hbm-memory](hbm-memory.md) (the expert call’s memory-hierarchy content), [ai-datacenter-power](ai-datacenter-power.md).**

## 🆕⚠️ 2026-09-10 (/run-inbox) — **J.P. MORGAN MAKES "DO TOKEN COSTS BECOME AN INCREMENTAL HEADWIND FOR SOFTWARE?" ITS DEBATE #2 FOR THE WHOLE SECTOR — BUT THE DECK ARRIVED AS AN UNPOPULATED TEMPLATE, SO THIS PAGE CARRIES ITS PROSE AND NOTHING ELSE.**

_Source: [**J.P. Morgan · Samik Chatterjee, CFA (AC) — "Enterprise Software — Fall Series Conference Call Slides" (2026-09-10)**](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/JPM_Enterprise_Software__2026-09-10_5443313.html)._

⚠️⚠️ **READ THIS BEFORE USING ANYTHING BELOW — THE DECK ON DISK IS LARGELY AN UNPOPULATED TEMPLATE.** It still ships its placeholder strings — `[Key takeaway #1]`, `[Key players / demand drivers / J.P. Morgan view]`, *"Columns: Ticker | Rating | Price target | …"*, *"One-line thesis per name, grouped by rating"* — and the comps tables, the rank-order table and most charts are **IMAGES WITH NO TEXT LAYER**. **Only genuine prose is logged here. NO TABLE CONTENT WAS INFERRED AND NO CHART VALUE IS CARRIED**, including the stray `$3,000B` axis label sitting loose in the text layer of the AI-infra capex chart — chart values extracted from a PDF text layer mis-pair with their labels, so it is not used. **Nothing below is a rating, a price target or an estimate, and none is adopted.**

🔴 **THE HOUSE POSITIONING, WHICH IS PROSE AND THEREFORE USABLE: Top Picks **MSFT · NOW · DDOG · SNOW**; Top Avoids **INTU · FIG**.** ⚠️ *Cover-page list only — no ratings, PTs or upside/downside came through, because the rank-order table is an image.*

🔴🔴 **DEBATE 2 IS THIS PAGE'S OWN QUESTION, ASKED BY A BULGE-BRACKET SOFTWARE FRANCHISE: "DO TOKEN COSTS BECOME AN INCREMENTAL HEADWIND FOR SOFTWARE COMPANIES?"** JPM's prose answer, in three parts: *"As AI moves from pilot to production, **LLM ECONOMICS/TOKENS BECOME CENTRAL TO BUYING DECISIONS**"*; *"**GROSS MARGIN PRESSURE SHOWS UP AT AN AGGREGATE LEVEL, WITH VARIED IMPACT BY COMPANY**"*; and the takeaway — *"**EARLY GROSS MARGIN PRESSURE HAS BEEN LED BY SOFTWARE COMPANIES PRIORITIZING ADOPTION / ENGAGEMENT ON THEIR AI OFFERINGS**."* ➤ **That is the same mechanism this page carries from the sellers' own mouths — [[CRM]]'s *"we're covering some of the token spend"* and Slack's *"we actually take on the burden of token economics ourselves"* (09-09) — here generalised across a coverage universe and stated as an OBSERVED AGGREGATE rather than a forecast. It is the first time a large software franchise has made the token bill one of its numbered sector debates.**
- **THE METHOD AND THE CONSTITUENT LIST, BOTH IN PROSE AND BOTH WORTH HAVING:** the underlying exhibit measures the **median, 25th percentile and 75th percentile CHANGE IN LTM GROSS MARGIN FROM 4Q23 THROUGH 1Q26 (calendar-year basis)** across a named set — **Figma, Cloudflare, Workday, Twilio, ZoomInfo, Snowflake, ServiceNow, Dynatrace, Dropbox, Datadog, DocuSign, HubSpot, Paycom, Monday.com, Zoom Communications, Adobe, Intuit and Salesforce** (18 names). ⚠️ **NO PERCENTILE VALUE IS LOGGED — the chart has no text layer, and this page does not guess at pixels.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **AND CARRY JPM'S OWN FOOTNOTE CAVEAT, BECAUSE IT IS EXACTLY THE KIND THAT GETS DROPPED IN A RELAY:** *"**Percentile values are calculated INDEPENDENTLY EACH PERIOD**, so the companies represented at each percentile level **VARY OVER TIME** based on relative performance and **DO NOT TRACK A FIXED COMPANY OR SUBSET**."* ➤ **The series is a CROSS-SECTIONAL DISTRIBUTION, not a panel. It cannot be read as "the median company's gross margin fell by X", and no one should chain it into a per-company margin bridge.**

🔴 **DEBATE 3 — "DO ROUTING + MODEL MIX CHANGES REVERSE COST-TO-SERVE PRESSURE?" — IS THE MITIGATION LEG OF THE SAME ARGUMENT, AND JPM STOPS SHORT OF SAYING IT WORKS ON ITS OWN.** *"**MULTI-MODEL ORCHESTRATION AND ROUTING** can optimize **COST-TO-QUALITY PER REQUEST**"*; *"**SMALLER/DOMAIN MODELS** can handle **BOUNDED, HIGH-VOLUME TASKS** at lower cost"*; *"**OPEN-WEIGHT MODELS** can offer **MEANINGFUL COST LEVERAGE** versus frontier models"*; takeaway — *"software companies need to pull **BOTH LEVERS, MONETIZATION AND LEVERAGE OF LOWER COST MODELS**, to drive **GROSS MARGIN RECOVERY**."* ➤ **Note what that concedes: cheaper models alone do NOT fix the margin in JPM's own framing — monetisation has to arrive alongside. That is the clearest sell-side statement this page carries that downtiering is NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT, and it sits directly on top of the page's 08-31 counter-example, where a CFO tried downtiering, hit a quality wall and went back to the expensive model.**

🔴 **THE MODEL PRICE CARD IN JPM'S DEBATE-3 FOOTNOTE, SOURCED TO "COMPANY WEBSITES AS OF 7/21/26" (input/output per Mtok):** [[ANTHROPIC]]'s **Sonnet 5** was on **INTRODUCTORY pricing of $2/$10 through 8/31/26, REVERTING TO $3/$15 on 9/1/26** — i.e. a **+50% list increase that has ALREADY TAKEN EFFECT**; and [[ANTHROPIC]]'s **Opus 5.0, priced at $5/$25 and released 7/24/26**, is **excluded from JPM's comparison table** *"as third-party benchmarking has not been published as of 7/24/26."* Also referenced in the same footnote: **Kimi K2.7 Code**, whose total dollar cost to run the AA Intelligence Index is **undisclosed by Artificial Analysis**. ⚠️ **A PROMOTIONAL PRICE EXPIRING IS A PRICE RISE WITHOUT A PRICE ANNOUNCEMENT — the kind of move that never appears in a "token prices only fall" series. Logged for this page's deflation-spine ledger; not adopted as a repricing event, and no volume or mix is attached to it.**

🔴 **DEBATE 1 — "WHERE IS AI DISRUPTION RISK LIKELY IN THE STACK?"** *"**Risk is generally HIGHER TOWARD THE APPLICATION LAYER and LOWER IN INFRASTRUCTURE**"*; *"**DETERMINISTIC OUTCOMES + WORKFLOW/SYSTEM-OF-RECORD CONTEXT** are key to translating **COMPUTE INTO BUSINESS RESULTS**"*; and the nuance JPM puts against its own framework — *"**LARGE PLATFORMS OFTEN SPAN MULTIPLE LAYERS**, so **'ONE LABEL' CAN BE MISLEADING** and also similarly **TOUGH TO DISRUPT**."* Takeaway: *"we are focusing on **DISRUPTION RISK THROUGH POSITIONS IN THE STACK AND IMPACT ON FUNNEL CREATION**."* ⚠️ **The accompanying stack map is an image — no company was read off it, so this page carries the RULE and not the placements.**

⚠️ **DEBATE 4 — "SEAT-BASED HEADWINDS vs CONSUMPTION-BASED MONETIZATION", AND IT NAMES TWO COMPANIES DELIBERATELY GOING THE OTHER WAY.** *"**CONSUMPTION MONETIZATION** is positioned as **INCREASINGLY IMPORTANT** across coverage"*; *"**SEAT-COUNT HEADWINDS EXIST**, but revenue drivers can **SHIFT TOWARD CONSUMPTION** over time"*; *"**MOST FIRMS ARE NOW MOVING FROM ADOPTION-FIRST TO MONETIZATION FOCUS**, although some like **HUBSPOT AND ADOBE** have decided to pursue **HIGHER ADOPTION**"*; takeaway — *"**CONSUMPTION UPSIDES HAVE TO OVERPOWER SEAT-BASED HEADWINDS**."* ➤ **That last clause is a testable claim rather than a view: it says the seat-erosion drag is real and already running, and that the consumption line has to out-run it.** ➤ **A CROSS-REFERENCE THE DECK SETS UP BUT DOES NOT MAKE, FLAGGED AS THIS PAGE'S INFERENCE AND NOT JPM'S: Debate 2 says early gross-margin pressure has been LED BY the vendors prioritising adoption/engagement, and Debate 4 names HUBSPOT and ADOBE as the two that chose higher adoption — both of which sit inside Debate 2's 18-name constituent set. JPM never joins those two sentences. If the join holds, HubSpot and Adobe are the cleanest natural experiment available for "adopt first, monetise later".**
## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-09 (/run-inbox, 23:00 window — **PRIMARY TRANSCRIPTS**) — **THE TWO LARGEST WORKFLOW INCUMBENTS ANSWERED THE FRONTIER-LAB QUESTION ON THE SAME STAGE, ON THE SAME DAY, IN OPPOSITE WAYS — AND THE DIFFERENCE IS *WHO OWNS THE TOKEN BILL*. PLUS: SALESFORCE FINALLY NAMES THE PRODUCT BEHIND ITS "WE'RE COVERING SOME OF THE TOKEN SPEND" ADMISSION.**

_Sources: **MANAGEMENT primaries**, Bloomberg transcripts of two Goldman Sachs Communacopia + Technology Conference sessions, both 2026-09-09 — [[NOW]] CEO Bill McDermott (*FINAL*) and [[CRM]] President/CCO Bill Patterson with Slack EVP/GM Rob Seaman (*INITIAL DRAFT*). Neither is broker research: no ratings, no price targets, no estimates. ⚠️ The CRM draft mislabels Seaman as "Robin Washington" (COO/CFO) — the Slack quotes are Seaman's._

🔴🔴🔴 **THE NAMED EXPOSURE, WHICH IS WHAT THIS PAGE HAS BEEN MISSING SINCE 08-27.** This page's central application-vendor datapoint is Mike Spencer's admission that Salesforce *"didn't raise margin guidance on the year… because we're **COVERING SOME OF THE TOKEN SPEND**"* — with no product named. It is now named, by Slack's own GM: **Slackbot is *"built on top of Anthropic"* and *"one of the bets that we make is **we actually take on the burden of token economics ourselves**"*, bundled into Salesforce's *"highest grade plan."*** ➤ **An uncapped variable inference cost sitting behind a fixed-price seat SKU. And the contrast inside the same company is what makes it measurable: on **Agentforce, Piper and Fin the customer is explicitly *"not paying for Anthropic Tokens"*** because they contract with [[ANTHROPIC]] directly (*"you do that with them, with Anthropic"*). So the same vendor runs BOTH models side by side — pass-through on the agent platform, absorption on the collaboration layer — and only the second one hits its gross margin.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/CRM_GS_09-09-26.html)

🔴🔴 **THE DOWNTIERING IS NOW A DELIBERATE PRODUCT STRATEGY AIMED AT THE CUSTOMER'S TOKEN BUDGET, DESCRIBED IN FULL.** Seaman: Salesforce will *"tie into any harness that you use, but we are going to have an **opinionated harness out of the box**, and that is Slackbot"*; the pitch to the buyer is *"an easier way to **budget**"* — *"our knowledge workers that are customers are able to go to Slackbot and get done much of what they might have done in an **open-ended cost tool**"*, while *"the more specific higher order… **deep knowledge work**… will happen over there"* (Claude Code, "Cowork", unlimited-token seats). And the monetisation claim: *"we see often **the premium that we're getting coming at the expense of utilization of other tools**, frankly"* — with Huddles and documents *"taking **30% of the workload** off of some of those other tools."* ➤➤ **This is the cleanest statement on this page of the two-tier equilibrium the theme predicts: a cheap bundled default absorbing the high-frequency/low-value tokens, with frontier-priced seats reserved for the residual. The vendor captures the spread — and explicitly says the spread comes out of a COMPETITOR'S usage, not out of new budget.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/CRM_GS_09-09-26.html)

🔴🔴 **THE OPPOSITE ANSWER, SAME CONFERENCE, SAME DAY — AND IT IS AN EXPLICIT REFUSAL TO CARRY TOKEN COST.** [[NOW]]'s McDermott frames the labs' economics as somebody else's problem: the intelligence they create is *"inspiring, but it's also **very costly**, and it will be very costly to continue the pace"*, whereas ServiceNow gets *"to take advantage of all that compute, of all that intelligence… **without the cost** associated with diving deeply into compute or deeply into the lab model, where **they have to spend billions and billions** to create this knowledge. **We just get to benefit from all of it**."* Then the direct shot at the Claudeforce construction: *"**while others have made MARKETING MOVES, announcing those exciting partnerships**… we've already been doing that for months and months now with complete integration into **all** the lab models… **we'll hire the right model for the right job** and will enable the customer to **manage the economics of their token usage** by using the right platform for the right use case… **we didn't need a marketing event to actually pull that off**."* **And he gives the theme its phrase of the day:** *"we've moved from an environment of, I would say, **token maxing to token economics**"*, *"now you're into the **token economics game**."* ➤➤ **TWO INCUMBENTS, TWO ANSWERS, AND THE TESTABLE CONSEQUENCE IS A GROSS-MARGIN SPREAD: Salesforce buys distribution and depth by absorbing inference on one product and taking an equity stake; ServiceNow buys optionality by routing and absorbing nothing. If the theme's deflation spine holds, the router wins the margin; if frontier capability keeps compounding and integration depth is what customers pay for, the absorber wins the revenue. Both companies report quarterly — this is a falsifiable pair, and it should be tracked as one.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/NOW_GS_09-09-26.html)

⚠️ **THE ELASTICITY WALL, FROM THE DISTRIBUTION SIDE — the counterweight to this page's own price-cut-drives-volume evidence.** Two weeks after the Claudeforce launch Salesforce segments its own base into three cohorts and only the first is buying: heavy early adopters who had already hand-built the integration; a second cohort *"still needing some convincing"* on *"the **economics and the true math** of how this works at scale"* because *"**not every small business has an endless token budget**"*; and a third with geographic *"**AI hesitancy**"*, *"not here in San Francisco per se, but as you go to like other countries around the world."* ➤ **The affordability ceiling is being reported by the seller, in the mid-market and ex-US, at current frontier prices — which is exactly where a price cut would show up first if the elasticity result this page logged on 09-09 generalises beyond the API.** ⚠️ No attach rate, pipeline or ARR was given for Claudeforce; cohort sizes are qualitative. [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/CRM_GS_09-09-26.html)

⚠️ **AND ONE VENDOR TCO RATIO, LOGGED BUT NOT ADOPTED, BECAUSE IT POINTS THE OTHER WAY ON THE BUILD-VS-BUY LEG:** McDermott claims enterprises attempting to build agentic workflows outside a platform found *"there is a **5x to 10x TCO difference**, meaning it's 5 times to 10 times **more expensive** to even try that than actually use a proven platform"* — on integration, security and ongoing maintenance rather than on tokens — alongside *"**90% of the organizations** in the world economy today **do not have visibility on the agents**"* and *"the average company is using **47 different security tools**."* ⚠️ **No methodology, no sample, no source. Recorded because it is the incumbent's counter to the cheap-tokens-enable-DIY argument, not because it is evidence.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/NOW_GS_09-09-26.html)

🆕🔴🔴 **ADDENDUM 2026-09-14 — THE SAME CONFERENCE, READ ACROSS ALL FIVE ENTERPRISE SESSIONS AT ONCE, AND THE CONVERGENCE IS THE FINDING: [[NOW]], SAP, Databricks, [[SNOW]] and [[CRM]] ALL PITCHED GOVERNANCE + BUSINESS CONTEXT + MODEL-AGNOSTIC ROUTING AS THE CONTROL POINT — AND AGENT MONETISATION WAS UNRESOLVED AT EVERY SINGLE ONE.** _(Source: the analyst's OWN consolidated GS Communacopia 2026 conference notes, 2026-09-14 — company MANAGEMENT speaking at a Goldman-hosted conference, NOT Goldman Sachs research; no rating, PT or estimate appears anywhere in the document.)_ ➤ **For this page the convergence is the point, not any one pitch: when five vendors independently describe the SAME moat in the same week, routing and governance are a CATEGORY REQUIREMENT rather than a differentiator — which is the structural version of the 08-31 finding that model neutrality stopped being a viable business model. The differentiator has to be somewhere else, and on the evidence of this week it is WHO OWNS THE TOKEN BILL.** 🔴🔴 **AND THAT IS NOW A FOUR-NAME, FALSIFIABLE SPLIT — the escalation of the two-name pair logged above. ABSORBING: [[CRM]] takes the Slackbot token burden inside a fixed-price seat (already on this page). ACCEPTING DILUTION OUTRIGHT: [[SNOW]]'s CEO said on stage that if CoCo adoption materially accelerates revenue while REDUCING gross margin, he would consider that an ACCEPTABLE TRADE — with the commitment redirected to OPERATING leverage, and the recovery located at the inference GATEWAY (his ranked hierarchy is CoCo/CoWork first, gateway second, being a mere data backend to someone else's model last; simple resale of another provider's inference he called a poor-quality business). REFUSING: [[NOW]]'s McDermott carries no token cost at all. DENYING THE PROBLEM: SAP's CEO says he does NOT expect AI mix to damage the medium-term gross-margin trajectory — because he routes around it.** ⚠️ **Attribution discipline, because the conference-level summary compresses this badly: "several vendors accepting AI gross-margin dilution" resolves on the transcripts to [[SNOW]] EXPLICITLY and [[CRM]] BY CONSTRUCTION. SAP is the OPPOSITE case and must not be folded into the same claim.**

🔴🔴 **SAP PUTS THE FIRST HARD MULTIPLE ON ROUTING AS A MARGIN DEFENCE, AND IT IS ~5x.** (SAP CEO Christian Klein, GS Communacopia 2026 — SAP broadcast, 2026-09-14; SAP has no wiki page and none was created.) **The mechanics as management described them: SAP is intentionally model-agnostic, EVERY AGENT IS TESTED AGAINST AT LEAST SIX OR SEVEN MODELS AND THE MODEL CAN BE CHANGED WITHIN A DAY; for suitable European workloads, using Mistral reportedly CUTS MODEL COST ~5x while preserving sufficient accuracy. Early agent margins were WEAK because SAP prioritised proving customer value; the stated repair path is model routing, open-weight models, scale, and higher pricing once ROI is demonstrated.** ➤ **This is the cleanest vendor-side number this page has on the downtiering trade: not a price cut by a lab, but a BUYER substituting a cheaper model at constant task and claiming a 5x saving. It sits directly against the 08-31 counter-example on this page where a CFO tried downtiering, hit a quality wall and went back to the expensive model — the difference SAP claims is WORKLOAD SELECTION ("suitable" workloads only), which is exactly the segmentation the failure case lacked.** ⚠️ **Unverified vendor claim: no methodology, no workload definition, no sample, and no accuracy metric was given; "~5x" is a cost ratio on an unnamed baseline. Logged as the seller's number, not as a measurement. SAP's context moat is claimed as an ERP ontology of ~7 million relationships or "triples" plus inherited permissions and write-back controls — explicitly NOT the underlying LLM — with Joule Work plus ~400 agents shipping this fall (~150 customers testing, ~80,000 of 100,000-plus employees using it internally), and consumption revenue targeted at 30% of the business by the end of the decade.**

⚠️ **AND THE CONSUMER-INTERNET COUNTERWEIGHT FROM THE SAME WEEK, WHICH THIS PAGE SHOULD CARRY BECAUSE IT DATES THE DEMAND SIDE: consumer internet is treating AI as a CHANNEL rather than a threat, for now — and both incumbents put the interface shift 18-24 MONTHS OUT.** ([[BKNG]] and Airbnb, GS Communacopia 2026 — broadcast and small-group sessions, 2026-09-14; Airbnb has no wiki page and none was created.) **[[BKNG]]: LLM traffic, paid and unpaid, remains SIGNIFICANTLY BELOW 1% of traffic and AI-agent bookings under 1% of bookings, while customer-service cost per booking is down MORE THAN 10% y/y. Airbnb: 50% of customer service handled by AI with headcount NOT reduced, Claude Code delivering 2x code output, AI traffic to the platform still very low — and management's reason for the delay is an INTERFACE argument rather than a capability one: a chatbot is "the wrong interface for 90% of use cases", the largest revenue will come from MULTIMODAL experiences, and things should change over 18-24 months.** ➤ **Two things follow for this page. (1) The token demand that agentic consumer commerce is supposed to generate has a NAMED START DATE from the demand side, and it is not now — which is the same conclusion the 09-08 compute-constraint explanation for slipping agentic ship dates reached from the supply side, arrived at independently. (2) Where tokens ARE being consumed profitably today is INTERNAL productivity, measured in unit costs: cost per booking and cost per merge request, not consumer-facing agents.** ⚠️ **Airbnb's metrics are activity volumes against flat headcount and Booking's are unit costs in the P&L; they are NOT comparable as stated, and neither company disclosed a token bill.**

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-08/09 — **THE INDUSTRY IS SPENDING ~$200bn A YEAR TO GENERATE ~$150bn OF MODEL REVENUE, AND BROADCOM'S CEO SPLITS IT: OPEN WEIGHTS MAKE HALF THE TOKENS AND A FIFTH OF THE MONEY. PLUS MORGAN STANLEY'S $50-60tn TAM WITH A $30bn/GW REVENUE *DERIVED FROM TOKEN THROUGHPUT × PRICE*, META'S MUSE SHIPPING INTO THE $30tn CONSUMER AGENTIC TAM, AND A COMPUTE CONSTRAINT ON AGENTIC SHIP DATES.**

_Sources: **Hock Tan, CEO, [[AVGO]]** — GS Communacopia + Technology Conference, **2026-09-08** ([BBG **INITIAL DRAFT** transcript](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/AVGO_GS_08-09-26.html), **PRIMARY / MANAGEMENT**); **Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak et al** — *"The Morgan Stanley AI Guidebook: Navigating the Age of Inference"*, 99pp, **2026-09-07** ([source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/INTERNET_20260907_2101.html)); **Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak** — *"Muse Has Arrived; What Matters Next?"*, **2026-09-09** ([source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/META_20260909_0116.html); **[[META]] OW, PT $775, Top Pick**); **Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak** on the **2026-09-08** *Broadening* webcast ([source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_09_08_bkng_cvna_wab_morgan_stanley_broadening_webcast_se.html)); **Jean Hu (CFO) + Matt Ramsey, [[AMD]]** — **Citi** 2026 Global TMT, **2026-09-08** ([BBG **LIVE**](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/AMD_CITI_GS_08-09-26.html)); **Thomas Kurian, CEO Google Cloud ([[GOOG]])** — GS Communacopia, **2026-09-08** ([BBG **LIVE**](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/GOOG_GS_08-09-26.html)); read-throughs from **DigitalOcean management** and **Brian Chesky, CEO Airbnb** (both GS Communacopia, **2026-09-08**). ⚠️ **The Bloomberg transcripts are DRAFT/LIVE ASR and will be superseded by FINALs (Airbnb's is already FINAL); garbled words are flagged inline, never repaired.** ⚠️ **NOTHING ON THIS PAGE IS SUPERSEDED — no token price, elasticity, index level, margin ladder, ARR figure, rating or PT already carried is replaced; META stays OW/$775/Top Pick, unchanged.**_

### 🔴🔴🔴 (1) THE RICHEST DATAPOINT IN THIS BATCH — A P&L FOR OPEN WEIGHTS vs CLOSED FRONTIER, FROM THE SUPPLIER WHO SELLS TO BOTH (Hock Tan, management, 2026-09-08)

- **THE SETUP, IN HIS WORDS, WITH HIS OWN SOURCING: *"you can get these sources of data from **OpenRouter, Vercel**. The total amount of tokens consumed and generated, the cost of generating those tokens to be consumed globally. And **this data is obviously for inference**"* — the cost of producing the world's tokens is *"around **$200 billion a year** now. Give or take, rough numbers"*, against model-layer revenue of *"around **$150 billion**."***

| Layer (inference only) | Share of TOKENS generated | Model-layer REVENUE | COST of generating those tokens |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Closed frontier models** | *"half of it is generated through a frontier model, closed systems"* | **~$120bn** (*"75% at least is coming from frontier models"*) | *"they spend roughly $100 billion"* |
| **Open-weight players** | *"the other half, roughly, in fact more than half"* | **~$30bn** | *"the other $100 billion"* |
| **TOTAL** | 100% | **~$150bn** | **~$200bn** |

- 🔴🔴 **LOG THE AGGREGATE PLAINLY, BECAUSE IT IS THE MOST UNCOMFORTABLE SENTENCE ON THIS PAGE: *"This generative AI today, I know it's early stage, things are still growing, but **we're generating $200 billion of token costs to generate $150 billion**."*** ➤ **On the CEO of the largest merchant custom-silicon supplier's own numbers, the model layer in aggregate spends ~$1.33 of infrastructure cost for every $1.00 of inference revenue it earns. This page has measured tokenmaxxing in prices, indices, elasticity and ARR. This is the first time it has a whole-industry cost-versus-revenue frame, and it is negative.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE WEDGE INSIDE IT IS THE ACTUAL CALL: frontier earns **~$120bn on ~$100bn of spend**; open weights earn **~$30bn on ~$100bn of spend** — a ~70% shortfall on identical infrastructure outlay. *"You think that's a sustainable model? We don't know, but the proof's one thing. **The value goes to where intelligence continues to improve**."* His forward view: *"over the next five years, I believe, [the value chain] will reside with the **leading edge frontier models**, as opposed to the open weight models, **even though they may coexist**."***
- ⚠️⚠️ **BASIS DISCIPLINE — FOUR LIMITS, ALL FROM THE TRANSCRIPT ITSELF, AND NONE OF THEM OPTIONAL.** **(a) INFERENCE ONLY.** He says so explicitly and excludes training: *"[Training] is not easily monitored, so it's not just inference"* — ⚠️ **the ASR renders "training" as "Trading"; read as training, flagged not repaired.** **(b) ROUND NUMBERS BY HIS OWN ADMISSION** (*"Give or take, rough numbers"*) — do not treat $120bn/$30bn as measured. **(c) SOURCED TO OpenRouter AND Vercel**, so this page's standing OpenRouter rule applies in full: read OpenRouter as **rank and paid-token share, not implied dollars**. **(d) THE REVENUE LINE IS MODEL-LAYER ONLY** — *"the revenue you could attribute been created and earned by those model guys"* — **NOT** consumer subscription revenue, **NOT** application revenue, **NOT** cloud revenue.
- 🔴 **HOW IT SITS AGAINST THIS PAGE'S OWN SPINE, AND IT RESOLVES A CONFUSION RATHER THAN CREATING ONE: the Jefferies token-share series already on this page (open weights **28% → 62%** of tokens, 09-03) is a **VOLUME** series. Hock's split says a **≥50% VOLUME share for open weights is entirely compatible with a ~20% REVENUE share**. Both can be true simultaneously.** ➤ **STOP READING OPEN-WEIGHT TOKEN SHARE AS A REVENUE-SHARE PROXY ANYWHERE ON THIS PAGE. The two series diverge by roughly 3x and the divergence is the monetisation gap, not a measurement error.**
- ⚠️ **THE BUYER-SIDE COMPLAINT HE REPORTS, WHICH CUTS THE OTHER WAY AND IS LOGGED FOR SYMMETRY: *"I hear all the time, as I go and sell products to enterprises, how they **don't like the fact token cost is getting expensive** when they start using it for very complex application that **maybe open source will give them a cheaper source**."*** ➤ **The supplier's own field evidence is that enterprise buyers are being pushed TOWARD open weights by cost — while his P&L says open weights are where the money is not. That is the whole debate on this page compressed into two adjacent sentences from one speaker.**

### 🔴🔴 (2) MORGAN STANLEY'S AI GUIDEBOOK — THE $50-60tn TAM, AND A $30bn/GW REVENUE FIGURE THAT IS *BUILT OUT OF TOKENS* (2026-09-07)

- 🔴 **THE TAM, AS PRINTED: *"**The $50-$60tn Global Knowledge Work & Consumer TAMs:** We see a **$20-$30tn opportunity in the global knowledge work spend to be digitized through GenAI tools**. We also see **~$30tn of consumer spend** (across retail, travel, autonomous, advertising, etc) to be digitized."*** ⚠️ **A DIGITISABLE-SPEND TAM, not a revenue forecast and not a token-spend forecast. It is the denominator against which every monetisation number on this page is small — treat it as a bound, never as an addressable revenue pool.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THIS IS WHY THE GUIDEBOOK BELONGS ON *THIS* PAGE RATHER THAN ONLY ON THE CAPEX PAGE — THE PER-GW REVENUE IS DERIVED FROM TOKEN ECONOMICS: *"In addition, we **sensitize token throughput and pricing per million tokens** to arrive at **$30bn/GW in base case revenue**."*** ➤ **A token-price × token-throughput model is now the foundation of a per-gigawatt revenue estimate that the whole street is quoting. Every deflation datapoint on this page feeds directly into it, which means this page's argument is now load-bearing for AI-infrastructure ROIC, not just for the model layer.**
- **THE TWO ROIC LADDERS, VERBATIM FROM THE NARRATIVE BULLETS (not from charts):**
  - **OWNED infrastructure — *"model builders that serve APIs on their own infrastructure would be able to monetize capacity at **~$30bn/GW**"*; *"**$8bn/GW of operating costs** (which includes $4.6bn of IT depreciation) leave **~$23bn/GW of incremental EBIT at 75% incremental EBIT margins**"*; *"Post-tax, this equates to **~$18bn/GW of NOPAT or ~46% ROIC**."***
  - **THIRD-PARTY infrastructure — *"model builders that serve APIs on external infrastructure would be able to monetize capacity at **~$41bn/GW**"*; *"**$28bn/GW of rental costs** leaves **~$13bn/GW of incremental EBIT**"*; *"Post-tax, this equates to **~$10bn/GW of NOPAT**"* at **~25% ROIC**.***
  - ➤ **The spread is the point: ~46% ROIC on owned iron vs ~25% on rented, on the same tokens. MS's own framing of debate 3: *"We paint paths to **25-50% GenAI ROIC** across hyperscalers and models with APIs. The **highest returns accrue to the model layer running on top of OWNED infrastructure** ([[META]], [[GOOG]], SpaceX)."***
- ⚠️⚠️ **GW-BASIS LABEL, MANDATORY BEFORE ANYONE QUOTES THESE: these are **MODEL-LAYER API REVENUE per GW**. They are **NOT** a build cost, **NOT** vendor chip content per GW, and **NOT** the labs' own total ARR per GW. The per-GW basis table lives on [ai-datacenter-power.md](ai-datacenter-power.md) — add these two rows there before netting anything.**
- 🔴 **AND MS DISAGREES WITH HOCK ON OPEN WEIGHTS, WITHIN 24 HOURS, WHICH IS WORTH MORE THAN EITHER VIEW ALONE. MS debate 6: *"**Leading cost to serve will matter through the age of inference.** We think **open-weights and lower-cost models are CRITICAL to driving GenAI adoption** across the economy. These will **pressure leading labs to innovate to capture share of inference spend**, while also **reenforcing the value of the hyperscaler concierge layer** (AWS Bedrock, [[MSFT]] Foundry, [[GOOG]] Vertex) that optimizes compute efficiency by task."*** ➤ **Hock says value accrues to closed frontier over five years; MS says open weights are the adoption mechanism and the pressure source. NEITHER ADOPTED. The observable that separates them is the one Hock supplied: **does open-weight REVENUE share close toward its ~50%+ token share, or stay near 20%?** That is now this page's cleanest falsifiable test.**

### 🔴 (4) A DEMAND-SIDE EXPLANATION FOR WHY AGENTIC PRODUCTS KEEP SLIPPING — AND IT IS A COMPUTE CONSTRAINT (MS · Brian Nowak, webcast, 2026-09-08)

- 🔴 **From MS's head of internet research, unprompted, inside a [[BKNG]] pitch: *"we are currently in a **COMPUTE CONSTRAINED ENVIRONMENT, which is holding back the pace at which any of these big mega cap tech companies ship new agentic products**. Any of these agentic products we've written about the last few years, they have not come as fast as we thought. **They've come slower than I thought they would have.** Part of it is because they are compute constrained."*** ➤ **This page keeps logging agentic-product announcements as demand events. Here is the analyst who has been forecasting them saying the ship dates are being set by COMPUTE AVAILABILITY, not by product readiness. Two consequences: (a) agentic token demand is a BACKLOG, not a run-rate — the slippage is supply, so the demand does not evaporate, it queues; (b) it is a competitive opening for domain-specific incumbents, which is exactly the use Nowak puts it to (Booking's own agents and priceline's "Penny").**

That's a **relatively small piece of the market today**, but I think **depending on economics could grow** into a larger piece over time."*** ➤ **A latency-tier market for tokens, sized by the vendor as small today and gated on economics. Pair it with the Astra/Sol/Luna/Terra tiering already on this page: the price card is segmenting by latency, and now the SILICON is too.**
