Do NOT adopt it and do NOT net it against any measured $/M-token series on this page.** ⚠️ Kurian's separate **"80% better price performance for inference"** silicon claim, and the ~30pp gap against SemiAnalysis' measured figure, are logged on [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md) — not restated here.

### ⚠️ (7) TWO READ-THROUGHS FROM COMPANIES WITH NO WIKI PAGE — AND BOTH EARNED THEIR PLACE

- 🔴 **DIGITALOCEAN (CEO + CFO, GS Communacopia, 2026-09-08) — the sharpest operator taxonomy this page has been given, from a provider that sells tokens for a living. ⚠️ No DOCN wiki page exists; plain text, read-through only.** Management splits AI infrastructure into **"token flow"** and **"agent flow"** and dismisses the third thing everyone models: *"**surge pricing or scarcity based GPU pricing is NOT a slipstream. It is a TEMPORARY SPIKE.** I mean, we are playing in that arena as well, but I think **durable slip streams are token flow and agent flow**."* On monetisation, and this is the line that matters here: *"from an economic value capture point of view for a provider like us, **NOT ALL TOKENS MONETIZE THE SAME WAY**. There are the advanced reasoning models like K3 or GLM 5.3 [that] monetize at a completely different rate versus a DeepSeek flash"*, and *"**your tokens on Tuesday morning are of higher quality than the tokens you got on Monday morning**"* because of continuous overnight post-training loops. The CFO puts a mix number on it: **~15% of AI revenue is bare-metal GPU and ~85% is inference services**, against ~70% gross margins in the legacy core cloud, with the upside sitting at **renewal** rather than in the contract: *"we've **abstracted the delivery of the value, which is TOKENS, from the underlying infrastructure**. So the more efficient we can be, **the more tokens we can produce out of the same infrastructure**"* — including off-peak batching and routing non-US traffic into North American off-hours.
  - ➤ **WHY IT MATTERS TO THIS PAGE: it is a seller stating that per-token price is the WRONG unit of measurement for the seller too — quality tier, latency tier and utilisation determine realised revenue per token. That is the supply-side mirror of Sarah Friar's "cost per task, not cost per token" (09-08 block below) and of DOCN's own "not all tokens monetize the same way." Two independent sellers, same week, both moving off the per-token unit.**
- 🔴 **AIRBNB (Brian Chesky, CEO, GS Communacopia, 2026-09-08 — BBG FINAL transcript) — a large enterprise BUYER of tokens contradicting the entire value-accrual argument above. ⚠️ No ABNB wiki page exists; plain text, read-through only.** Chesky: *"right now **most of the value is going to the stack and to enterprise**… **most of the money, I believe, it's going to go to the CONSUMER LAYER, not enterprise, not the GPU stack, not the data centers**. It's going to be the consumer. And **we haven't seen that revolution yet**."* His evidence is the absence of one: *"If you open your phone and you look at all the apps in your home screen and ask **what apps have changed since the launch of ChatGPT, including ours, almost no app has changed**."* And on the buyer's own metric: *"we now ship **80% more features than a year ago**. It's nearly double the output… **I don't care how many tokens we're using. I just care how many features we're developing.**"*
  - ➤ **THREE-WAY DISAGREEMENT NOW ON THE RECORD IN ONE WEEK, AND THIS PAGE SHOULD KEEP ALL THREE: Hock Tan says value accrues to the **closed frontier model layer**; Morgan Stanley says the **model layer on OWNED infrastructure** earns ~46% ROIC and prices a **$30tn consumer TAM** as still contested; Chesky says it goes to the **consumer application layer** and that the consumer revolution **has not started**. ⚠️ Note the incentive of each speaker — a silicon supplier to frontier labs, a sell-side analyst covering consumer internet, and a consumer app CEO. **NONE ADOPTED.** But Chesky's is the useful one for a token page precisely because he is the buyer: **he has stopped counting tokens and started counting shipped features**, which is the outcome-based pricing direction this page has logged from Friar and Brockman, arriving from the demand side.**

## 🆕🔴 2026-09-08 — **THE SELLER'S OWN UNIT: OPENAI'S CFO SAYS JUDGE COST PER TASK, NOT COST PER TOKEN — AND PUTS FIRST-PARTY NUMBERS ON BOTH HALVES OF THE 09-07 BARBELL.**
_(Source: **Sarah Friar, OpenAI CFO — GS Communacopia + Technology Conference fireside, NYC, 2026-09-08**, via [relayed notes](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_09_08_openai_friar_gs_communacopia_notes.html). ⚠️ Relay author unconfirmed; quotes as rendered by the relayer. Full block on [[OPENAI]].)_
- 🔴 **EFFICIENCY, FIRST-PARTY: "by an Artificial Analysis comparison, Astra needs 68% FEWER OUTPUT TOKENS than a rival frontier model to reach comparable results."** ➤ **This is the seller's answer to the 09-07 question "why did the frontier tier reprice UP 2.5x?" — fewer tokens per task at a higher price per token can still be a lower price per TASK. It also means per-token price series (this page's spine) now understate the deflation the buyer actually sees on the frontier tier. Cross-read with the Jefferies 09-07 "Pareto frontier" framing.** ⚠️ The rival model is unnamed in the relay; Artificial Analysis is the comparator, not OpenAI.
- 🔴 **ELASTICITY, FIRST-PARTY: the ~80% Luna price cut "a few weeks ago" produced a 10x LIFT IN USAGE and "the highest share on OpenRouter, above the next Chinese model."** ➤ **10x volume on −80% price ≈ 2x gross token revenue on that tier IF mix held — a stated elasticity well above 1, which is the bull case for the volume half of the barbell.** ⚠️ **Per the wiki's OpenRouter rule: read OpenRouter as rank + paid-token share, not implied dollars — implied-$ is a ceiling and not sign-preserving when cache mix shifts. The Silicon Data token-expenditure index bottoming the week of 30 Aug (09-07 block) is the same shift seen from the spend side: more tokens, cheaper tokens.**
- 🔴 **"INFERENCE IS NOT FREE" — THE OPEN-WEIGHT REBUTTAL ON SERVING COST, NOT QUALITY: open weights "have a place," but customers pay for the holistic stack, and "Luna on Cloudflare is cheaper than GLM 5.3, so a frontier lab can serve inference below an open-source deployment."** ➤ **Direct counter to the 09-03 Jefferies token-share series (open 28% → 62%) and to the GLM-5.3 Flash parity item on [[OPENAI]]: the claim is that a hyperscale serving fleet beats a self-hosted open model on $/token even before quality. Not independently verified; [[NET]] (Cloudflare) is the named host.**
- **PRICING ROADMAP FROM THE CFO: subscription → consumption → outcome; "love to get us away from token counting" toward upside-sharing in verticals (chip design, life sciences).** ✅ Same direction as Brockman's "per-token pricing should stop existing" (09-03/04 block) and The Information's outcome-based pricing report (08-31). **Demand-intensity ladder given alongside: frontier-firm customers use ~8x tokens/user/week vs average (from ~3x), OpenAI internal ~33x** — the diffusion argument in section (5) of the 09-03 block, from the seller.

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-07 — **THE ASTRA PRICE CARD IS A BARBELL — FLAGSHIP +2.5x, VOLUME TIERS −80% / −20% — AND TWO LARGE BUYERS PUBLISHED HARD EFFICIENCY NUMBERS THE SAME WEEK.**

**(1) THE PRICE CARD, PROPERLY DECOMPOSED** (Jefferies · **Thomas Chong / Zoey Zong**, "AI Series #69: AI Productivity; Model Tiering and Pareto Frontier", 2026-09-07)
- 🔴 **"GPT-6 API PRICE IS 2.5x OF GPT-5.6 SOL. Separately, OAI LOWERED PRICES FOR GPT-5.6 LUNA AND TERRA BY ABOUT 80% AND 20% RESPECTIVELY, mainly due to (1) models are MORE EFFICIENT TO RUN and pass on the gains to customers… (2) For Luna / Terra, the models provide a COST-EFFECTIVE WAY TO HANDLE HIGH VOLUME WORK at high levels of quality. For Sol, the model provides customers with RESPONSE TIME [that] becomes more important."**
- ➤➤ **THIS PAGE ALREADY LOGGED THE 2.5x FRONTIER REPRICING ON 09-03/04 AS "THE SHARPEST COUNTER-EXAMPLE TO ITS OWN DEFLATION SPINE." What is new today is the OTHER HALF OF THE CARD, and it restores the spine: the same act that raised the frontier 2.5x CUT LUNA BY ~80%. OpenAI is not choosing between inflation and deflation — it is SEGMENTING, and the two halves of this page's argument are now both true simultaneously and by design. 🔴 THE INVESTABLE FORM OF THE QUESTION CHANGES ACCORDINGLY: not "are token prices rising or falling" but "WHAT SHARE OF WORKLOAD SITS AT EACH RUNG, AND WHICH WAY IS IT MIGRATING." Jefferies supplies the first read on that: the SILICON DATA LLM TOKEN EXPENDITURE INDEX "hits BELOW 1.0 and reaches BOTTOM week of 30 AUG… It reflects APPEAL OF COST-EFFICIENT MODELS", with OpenRouter tokens +1.8% w/w against +21% the prior week. Expenditure falling while volume grows IS the mix-down, measured.**
- ⚠️ **CORRECTION TO THE 09-06 WIKI FRAMING, CARRIED ACROSS PAGES: the 09-06 changelog contrasted [[META]] shipping Muse Spark 1.3 with no per-token cut against "[[OPENAI]] shipping Astra at Anthropic's price", casting OpenAI as the price aggressor. With the full card in view that framing is wrong at the top of the ladder — OpenAI RAISED the frontier — and Meta's unchanged pricing looks less anomalous, especially since a third-party evaluator (Vals AI, relayed by @alexandr_wang 09-06) puts Muse Spark 1.3 Max "COMPETITIVE WITH CLAUDE FABLE 5 AND GPT-5.6 SOL WHILE 4-8x CHEAPER."**

**(2) TWO BUYERS PUBLISH EFFICIENCY NUMBERS, AND THEY ARE THE BEST EVIDENCE THIS PAGE HAS**
- 🔴 **[[MSFT]]: "Microsoft recently said it INCREASED THROUGHPUT FOR COPILOT WORKLOADS BY ROUGHLY 4x SINCE THE BEGINNING OF THE YEAR through optimization across SILICON, SYSTEMS AND SOFTWARE"** (cited by Gaetano Research, 2026-09-07 — *independent Substack author, not a broker*). *The author's frame: efficiency is the AI cycle's DWDM — the innovation that makes installed capacity dramatically more productive and thereby undercuts the incremental-demand case.*
- 🔴 **[[UBER]] CFO Balaji Krishnamurthy, 2Q26 call: "On AI, we are OPTIMIZING TOKEN SPEND by setting better defaults for different use cases, MOVING CERTAIN TASKS TO LOWER-COST OR OPEN-WEIGHT MODELS, and letting employees more clearly understand and manage their spend. As a result, COST PER TOKEN HAS DECLINED over the past several months, EVEN AS ADOPTION HAS CONTINUED TO INCREASE, allowing us to keep OVERALL AI SPEND BROADLY STABLE."** (relayed Bernstein spec sales · Tyler Seidman, 2026-09-07)
- ➤➤ **THE UBER QUOTE IS THE CLEANEST CONFIRMATION OF THE CENTRAL TOKENMAXXING MECHANISM THIS PAGE HAS EVER CARRIED, AND IT COMES FROM A CFO ON THE RECORD: adoption UP, cost per token DOWN, TOTAL SPEND FLAT. ⚠️ It also sits directly against the 08-31 entry on this page — a CFO who "tried downtiering, hit a quality wall and went back to the expensive model", the first observed FAILURE of the mechanism. Both are now on the record from named companies. The difference is likely WORKLOAD MIX (Uber's tasks are routable; the failed case was not), and that is the variable this page should be tracking, not the direction of prices.**

**(3) THE CAPABILITY CLAIM AND ITS SHAPE OF PUSHBACK** — Astra scored **99.9% on ARC-AGI-3 vs Sol at 7.8%** (Meritz Securities via @jukan05, 2026-09-07), was pre-trained on **>100,000 GPUs at the [[ORCL]] Stargate site in Abilene, TX**, and uses **"RECURRENT DEPTH", "a technique that improves cost and performance BUT OBSCURES THE AI'S REASONING, making it harder to monitor"** (The Information, 09-07). ⚠️ **The critics converge on one shape: @martin_casado (a16z) — "there is clearly a meaningful step in COMPUTER USE. But I don't notice a meaningful step in CODING"; @emollick — "jagged AGI… if you mean better than a human expert at most human tasks, WE AREN'T THERE"; @NateSilver538 — lab-published benchmarks are "a PR exercise." Every critic concedes computer use and disputes coding. If that holds, the incremental token demand from Astra is AGENTIC, which is the high-token-per-task regime — bullish for volumes even where it is bearish for the coding narrative.**

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-05 — **A NEW $/MW MONETISATION LADDER LANDS AND IT DOES NOT RECONCILE WITH THE ONE THIS WIKI ALREADY CARRIES — A ~3x GAP ON WHAT LOOKS LIKE THE SAME UNIT. PLUS THE FIRST OPEN-WEIGHT MODEL TO PRICE *ABOVE* THE HOSTED TIER ON THE COST FRONTIER.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE CLAIM: *"Zhipu is monetizing their compute at $8M-10M/MW ($2B ARR & 200MW-250MW of inference compute). [[ANTHROPIC]] & [[OPENAI]] are monetizing at $40M-50M/MW."*** (@zephyr_z9, corpus, 2026-09-05.)
  - ✅ **THE ZHIPU HALF IS SELF-CONSISTENT AND CHECKABLE: $2bn ARR ÷ 200-250MW = $8.0-10.0M/MW. ARR is explicit, so the unit is unambiguously REVENUE PER MW PER YEAR.**
  - ⚠️⚠️ **THE ANTHROPIC/OPENAI HALF DOES NOT RECONCILE WITH THIS WIKI'S EXISTING LADDER, AND THE GAP IS ~3x. The standing mark is 22V's **~$16M/MW/YEAR of contract revenue** (built from three deals, +60% vs last year). $40-50M/MW/year would be roughly THREE TIMES that. No arithmetic is shown in the tweet, and no denominator is stated — it is not said whether the MW figure is contracted, energised, or utilised, nor whether the numerator is ARR, GAAP revenue or bookings.**
  - ⚠️ **AND NOTE THE COINCIDENCE THAT MAKES THIS DANGEROUS: $40-50M/MW is NUMERICALLY IDENTICAL to two RENTAL rates already on the wiki — [[NBIS]]'s short-dated $40-50M/MW and [[GOOG]]'s June rental of 100k [[SPCX]] GPUs at $50/watt = $50M/MW. A model-lab REVENUE-per-MW figure and a neocloud RENTAL rate are different sides of the same transaction and must never be conflated; the fact that they print the same number is a reason for MORE suspicion, not less.**
  - ➤ **RESOLUTION: NOT ADOPTED. The Zhipu figure is logged as a usable Chinese-lab monetisation benchmark; the Anthropic/OpenAI figure is logged as UNRECONCILED against 22V and is not to be used in any $/MW comparison until a denominator is attached. Standing rule reaffirmed: the four bases in circulation — annual contract revenue per MW, lifetime revenue per MW, rental rate per MW, and BUILD COST per MW — do not net, and the label "$/MW" identifies none of them.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE REASON THIS SUDDENLY MATTERS MORE: [[ANTHROPIC]]'s IPO investors are reportedly demanding exactly these two ratios. *"Potential Anthropic IPO investors are pressing the company for details beyond standard financial statements, including REVENUE PER TOKEN and REVENUE PER GIGAWATT OF COMPUTE. What Anthropic discloses could SET A BENCHMARK for [[OPENAI]] and other AI companies."*** (@theinformation, corpus, 2026-09-04.) ➤ 🔴 **If the S-1 carries a revenue-per-GW line, this theme gets its first AUDITED denominator and the entire $/GW ladder on this wiki becomes checkable against a filed number rather than triangulated from deal headlines. That is the single highest-value disclosure to watch for in the next 30 days — see [[ANTHROPIC]] for the IPO timing, which moved twice this week.**
- 🔴 **THE COST FRONTIER MOVED, AND AN OPEN-WEIGHT MODEL CROSSED A LINE IT HAD NOT CROSSED BEFORE: on Signal 65's PINNACLE benchmark, **Alibaba's Qwen3.8-Flash-Next lands THIRD OVERALL at 17 CENTS PER CORRECT TASK on a B300 — *"the first open-weight model ABOVE the hosted tier on the cost frontier, NOT BELOW."*** Also newly benchmarked: **Meta Muse Glimmer 30B** and **[[OPENAI]] GPT-5.6 Sol at max reasoning.** (@PatrickMoorhead, corpus, 2026-09-05.) ➤ **The open-weight cost argument has always been "cheaper, worse." This is the first sighting on this page of an open-weight model beating hosted models on cost-per-CORRECT-task, which is the metric that actually governs enterprise substitution. ⚠️ One vendor-run benchmark, one hardware configuration (B300), no independent replication.**
- 🔭 **THE INTELLIGENCE-INDEX RESHUFFLE, for the record and with its caveat. THAT IS THE CLEANEST CONFIRMATION THIS PAGE HAS OF ITS OWN GROSS-vs-NET ARR TRAP — DELIVERED FROM THE SIDE THAT SAID NO — AND IT COMES WITH THE FIRST OBSERVED ANSWER TO "IS THE OPTIMIZATION CYCLE IN THE NUMBERS YET?" _(Source: **UBS · Karl Keirstead** — post-print client call on the [[SNOW]] Q2 FY27 print, host Cristina Yer (UBS), **2026-09-03**. - 🔴🔴 **(1) THE GROSS-vs-NET TRAP, CONFIRMED BY A REFUSAL — and this page's Barclays block (Aug 28) predicted exactly this failure mode.** Keirstead: *"I asked if the second half guide assumes any **low gross margin token reselling or pass through revenues**. And they said no, that **their appetite to do this is very low**. That's in contrast… with **Data Bricks, that are chasing that revenue opportunity**."* ➤ **The consequence for anyone comparing growth rates in this layer: the two closest comparables are reporting on DIFFERENT REVENUE BASES.** Keirstead has to net it out by hand to compare — *"Data Bricks, **even when you strip out the pass through revs**, grew its core by **mid 60s**"* — against [[SNOW]]'s 37% and [[PLTR]]'s *"90, 95%"*. ⚠️ **A growth-rate table across these three that does not state whether pass-through is in or out is not a comparison.**
- 🔴 **(2) WHERE THE TOKEN COST LANDS WHEN YOU *DON'T* RESELL IT: gross margin, and the number is small.** [[SNOW]] cut FY27 product gross margin **75% → 74%**, which management attributed to *"the rising assumed mix from lower gross margin AI revenues."* Keirstead pressed on the size: CoCo margins are *"much lower"* — the CFO *"would not be explicit"* — climbing later *"as Snowflake finds efficiency gains and as revenues scale."* ➤ **His read is the load-bearing part: *"the massive ramp in AI revenues only weighs on Snowflake gross margins… by 100 bips. I can easily tolerate that."*** For a vendor absorbing token cost rather than passing it through, **the whole AI ramp cost 100bp of product GM.** ✅ **Compare directly with the Aug-27 block on this page** — a $46bn application vendor that did **not** raise margin guidance *because* it was absorbing token cost. Two absorption cases now, and the measured drag at the data layer is one point.
- 🔴🔴 **(3) THE OPTIMIZATION CYCLE — ASKED DIRECTLY OF THE COMPANY BEST PLACED TO SEE IT, AND THE ANSWER IS "NOT YET".** Keirstead: *"there was **no evidence in the Snowflake numbers last night and in the guidance for the second half** that there was a ton of wasteful Snowflake spend, let's say, in the first half that customers started token optimizing and optimizing in general, **such that it weighed on Snowflake's growth**… it's clearly signaling that there there wasn't a ton of wasteful use of Cortex Code. **But might there be next year the beginnings of a little bit more optimization. Probably, yeah.**"* ⚠️ **THIS IS A DIRECT COUNTERWEIGHT TO THE AUG-10 BLOCK on this page** (~15 enterprise IT execs reporting they had already moved *from* token-maxxing *to* token optimization, with spend not falling): at one large consumption vendor with same-quarter visibility, the transition is **not yet visible in revenue**. Both can be true — enterprises optimizing while aggregate spend still grows — but the page should stop treating the phase change as *observed at the vendor level*. ➤ **And the reflexive twist worth logging on its own:** customers are *"signaling that they'd like to use"* Cortex Code **to optimize their own Snowflake spend** — the AI product sold as the instrument of the optimization aimed at its own platform. Keirstead's precedent is explicit: *"everybody involved in Snowflake in 2023, 2024 had to suffer through an optimization trend… that had a discernable effect on the growth rate."*
- ⚠️ **(4) MODEL NEUTRALITY, RE-ARGUED AS A QUALITY *AND* COST ADVANTAGE — a vendor claim, unbenchmarked, and the analyst flags the bias himself.** Asked about customers harnessing *"Claude or any other frontier model directly on data stored in Snowflake"*, Ramaswamy argued those customers *"consistently point to performance degradation… **the SQL query results from using Frontier Lab models directly for data tasks are poor, their inaccuracy is too high, and they can also end up being costly** relative to harnessing Claude or GPT models from within the Snowflake optimized AI harnesses."* Keirstead's own caveat: *"now he's biased, obviously."* ➤ **Read this against the Aug-31 block declaring model neutrality dead as a business model after [[OPENAI]] cut Cursor off.** The distinction that reconciles them: neutrality died for an *application* reselling someone else's frontier access; it is being re-pitched by *platforms sitting on the customer's own data* — Keirstead notes **[[MSFT]], [[PLTR]] and Snowflake are all making the same pitch.** ⚠️ **No independent benchmark exists for the accuracy claim. Do not carry it as fact.**
- 🔴 **(5) THE NEXT LEG, WITH A NAMED AND MONITORABLE PROXY — post-training / fine-tuning open models on corporate data.** Ramaswamy raised it unprompted on the call; pressed, he said it is *"very early, super early"* and Keirstead reads it as *"really more of a next fiscal year thing."* ➤ **The tell Keirstead is watching is specific and this page can watch it too: *"the two companies, both private, that are most tethered to this idea of taking open models and fine tuning them to create a plethora of custom or specialized models are **Fireworks and Base 10**… if they start inflecting, that'll be a signal to me that this phenomenon is taking off."*** He also notes **Karp and Nadella have both been vocally supportive** of the custom-model path. **This gives the open-weight-customization leg of this page its first named leading indicator.**
- ✅ **(6) THE DEMAND-SIDE COUNTERWEIGHT TO THE ROUTING-DEFLATION SPINE, stated as a net judgment rather than a datapoint.** Keirstead: *"if the Snowflake results are any indicator, **the use cases for AI are broadening out at a pace that the sheer demand for models and associated compute might offset much of the pressure from routing to lower ASP models and reducing wasteful token [consumption]**."* He grants the risks are real to frontier-lab growth rates and that UBS has been writing about routing-to-cheaper-models for months — but lands *"more firmly in the long AI trade, rather than in the camp that might be more inclined to chase cheap [names] with minimal AI exposure."* ⚠️ **Judgment, not evidence** — logged because it is the position an analyst who has been running the token-optimization argument on this page since July has now taken.

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-03/04 — **THE FRONTIER TIER REPRICED *UP* 2.5x AND LANDED EXACTLY ON [[ANTHROPIC]]'s NUMBER. THAT IS THE SHARPEST COUNTER-EXAMPLE THIS PAGE HAS TO ITS OWN DEFLATION SPINE — AND THE PRESIDENT OF THE COMPANY DOING IT SAID PER-TOKEN PRICING SHOULD STOP EXISTING.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE PRICE, AND THE PRICE-SETTER: [[OPENAI]]'s **GPT-6 Astra** launched 2026-09-03 at **$10 per million input tokens / $50 per million output tokens — 2.5x GPT-5.6 Sol, and *"the same price as [[ANTHROPIC]]'s recently-released FABLE 5.1, showing how ANTHROPIC IS STILL SETTING PRICES FOR THE REST OF THE INDUSTRY."*** (The Information · Stephanie Palazzolo, 2026-09-04; corroborated by The Deep View via MS desk.) ➤ 🔴 **TWO THINGS THIS PAGE HAS TO HOLD AT ONCE, AND THEY POINT OPPOSITE WAYS.** ① **The FRONTIER TIER just repriced UP by 150% and the two leading labs are at an identical list price** — that is not commoditisation, it is a duopoly clearing at the same number. ② **This page's own 08-22 entry has OpenAI CUTTING GPT-5 API and credit pricing.** ➤ **The correct reading is that the price ladder is STRETCHING, not falling: the trailing tier deflates toward open-weight while the frontier tier inflates. A blended "$/M token" series that mixes the two now measures mix, not price.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE EFFICIENCY LEG MOVED IN THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION IN THE SAME ANNOUNCEMENT, WHICH IS WHY THE PRICE RISE IS NOT WHAT IT LOOKS LIKE: Artificial Analysis measures Astra's *"biggest advance"* as **coding-agent efficiency — a score of 67, roughly matching Opus 5, Fable 5 and Muse Spark 1.3 — while MATERIALLY REDUCING TOKEN USAGE BY ONE-THIRD AND COST PER CODING TASK BY MORE THAN 50%.*** (via MS desk · Tom Wigg, 2026-09-04.) ➤ ✅ **THIS IS THE THEME IN ITS PUREST OBSERVED FORM: PRICE PER TOKEN +150%, TOKENS PER TASK −33%, COST PER TASK −50%+. All three at once, from one release. Anyone tracking this theme on $/token alone would have recorded a 2.5x inflation on a product that got half as expensive to use.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE COMPANY SAID THE MEASUREMENT ITSELF IS WRONG, WHICH IS THE MOST STRUCTURALLY IMPORTANT QUOTE THIS PAGE HAS RECEIVED ALL QUARTER: *"**Pricing tokens doesn't make any sense.** Our tokens are **not the same as our competitors' tokens**, they're **not the same between different model families**… What you actually want is… **THE PRICE PER TASK IS WHAT MATTERS**"* (Greg Brockman, OpenAI President, press briefing, via The Information, 2026-09-04). **Consistent with the earlier report that OpenAI *"had begun giving some customers the option to PAY FOR AI ONLY WHEN IT WORKS."*** ➤ ⚠️⚠️ **IF PER-TASK PRICING ARRIVES, EVERY $/M-TOKEN CONSTRUCTION ON THIS WIKI LOSES ITS DENOMINATOR — including 22V's [[ANTHROPIC]] revenue build ($1.75/M tokens today → $1.00/M in the out-years), the Zhipu/Z.ai +101% API pricing counter-example, and the open-vs-closed token-share series. **Flagged now, before it happens, so the page can date when the metric broke rather than discovering it retrospectively.** Also note the incentive: the lab arguing tokens are the wrong unit is the one whose token price just rose 150% while its tokens-per-task fell a third.**
- ✅ **THE COST-CURVE CLAIM FROM THE SILICON SIDE, WHICH IS THE SUPPLY-SIDE OF THE SAME MECHANISM: *"Broadcom, [[NVDA]] and AMD are driving down **COST PER TOKEN AT 60-100%+ PER YEAR**, further enhancing the profitability of frontier model builders"* (JPM · Harlan Sur, [[AVGO]] post-print call, Capstone-attended recording, 2026-09-04). ⚠️ **A supplier's sell-side asserting the rate at which its own customers' costs fall — directionally consistent with everything else on this page, but not independently measured.**
- 🔴 **AND THE UNIT ECONOMICS THAT NUMBER IS BEING USED TO JUSTIFY, WITH A ~30% DISAGREEMENT INSIDE IT: Broadcom management, via JPM, puts [[ANTHROPIC]] at **$65bn ARR on only 2GW with $10bn of COGS** — a ~6x gross spread, plus *"reports Anthropic was EBITDA profitable in the June quarter."* **22V Research builds the same 2GW bottom-up and gets $45bn** (2GW × 60% utilisation × 50% sold × $1.75/M tokens), scaling to *"north of $200B per year"* at 7GW and $1.00/M tokens. ➤ ⚠️ **A ~$20bn gap on an identical capacity base. The higher number comes from the vendor selling the chips and underwriting the financing; the lower comes from an outside analyst who publishes his assumptions. Neither is adopted; both are dated on [[ANTHROPIC]].**
- ⚠️ **THE QUALITY INDEX DID NOT MOVE, WHICH IS THE PART THE PRICE INCREASE HAS TO BE JUDGED AGAINST: on **Artificial Analysis' INTELLIGENCE index Astra scored 61 — the SAME as GPT-5.6 Sol and Grok 4.6, versus Fable 5.1 at 66**; Agentic Index **51 vs Grok's 59** — while simultaneously **saturating ARC-AGI-3 (62.7% standard harness, 99.9% with a new provider adapter, vs Opus 5 at 30.2% and Sol at 7.8%)** (@Hesamation and ARC via MS desk, corpus, 2026-09-03/04). ⚠️⚠️ **THREE DIFFERENT METRICS, THREE DIFFERENT ORDERINGS. Do not collapse them. A corpus practitioner calls it *"the biggest discrepancy I've seen in a model's evals."* The honest summary: large efficiency and reasoning-harness gains, no measured gain on composite quality, and a 150% price increase.**

  *(Jefferies, sourced to Company Data / Bloomberg / Financial Times. **Dec-26E is Jefferies' estimate, flagged with an asterisk on the slide.**)* ➤ **Three consecutive 10x years on the actuals (0.1→1→10), and the Dec-26E bar is the fourth. The Feb-26 → Jul-26 leg alone — $14bn to $65bn in five months — is a 4.6x inside one half-year.**
- **REPORTED quarterly revenue, p37 — the same story on a GAAP-shaped basis rather than ARR: **$787m in 2Q25 → $11,500m in 2Q26, >14x y/y.*** ⚠️ **Note the basis difference and do not mix the two series: p26 is ARR (an annualised run-rate), p37 is reported quarterly revenue. $11.5bn in a quarter against $65bn of Jul-26 ARR is internally coherent; against $47bn of May-26 ARR it is not, so pick a date, not a pair.**
- **p6, the framing chart: *"Years to Estimated $200B Annual Revenue"* — **Anthropic 7 years vs AWS 25 years.*** ➤ ⚠️ **Both endpoints are ESTIMATES on Jefferies' construction ("Estimated $200B"); Anthropic has not reached $200bn. Read it as a slope comparison, not as an achieved fact.**
- ✅ **AND THE GROSS-PROFIT LEG, FROM A COMPLETELY SEPARATE SOURCE THE SAME WEEK (JPM · Harlan Sur, "AVGO post-FQ3 group callback", 2026-09-03, relaying Hock Tan): [[ANTHROPIC]] is driving **~$30bn of ARR per gigawatt** of deployed capacity against a **fully-loaded cost of ~$5bn per year** on that gigawatt.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_09_03_jpm_avgo_call.html) ➤ **JPM's read: *"very, very strong gross profit per token,"* with press reports that Anthropic was already operating-profit positive in the June quarter (press-reported, not disclosed).** ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT GUARD, MANDATORY: ~$30bn/GW is REVENUE PER GIGAWATT PER YEAR and ~$5bn/GW is COST PER GIGAWATT PER YEAR — two annual P&L flows on the same denominator, which is why they may legitimately be compared to each other. NEITHER is capex, build cost, or vendor content per GW, and neither may be netted against the $/GW bases on [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md) or the $16M/MW/yr contract-revenue anchor on [ai-datacenter-power.md](ai-datacenter-power.md).**

THAT IS THE LARGEST COUNTER-EXAMPLE THIS PAGE CARRIES TO ITS OWN TOKEN-DEFLATION SPINE — AND IT IS CHINESE OPEN-WEIGHT.**)

**Sources: (1) MS · Gary Yu / Lydia Lin / Yang Liu, "Z.AI CO., LTD. — 1H26: ARR Guidance Raised", 2026-08-31 (10:59 PM GMT, Morgan Stanley Asia)** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ZAICO_20260831_2259.html) · **(2) FD (Robonomics), "China Open-Source LLM Tracker", 2026-09-01** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/China_Open-Source_LLM_Tracker_-_by_FD_-_Robonomics.html) — ⚠️ **source (2) is an INDEPENDENT SUBSTACK NEWSLETTER, NOT A BROKER: no rating, no price target, no model. Logged at that evidentiary level throughout.** ⚠️ **The subject — Z.AI CO., LTD. / Zhipu AI (2513.HK) — is OFF-COVERAGE and has NO wiki page; nor do MiniMax, DeepSeek, Moonshot/Kimi or ByteDance. Plain text only. The listing-level marks are carried on [outros-asia.md](outros-asia.md).**

### 🔴🔴 (1) THE CORROBORATION IS THE POINT — SIX FIGURES, TWO UNRELATED SOURCES, ONE WEEK

Almost every China open-weight datapoint on this page arrives once, from one relay, and cannot be checked. **These two can.** Morgan Stanley's numbers come from company management around the 1H26 results; FD's come from his own tracker of public disclosure — and they agree on: **monthly-based ARR ~US$1.6bn and weekly-based ARR ~US$2.0bn in August**; **token usage +40x YTD**; **average API pricing +101%**; **revenue per unit of compute +14x**; **unit inference cost −80%**; and a **year-end ARR of ~US$2.4-2.5bn**. ➤ **Treat the Zhipu series as VERIFIED rather than relayed — it is now the best-evidenced open-weight monetisation series in this wiki, and the only one with two independent readings of the same six numbers.** ⚠️ *Not identical in kind: MS's US$2.4bn is COMPANY GUIDANCE that MS adopted as its own estimate; FD's ~US$2.5bn is his own trend extrapolation. Do not merge them into one "consensus".*

### 🔴🔴 (2) 40x VOLUME **WITH** PRICE UP 101% — THIS IS NOT A PRICE WAR, AND IT BREAKS THE PAGE'S DEFAULT MECHANISM

*"From January–August, token consumption grew 40x+, including 23x+ growth in coding plan tokens. API average pricing rose 101%"* (MS · Gary Yu, "Z.AI CO., LTD. — 1H26: ARR Guidance Raised", 2026-08-31). FD reaches the same place independently and generalises it: *"this growth did not come from a price war. **DeepSeek raised prices by roughly 3-12x, while usage continued to grow.** Zhipu tells a similar story — token usage surged even as average API pricing increased"* (FD (Robonomics), "China Open-Source LLM Tracker", 2026-09-01). ➤ **This page's spine — SIG's measured closed-vs-open deflation, the 08-24 "volume up, price per token down, share rotating to open weights" synthesis, the ~unit-elastic GPT-5.6 Luna event study — carries an unstated assumption that open-weight share is won on PRICE. In the two largest Chinese cases the price went UP, by ~2x and by 3-12x respectively, and volume compounded anyway.** The coherent read is that these names were priced far below their own marginal cost at the start of the year and are now walking price back INTO a demand curve still shifting right faster than the move up the curve. ⚠️ **It also means "open weights" and "cheap tokens" are not the same trade, and this page should stop treating them as one.**

### 🔴🔴 (3) THE UNIT ECONOMICS, WITH THE LEVEL AND NOT JUST THE GROWTH RATE — API REVENUE STILL DOES **NOT** COVER COMPUTE COST

**Computing multiplier (API revenue ÷ computing cost) rose 14x y/y in 2Q26 — to 0.46x** (MS · Gary Yu, 2026-08-31). ➤ **0.46x is the number that matters: after a 14x improvement, a dollar of compute still returns forty-six cents of API revenue. The business is not yet paying for its own inference, let alone its training.** **Unit inference cost fell 80% in 1H26** (both sources), and FD adds the margin the MS note does not print: **API gross margin 24.6%**, plus *"revenue per unit of compute increased 14x"* — the same coefficient, arrived at separately. ⚠️ **Quote the LEVEL (0.46x, 24.6% GM) alongside the growth (14x) every time: a 14x improvement to 0.46x is a statement about how far below water the starting point was, and it reads as a bull point if the level is dropped.** ➤ Reconcile against the 08-28 Barclays gross-vs-net primer already on this page: this pairs a **gross platform revenue** line against a **direct compute cost** line, the most favourable pairing available — the fully-loaded number is worse than 0.46x, not better.

### 🔴 (4) THE ARR LADDER, AND THE ONE MONTH THAT CARRIES IT

FD's monthly ladder, which the MS note does not publish: **MaaS/API ARR ~US$250m (March) → ~US$1bn (July) → US$1.6bn monthly run-rate / US$2.0bn weekly run-rate (August)** — *"ARR has grown 6x+ since March"* — plus the line that shows how violent August was: ***"August revenue alone was higher than Zhipu's entire 1H26 API revenue."*** **Year-end ARR guidance was raised to US$2.4bn against MS's prior US$2.0bn estimate, and MS raised its own year-end estimate to US$2.4bn, in line with company guidance, flagging potential upside from better-than-expected computing supply** (MS · Gary Yu, 2026-08-31) — i.e. **the binding constraint on the ARR line is stated to be COMPUTE SUPPLY, not demand.** ⚠️⚠️ **A WEEKLY-ANNUALISED AND A MONTHLY-ANNUALISED ARR ARE NOT THE SAME NUMBER AND BOTH ARE QUOTED HERE — US$2.0bn is a last-week-of-August run-rate, US$1.6bn is the August month. This is exactly the annualisation-convention trap this page logged against the Anthropic ARR series on 08-18. When the underlying compounds monthly the weekly basis always prints larger; never compare a weekly-run-rate ARR against another company's monthly one.**

### 🔴 (5) MORGAN STANLEY'S MODEL — REVENUE UP 20-29%, FY26-27 EPS **CUT**, FY28 EPS **RAISED**, FIRST PROFIT IN FY28

Rating **Overweight**, industry view **In-Line**; **price target RAISED HK$1,700 → HK$1,800 (+5.9%)** against a **HK$1,195 close (2026-08-31)** = **~51% upside**. Market cap **US$70,977mn (~US$71.0bn)**, EV US$70,676mn, **466m diluted shares**, 52-week range **HK$2,980.00–116.10**, average daily trading value US$472mn. **ModelWare revenue: Rmb 724.3m (12/25) → 6,328.9m (12/26e) → 19,312.8m (12/27e) → 47,641.2m (12/28e)**; **EPS (Rmb) (19.95) / (8.59) / (4.21) / 12.02** vs **prior (7.50) / (5.84) / 5.40**; **EBITDA (Rmb mn) (3,509.9) / (4,162.6) / (2,462.5) / 5,170.2**. ➤ 🔴 **NOTE THE SHAPE, BECAUSE IT IS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT AN "ARR GUIDANCE RAISED" HEADLINE IMPLIES: MS RAISED REVENUE 20.4% / 25.6% / 29.3% FOR 26/27/28 AND STILL *WIDENED* THE FY26 LOSS PER SHARE 14.4% WHILE *RAISING* FY28 EPS 122.6%. The bridge is a lower gross-margin forecast plus higher R&D — the raise is being spent, and effectively the whole upgrade lands in the terminal year.** First modelled profitable year is **FY28**; operating loss widened 4.5% for 2026, narrowed 35.2% for 2027, operating profit raised 489% for 2028. ⚠️ **BROKER-NOTE INTERNAL INCONSISTENCY, LOGGED NOT RESOLVED: the prose says revenue forecasts raised "20.4%, 25.8%, and 29.3%" while Exhibit 2 of the same note says 20.4% / 25.6% / 29.3%. Use the exhibit (25.6%), per this wiki's standing rule that the tables outrank the summary page.**

**The 1H26 print underneath it is a MARGIN miss, not a revenue miss:** total revenue **Rmb954m (+400% y/y)** vs MSE Rmb967m (**−1%**), of which **cloud-based deployment Rmb825m (+18% vs MSE)** and **on-premise Rmb129m (−52% vs MSE, −20% y/y)** — the mix shifted to cloud harder than modelled. But **gross profit Rmb252m vs MSE Rmb308m (−18%)** on a **GP margin of ~26% against 32% modelled and 50.0% in 1H25**. **Net loss attributable Rmb2,071m** (−12% y/y, −20% vs MSE). Full-year GPM path: **41.0% (2025) → 31.3% (26E) → 35.3% (27E) → 40.1% (28E)**. ➤ **Gross margin roughly HALVED year-on-year while revenue 5x'd. That is the same fact as the 0.46x computing multiplier seen from the P&L side, and it is the line to watch rather than ARR.**

**Valuation:** DCF at **15% WACC / 3% terminal growth** (both unchanged); the PT implies **35x 2027e P/S** against a historical one-year-forward P/S of **176x**, with the stock at **25x 2027e P/S** — MS *"view it as undervalued."* **Bull HK$3,340 (40x 2027e P/S, Rmb33.8bn 2027 revenue) · Base HK$1,800 (35x, Rmb19.3bn) · Bear HK$670 (20x, Rmb13.5bn).** Consensus (Refinitiv): **mean PT HK$1,595.83**, range HK$905.76–2,298.95; rating distribution **81% Overweight / 19% Equal-weight / 0% Underweight**. ⚠️ *The note's MS-vs-consensus estimate exhibit is column-interleaved in the extract and its consensus revenue/EPS values cannot be assigned unambiguously — deliberately NOT quoted.*

### 🔴 (6) "CODING IS A META CAPABILITY, NOT A VERTICAL" — AND THE TAMs THAT FOLLOW FROM IT

Management's framing, which is a direct claim about where token demand goes next: coding *"should be viewed less as a commercial vertical and more as a prerequisite capability, evolving into a **'meta capability'** underpinning professional coworker agents across **cybersecurity, legal, and financial** domains"*, with GLM-5.3 capabilities *"already transferring into cybersecurity use cases"* and coding as *"the stepping stone toward **'cowork' agents** that can replicate complex professional workflows and act as digital co-workers."* **Z.ai sizes coding TAM at US$500bn and cowork TAM at US$5tn** (MS · Gary Yu, 2026-08-31). **The first hard proof point, from FD: 2,436 validated vulnerabilities discovered** — a countable output rather than a benchmark score. And on why the capability transferred at all: ***"GLM-5.2 and GLM-5.3 use the same base model, but heavier post-training improved end-to-end task completion by >50%"*** (FD (Robonomics), 2026-09-01) — **post-training, not scale, on an unchanged base.** On the roadmap, MS reports management describing **GLM-6.0 as a "fully self-training" model capable of self-evaluation and self-correction**, viewed internally as a milestone toward **RSI (recursive self-improvement)**, with meaningful model-competitiveness progress possibly emerging *"within the next one to two months"* — FD independently names the same next bet as **self-training / RSI**. ➤ **Read against this page's agentic-workload thread: if coding capability generalises into cybersecurity/legal/financial coworkers, the token demand curve does not saturate when the coding-assistant market does. That is a bull mechanism for tokenmaxxing that does not depend on price at all.** ⚠️ *Company self-assessment and company-sized TAMs. Logged as management's framing, not adopted.*

### 🔴 (7) THE INFRASTRUCTURE / DOMESTIC-SILICON LEG — THE HARDEST NUMBER IN EITHER SOURCE

MS: management reiterated an ***"all-in infrastructure"*** strategy over the past six months — acquiring infrastructure capability plus optimisation *"across model, network, and semi layers"* — citing **scale benefits from multi-source computing procurement, a domestic-GPU ramp-up and potential self-built data centres**, and product-level gains in **cache hit rates, token throughput and serving cost**, with management **seeing upside in full-year Open Platform GPM and targeting 50%+ within 12-18 months** (MS · Gary Yu, 2026-08-31). FD supplies the physical scale: ***"Zhipu is operating 100K domestic accelerators, and GLM-5.3 Flash handled 60-62T tokens in six days on domestic chips"***, with serving performance on the same hardware improved **~3x through infrastructure optimisation** (FD (Robonomics), 2026-09-01). ➤ 🔴 **60-62 TRILLION TOKENS IN SIX DAYS ON DOMESTIC SILICON, ON A STATED FLEET OF 100K DOMESTIC ACCELERATORS, IS THE MOST CONCRETE MEASUREMENT OF NON-NVIDIA INFERENCE THROUGHPUT THIS WIKI HOLDS.** Cross-read the 08-27 block on this page, where the supply-side proof arrived *"priced at ~3% of Claude Opus and run on Chinese silicon"* — same phenomenon, now with a token count and an accelerator count attached. ⚠️ **This is also the China-export read-through and it belongs on [china-export.md](china-export.md); that page was NOT edited in this run and the linkage is flagged for routing.**

**The platform/engagement disclosures behind the ARR, same note:** **Open Platform user base 7.4mn+**; **MaaS platform users +144% YTD**; **paying daily active users +603% YTD**; among the **top 10 customers by revenue contribution, average daily token usage +~98x (9,798%) YTD**; the company became *"the most prioritized supplier globally for four of the top 10 Internet companies in China"*; **average daily revenue +234x over 12 months**; **ZCode monthly active users exceeded 1mn in its first month**; and sales restrictions were removed on API + coding plan. Premium-customer counts as of end-August: **customers contributing ARR above US$100k / US$500k / US$1mn / US$10mn / US$25mn / US$250mn at approximately 115 / 25 / 37 / 8 / 2 / 2**, up by roughly **94 / 20 / 35 / 7 / 2 / 2** since the start of the year. ⚠️ **THAT LADDER IS NON-MONOTONIC AS EXTRACTED (25 customers above US$500k vs 37 above US$1mn is arithmetically impossible) — most likely a column-order artefact in the PDF extraction. Reported as printed, NOT reconciled, and the individual tier counts should not be used until the exhibit is re-read.**

### (8) THE SECTOR NUMBERS AROUND IT (FD only — independent newsletter; estimates unless marked disclosed)

- **China open-weight sector ARR US$10-15bn in total, of which core LLM revenue excluding ByteDance/Seedance is ~US$6-8bn**; **overseas revenue already ~US$2bn+**, with the estimated overseas mix **MiniMax 60.8% (disclosed) · Kimi >60% (est.) · Zhipu ~40% (est.) · Tencent ~5-10% (est.) · Alibaba ~5-10% (est.)**. Growth *"has been accelerating in recent months, particularly since June."*
- **MiniMax: ~US$100m ARR (Dec-25) → >US$150m (Feb-26) → ~US$300-430m (Apr/May-26) → >US$800m on a weekly run-rate (Aug-26)**, against management's prior YE26 target of **≥US$1.0bn**. **B2B is now ~80% of ARR** (from ~30% a year ago) with **B2B revenue +703% y/y**; B2C ~20%. **July token consumption 20x January**; **enterprise customers + developers >2m, roughly 10x YE2025**. Product: **MiniMax's H3, post-trained and accelerated on fal.ai, generates a 5-second video clip in ~3 seconds**; separately **Bilibili disclosed its AI video revenue has doubled**.
- **DeepSeek: Jan-July revenue ~US$70m (per The Information); since July pricing up ~3-12x depending on product with usage appearing to hold up; FD's best estimate of latest ARR ~US$1.2bn.**

- **ByteDance: ~US$4bn annualised AI revenue run-rate (company disclosed)**, of which **Seedance ~US$2bn (est.)**; **Doubao model calls 63T tokens/day (Dec-25) → 180T/day (Jun-26), ~2.9x in six months**, with Doubao *model* ARR estimated at only **~US$100m**. ➤ **180 trillion tokens a day against ~US$100m of model ARR is the cleanest available illustration of this page's "a token is a token" problem: enormous volume, almost no captured model-layer revenue.**
- **Alibaba: Bailian model & application-services ARR >RMB16bn / >US$2.35bn as of August, from >RMB8bn / ~US$1.18bn in May (more than doubled in ~three months), against a year-end target of >RMB30bn / ~US$4.4bn.** Alibaba states its **own proprietary models are the majority**, with third-party models also contributing meaningfully. Separately, **AI-related cloud products annualised revenue ~US$7.3bn** — a wider perimeter including **AI compute + MaaS + AI applications**. ⚠️ **Do not compare that US$7.3bn to any lab's API ARR: it is infrastructure-inclusive, and that is the same gross-vs-net trap the 08-28 Barclays block on this page documents.**
- **Kling: ~US$100m (Mar-25) → ~US$240m (Dec-25) → ~US$500m (Mar-26).**

- 🔴🔴 **THE MIX MOVED UP THE LADDER IN ONE MONTH, AND THIS IS THE FINDING THAT MATTERS TO THIS PAGE.** Between **Nov-25 and Dec-25 Opus went from 15.2% to 52.1% of Anthropic's own mix** — 10.2% ➜ 34.1% of all named-model usage — and it never went back: **Opus+Fable are 77.6% of Anthropic's mix in Jul-26** (74.9% in Jun-26). Sonnet, which alone held **68.8%** of the whole panel in Jan-25 and 54.0% as late as Nov-25, is **13.2%**. Haiku is 1.0%. ➤ **The direction is the opposite of tokenmaxxing's core prediction.** This page is built on work being routed *down* the model stack — the Aug-27 CRM block ("a deliberate strategy of routing work down the model stack"), the Aug-12 case study cutting spend ~62% with volume flat, the Aug-10 "token optimization" phase change. **In Ramp's panel, enterprise mix went the other way and stayed there for eight months.** ⚠️ Not a refutation: down-tiering and flagship-adoption can run in the same market on different workloads, and the Aug-31 block already logs *a CFO who tried down-tiering, hit a quality wall and went back to the expensive model* — **this series is the aggregate version of that CFO.**
- 🔴🔴 **CONSEQUENCE, AND IT QUALIFIES A NUMBER THIS PAGE ALREADY CARRIES — NOTHING IS OVERWRITTEN.** The spend-per-employee series (Aug-20 block below) changes regime in **exactly the month of the ladder flip**: median compound **+3.96%/mo over the twelve months to Dec-25 ➜ +12.44%/mo over the seven months since** ($5.26 ➜ $11.95, +127%). **The same panel therefore shows a price/mix shift and a spend acceleration co-timed to the month.** Flagship tiers list at a multiple of mid-tier per token, so **an unknown part of that +12.44%/mo is price and mix, not more AI being used.** ➤ **The file cannot separate them** — Ramp gives spend and model identity, never tokens. ⚠️ **Neither the volume read nor the price read is provable from this data.** What IS provable: anyone quoting the spend acceleration as pure diffusion owes a mix adjustment. Co-timing is not causation either way; both legs could follow a third driver (agentic coding adoption is the obvious candidate). **The Aug-20 block's numbers stand as written; this is a basis qualification on how they are read.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **THE PANEL CANNOT SEE OPEN WEIGHTS AT ALL — WHICH IS A STRUCTURAL LIMIT ON USING IT ON THIS PAGE.** Across 19 months and 49 models the providers are **Anthropic, OpenAI, xAI and Cursor. There is no Llama, no DeepSeek, no Qwen, no Mistral, no Gemini and no Gemma row in any month.** ➤ **This is irreconcilable with this page's own measurements unless the panel simply cannot observe self-hosted or cloud-routed open weights**: the Aug-24 block has **open-source token share at Vercel going 28% ➜ 62% in two months**, and the same day's block has **Thomson Reuters and AT&T named as substituting to open-weight models specifically to cut [[ANTHROPIC]] bills.** A corporate-card / bill-pay panel sees a *named vendor invoice*; an open-weight model served on your own GPUs or inside a cloud bill produces no named-model line item. **So Ramp measures the closed-frontier contest only, and the denominator excludes precisely the migration this theme tracks. Anthropic's 63.4% is a share of the closed-frontier set — never quote it as LLM market share.** ⚠️ It also means the series **cannot** be used as evidence for or against the open-weight thesis in either direction.
- 🔴 **ENTERPRISE AI HAS NO INSTALLED BASE — THE CLEANEST NEW FACT IN THE FILE.** Models first seen within three months hold **51.6-83.2% of all usage in every month of 2026** (Jul-26: 69.9%). Models older than twelve months hold **4.2%**. Nothing sediments: each release re-races the whole panel, and **34 models are live in Jul-26 against 7 in Jan-25** while HHI falls **52.1 ➜ 12.9**. ➤ Direct read-through to this page's pricing work: **there is no switching-cost cushion under any frontier price.** A vendor that loses the quality lead does not decay slowly — Opus 4.6 went **44.8% at debut ➜ 53.9% peak ➜ 6.9% within five months.** ⚠️ **Age is measured from first appearance in the panel (Jan-25), not true release, so the >12-month band is left-censored and only honest from Feb-26.**
- 🔴 **THE LAUNCH CURVES PUT A SHAPE ON THE PRICE WAR.** Nineteen observed launches (peak ≥4%): every one peaks **within 0-3 months of debut, median 1 month**, then decays. Largest debut in the file: **Opus 4.6 at 44.8%** (Feb-26). **OpenAI's largest is GPT-5.6 Sol at 14.9% in Jul-26** — marginally ahead of GPT-4.1's 14.7% (Apr-25) — and it is the whole of OpenAI's Jul-26 recovery to **36.0%, its best month since Sep-25**, against Anthropic's **−8.0pp** to 63.4%. ⚠️⚠️ **TIMING TRAP, AND IT IS A REAL TEST FOR THE NEXT DROP: Jul-26 PRE-DATES the 33% GPT-5.6 Sol *output* price cut reported in the Aug-24 block below.** Sol took 14.9% at full list. **If Ramp's share is spend-weighted, a 33% output cut MECHANICALLY LOWERS Sol's August share at constant usage; if it is usage-weighted it should raise it.** ➤ **The August print is therefore a free identification test of the weighting basis Ramp does not disclose** — and until it resolves, one month of OpenAI share gain is not a trend.
- **PRACTICAL RULE, alongside the one already set for the spend series:** quote model shares **off `model_share.json`**, always as *"share of the named closed-frontier models Ramp covers"* and never as market share; **do not chain this series to any token-volume, ARR or revenue figure** — the weighting basis is unknown and the vendor set is incomplete; and **do not read a single month's provider move as share shift** when 70% of the panel sits on models under three months old and one launch moves 8pp.

## 🆕🔴🔴 Recent signals (Aug 31, /run-inbox — **A CFO TRIED DOWNTIERING, HIT A QUALITY WALL AND WENT BACK TO THE EXPENSIVE MODEL — THE FIRST OBSERVED *FAILURE* OF THE CENTRAL TOKENMAXXING MECHANISM. PLUS THE REV-PER-GW SPREAD BLOWS OUT TO $10bn vs $30-50bn, AND MODEL NEUTRALITY DIES AS A BUSINESS MODEL.**)

**Sources: (1) UBS · Carl Keirstead / Taylor McGinnis / Roddy Sultan / Roger Boye — "State of AI and Software" update call, host Rob Ruble, 2026-08-31** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_31_ubs_the_state_of_ai_software.html) · **(2) Wells Fargo Software Team · Michael Turrin — "Rise of Open-Weight & Open-Source Model Impacts on Software", 2026-08-12** ⚠️ *filename says `20260819_…`; the note itself is dated 12-Aug-2026 — date corrected off the document* [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260819_Wells_Fargo_WDAY_Rise_of_Open_Weight_-_Open_Source_Model_Impacts_on_Softw.html) · **(3) BofA · Justin Post / Nafeesa Gupta — "Hotel booking capabilities launch in AI Mode", 2026-08-31** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/research1mlcom_20260831_BofA_AI_Mode_hotel_booking.html)

### 1. 🔴 The downtiering trade FAILED at one named account — and the CFO substituted volume control instead

**This page has treated model routing / downtiering as the primary token-cost lever and as the mechanism bending frontier-lab revenue. Here is the first documented case of it being tried and abandoned, from a Cursor-hosted dinner UBS ran with ~a dozen Bay Area CFOs:**

> *"The CFO of **J Frog**… tried **ROUTING TO CHEAPER [[ANTHROPIC]] SONNET MODELS TO CUT COSTS**, but for him, **THE PERFORMANCE DEGRADATION, DOWNTIERING FROM OPUS TO SONNET CLASS MODELS, WAS SO MATERIAL** [that] the employees pushed back that he ended up **ALLOWING THEM TO SWITCH BACK TO CLAUDE OPUS MODELS**, and he turned instead to **USAGE CAPS** to throttle excessive token consumption."*

⚠️ **Why this is more than an anecdote: the ASP-compression thesis and the volume-compression thesis have very different consequences for the labs, and they have been conflated on this page. If quality gates force enterprises back up the model stack and they cut TOKENS instead of PRICE POINT, the labs keep ASP and lose units — the opposite mix outcome to the one the market is modelling. One observation is not a trend, but it is the first observation of the mechanism running in reverse.**

⚠️ **AND THE OPEN-WEIGHT SUBSTITUTION IS NOT WHAT CFOs ARE ACTUALLY DOING, per the same dinner: *"we sensed some **HESITATION TO USE OPEN MODELS, WHETHER CHINESE OR EVEN US**, and instead CFOs were pushing for greater use of **CHEAPER CLOSED MODELS** where possible, especially for non-frontier tasks."* This sits in direct tension with the Wells Fargo open-weight thesis below and with the Vercel 28%→62% open-source token-share series this page carries. Logged as a contradiction, unresolved — the difference may be developer-platform traffic (where open weights win on price) versus enterprise CFO procurement (where they lose on trust).**

**The levers CFOs actually named: model routing (preferred), EMPLOYEE USAGE CAPS, and ANSWER CACHING, with *"an active debate about what the cap should be [and] to whom they should apply."* Every single CFO was focused on token efficiency, *"with experimental or superfluous AI use now under scrutiny"* — a theme UBS says it has picked up since May/early June, *"so it's now prevalent."*

🔴 **THE MEASUREMENT PROBLEM IS THE STATED BOTTLENECK ON ADOPTION, not cost:** CFOs *"struggl[e] to measure the benefits or returns on their AI spend… via benchmarks or internal Evals."* UBS's own institution: the UBS AI head said *"the pace at which UBS is moving forward on AI is THROTTLED SOMEWHAT BY THE FACT THAT HE HAD NO GOOD WAY TO MEASURE THE RETURNS on that spend, that he was effectively **JUDGING THE MERITS BASED ON VIBES** and qualitative feedback."* ⚠️ **UBS calls solving this *"an unlock"* — i.e. the binding constraint on enterprise AI spend right now is attribution infrastructure, not token price.**

⚠️ **AND THE VOLUME SIDE IS STILL VERTICAL, WHICH IS WHY UBS IS NOT BEARISH: *"AI token spend continues, DESPITE these efforts… We heard anecdotes of a **20x USAGE RAMP PER DEVELOPER SINCE YEAR END 25**. Planned **50 TO 100% AI SPENDING GROWTH plans through year end. NOBODY WAS THROTTLING THEIR AI INITIATIVES.**"* UBS's synthesis: the behaviour is *"probably bending the revenue growth curve a bit lower for the frontier labs… you're basically downtiering to lower ASP models. But… the overall TAM and AI spending trends are still so strong that all AI industry participants would likely continue to see robust growth. **So, token optimization efforts have NOT TURNED US INTO AI BEARS.**"*

### 2. 🔴 Model neutrality just stopped being a viable position — [[OPENAI]] cut Cursor off

> *"OpenAI announced that it will **END ACCESS TO GPT MODELS THROUGH CURSOR**. Post Cursor's merger with **SPACEX** and the launch of **GROK 4.5**… It means that Cursor is **NO LONGER A PLATFORM THROUGH WHICH USERS CAN ACCESS ANY MODEL**."*

🔴 **THE GENERAL RULE UBS DERIVES, and it is a structural statement this dossier should carry as a standing principle: *"MODEL NEUTRALITY IS THEREFORE BECOMING THE EXCLUSIVE DOMAIN OF SOFTWARE FIRMS THAT ELECT NOT TO BUILD A COMPETING FIRST-PARTY MODEL WITH FRONTIER LEVEL CAPABILITIES."*** Neutrality and first-party model ambition are now mutually exclusive positions in the stack — a distribution partner that acquires a rival frontier model gets cut off.

⚠️ **Two incumbents immediately claimed the shift favours them, and UBS declines to endorse either: [[MSFT]] argued the customer pivot to non-frontier models *"is an AMAZINGLY ATTRACTIVE WORLD FOR MICROSOFT"*; [[PLTR]] argues *"a shift away from frontier lab models is MASSIVELY TAM EXPANDING."* Keirstead: *"I want to dig in more to appreciate why."* Both are counterparties' framings of a narrowing of OpenAI's distribution surface — log the claims, not the conclusion.**

### 3. 🔴 The rev-per-GW spread — $10bn to $30-50bn, and the catches that stop it being a series

> *"[[ORCL]] put their **$10 BILLION PER GIGAWATT** number in our heads last fall. More recently, **SPACEX AND [[NBIS]] HAVE SAID THAT THEY'RE SIGNING NEW DEALS AT A $30 TO $50 BILLION PER GIGAWATT LEVEL.**"*

⚠️ **UBS ATTACHES TWO CATCHES AND THEY ARE LOAD-BEARING — this is exactly the kind of figure that gets chained into a run-rate and shouldn't be:**
1. **These are [[NVDA]] Vera Rubin clusters** — *"we need to be more thoughtful about how this pending GPU mix, **H100s to Blackwells to Vera Rubins**, are going to CASCADE into hyperscaler growth rates."* A generational ASP effect, not a market-wide reprice.
2. **These are SHORT-TERM, ~SIX-MONTH deals** *"priced at closer to **SPOT RATES** rather than long-term contracted rates."*

🔴 **AND THE SELLERS' MOTIVES DIFFER, which is the useful discriminator: *"SpaceX told us that they're doing the six-month deals FOR FLEXIBILITY, as they want the OPTION of allocating 2027 capacity to Grok. NEBIUS APPEARS TO BE DOING THIS FOR PURE ASP LIFT."* [[MSFT]] *"will NOT chase short-term deals, that Amy has NO INTEREST IN DE FACTO SHORT-TERM PRICE GOUGING"* — while separately being *"clear that they WILL"* mark contracts to market as they roll off. [[CRWV]] reported **5-10pts of contribution margin expansion on new contracts signed in 2Q**, with *"the spread… widen[ing] further in the months since"* (Deutsche Bank, 2026-08-31).**

**UBS's conclusion: *"I want to be more thoughtful about where on that revenue per gigawatt spectrum, $10 billion to $30 billion, the hyperscalars are moving to as it could have a **PROFOUND EFFECT ON OUR AZURE, AWS, AND ORACLE OCI ESTIMATES. THOSE FIGURES SHOULD BE BIASED UP GIVEN GPU PRICING ALONE.**"* ⚠️ **Cross-file to the rev-per-GW ledger, and keep the $/watt discipline this wiki already applies: these are REVENUE per GW, not build cost per GW, and must never be netted against the $6.4-13.2mn/MW construction-cost marks in [ai-datacenter-power](ai-datacenter-power.md).**

### 4. The open-weight thesis, from the other side — and note it moved multiples, not estimates

**Wells Fargo (2026-08-12) argues open models are net POSITIVE for incumbent platforms: *"lower cost of intelligence INCREASES VALUE OF INCUMBENT DATA & CONTEXT AT LOWER MARGINAL COST (+ve systems of record)… + LOWER RISK OF 'MODEL TAKE ALL' MARKET,"* naming **[[MSFT]] and [[NOW]] as *"most (+)"***. WF's framing of the industry shift is the phrase to carry: 🔴 ***"TOKEN-MAXXING SHIFTING TO VALUE-MAXXING"*** — its CIO survey finds customers *"increasingly willing to shift towards **'GOOD-ENOUGH' OPEN-SOURCE MODELS**, complementing costlier frontier models with a more-balanced tradeoff of cost vs performance… ultimately +ve for AI consumers."*

**PT changes (all with FY27/FY28 EPS explicitly UNCHANGED — "NC"): MSFT $650→$700 (OW) · [[NOW]] $160→$175 (OW) · SAP €165→€210 (OW) · [[CRM]] $200→$205 (EW) · WDAY $185→$215 (OW).** ⚠️ **WF states the mechanism itself: *"increasing terminal value of incumbent platforms improving MULTIPLES (thus increasing PTs…)."* Every one of these is a re-rating call with zero estimate revision — worth flagging because a five-name PT sweep reads like a fundamental upgrade cycle and is not one.**

**Context WF cites: open-model development picking up with **Kimi K3, GLM 5.2, DeepSeek V4**, and renewed US focus (Meta, Thinking Machines, NVIDIA), *"most notably NVDA's July 24 letter (signed by MSFT, [[META]], [[PLTR]], NOW, IBM)."*** ⚠️ **The offsetting call: cheaper intelligence is *"crowding out smaller / narrower players (-ve point solutions)"* — *"value becomes less about the dev or UI itself but more in the DATA & BUSINESS CONTEXT."***

### 5. Agentic commerce gets its first traffic measurement — and it is under 1%

🔴 **[[GOOG]] launched agentic hotel booking in AI Mode with in-flow Google Pay checkout ([[BKNG]], Expedia, Hilton, Marriott, IHG, Priceline, Trip.com and others as launch partners; OTAs remain merchant of record). Crucially: *"for now, there are NO RANKING ADJUSTMENTS FROM ADVERTISING, OR CPA FEES ON THESE BOOKINGS"*, with BofA expecting Google to add ad-based rankings and/or a CPA fee later.**

⚠️ **AND THE SIZE OF THE AGENTIC CHANNEL, MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSERTED (SimilarWeb, via BofA): *"As of Jul'26, **Gen-AI platforms contributed about 0.6% of the OTA's combined web traffic share** (accelerating vs **0.3% in Jan'26**)."* Over Apr-23 → Jul-26 the real channel shift was elsewhere: **Organic Search 36.3% → 30.7%, Direct 26.9% → 25.7%, Affiliates 11.7% → 15.7%, Paid Search FLAT at 14.9%.*** **This is the most concrete measurement on this page of how much commerce has actually moved to an agentic surface: doubling in six months, off a sub-1% base. Useful as a discipline against every "agents are eating the funnel" claim in the corpus.**

- 🔴 **TOKEN SPEND AS A SHARE OF HUMAN COMPENSATION — the most directly usable benchmark here (a16z portfolio, per the host): AI-native companies at *"high single digits, some at 10%+"*; *"old economy"* companies doing a good job at **~1%**; and within any company **the highest-spending engineers spend 10-100x the median engineer.**** Baker's own firm: **internal token consumption up ~100x from March to August**, and after getting Grokbot Enterprise, *"with two people using it, it looks like token spend might 10 or 20x in a month."* ➤ **The 1% → 10% spread is the adoption gap in one number, and it is checkable against enterprise budget disclosures. Treat the 100x/10-20x firm-level figures as anecdote from a small, unusually intense user base.**

The stated motive is IP control: **sharing your enterprise context with a frontier lab *"may be hazardous for your financial health,"*** sharpened by ZDR-policy shifts. Reported instance: **Grokbot itself is said to run Gemini 3.7 Flash + Grok 4.6 + some Opus behind a router.** Contenders named for that layer: **[[MSFT]], Databricks, Snowflake, [[PLTR]], [[CRM]]/Workday, the inference providers (Fireworks Nexus called the best broad instantiation today), and the vertical agents (Harvey, Cognition).** ⚠️ **Why it is harder than it sounds — and the caveat that makes this useful rather than promotional: *"coding is unique compared to everything else in knowledge work because it is verifiable and perfectly documented"*; legal is *"somewhat"* similar; **the remaining 1.5bn knowledge workers are neither, so the messy majority of the TAM does not inherit the coding playbook.** Category-size tell: **Kirkland & Ellis reportedly committing ~$500m to build this in-house** — validating the prize while illustrating the trap, since *"it's not a $500 million one-time build"* (the base model must be continuously swapped and re-tuned).

## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 29, /run-inbox — **THE KV CACHE IS THE PHYSICAL MECHANISM BEHIND THIS THEME, AND IT COMPOUNDS ON *TWO* AXES AT ONCE**)

🔴🔴 **BERNSTEIN SUPPLIES THE HARDWARE EXPLANATION THIS THEME HAS BEEN ARGUING AROUND: inference splits into two regimes with opposite bottlenecks. *"Inference can be divided into PREFILL and DECODE, with prefill 'COMPUTE-BOUND' and XPUs & HBM inside XPUs more important. DECODE is on the other hand 'MEMORY-BOUND', esp. with KV CACHE SIZE COMPOUNDING QUICKLY WITH BOTH TOKEN COUNT & CONCURRENT USER COUNT."***

➤ **WHY THAT IS THE KEY SENTENCE FOR THIS THEME: agentic workloads raise token count AND concurrency SIMULTANEOUSLY, so KV-cache pressure compounds on two axes at once rather than scaling linearly with tokens. "More tokens" is therefore not a linear cost curve — it is a memory-capacity wall, and it lands on DECODE, which is the serving side of the economics. Every token-growth chart on this page should be read against a KV-cache cost that grows faster than the token count itself.**

**Bernstein names the three ways out, and they map exactly onto the architecture race now underway: *"EXPANDING HBM CAPACITY at an affordable cost, OFFLOADING TO CONVENTIONAL DRAM & NAND, and/or CREATING ADDITIONAL MEMORY TIERS among them are becoming a key focus for innovations"* — i.e. HBF ([[SNDK]]/[[SKHYNIX]]), HBC ([[QCOM]]), CXL and "Storage Next", CMX, and zHBM ([[SAMSUNG]]). Also relevant: **agentic workflow *"first triggers many non-AI tasks for CPU & system memory"*, and RAG database SEARCH *"relies on system DRAM more"* — so agentic AI pulls CONVENTIONAL memory, not only HBM.** Full architecture map on [hbm-memory](hbm-memory.md).** (Bernstein · Mark Li / Mark C. Newman / Edward Hou / Phoebe Sun, "Global Memory: An AI memory primer", 2026-08-28) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_SNDK_Global_Memory_An_AI_memory_primer.html)

### 🔴 THE PRICE OF A TOKEN JUST GOT ATTACKED FROM ABOVE — [[META]] TO UNDERCUT THE FRONTIER LABS BY 75%

**Wells Fargo (Gawrelski, 07-09) reports that **META *"will launch model API system PRICED AT 25% THE COST of leading [[ANTHROPIC]] and [[OPENAI]] offerings"***, alongside Muse Spark 1.1 with paid developer tiers and *"standout"* agentic improvements — with Zuckerberg calling the model *"better than we expected"* and reports indicating it outperforms leading Gemini models.** ➤ **This is the most direct pricing threat to frontier-lab API economics logged on this wiki. A well-capitalised entrant with its own silicon and idle capacity pricing at a 75% discount changes the unit-economics debate from "does inference cost fall with scale" to "is inference pricing set by cost or by a competitor's strategic subsidy." Held as a stated intention, not a shipped price sheet.**

## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 28, /run-inbox — **THE FIRST SELL-SIDE P&L SHAPE FOR A FRONTIER LAB, AND AN EX-BEDROCK PM WHO SAYS THE HYPERSCALER IS THE *CHEAP* ROUTE TO A FRONTIER MODEL**) — 🔴🔴 **THIS PAGE HAS MEASURED TOKENMAXXING IN PRICES, INDICES AND LAB DISCLOSURES. TWO SOURCES THIS WEEK GIVE IT AN *ACCOUNTING* — BARCLAYS MODELS WHERE EVERY $100 OF LAB REVENUE ACTUALLY LANDS, AND AN OPERATOR EXPLAINS WHY ENTERPRISES ROUTE 90/10 THROUGH THE CLOUDS RATHER THAN THE LABS.**

### 🔴 (1) BARCLAYS: THE UNIT-ECONOMICS PRIMER — AND THE GROSS-vs-NET TRAP THAT BREAKS EVERY ARR COMPARISON ON THIS PAGE

Barclays' first attempt to standardise AI-lab financials starts from a warning this page should adopt: *"We expect some confusion as AI labs start reporting GAAP financials, as business mix and revenue recognition can vary. Similar to how UBER and LYFT are in the same core business yet gross bookings and net revenue have different definitions, comparing AI labs may prove difficult."*

- **The two archetypes (Barclays never names them; the identifications below are this wiki's inference).** **"Lab A"** ≈ API-weighted (~70% API / ~30% subscription in '26), no strategic-partner revenue share → reads as [[ANTHROPIC]]. **"Lab B"** ≈ subscription-weighted (~80% / ~20%), pays a 20%-of-revenue strategic partner → reads as [[OPENAI]] with [[MSFT]]. ⚠️ **Treat as a framework, not a disclosure.**
- **🔴 THE MARGIN TRANSFORMATION IS THE HEADLINE, AND IT IS ENORMOUS.** Paid-inference margin **'25 → '26: Lab A 14% → 65%, Lab B 18% → 48%.** Gross margin after final-training cost: **Lab A 4% → 55%, Lab B 8% → 38%.** Inference compute cost falls from **79%/61% of revenue to 30%/30%.** Cause per Barclays: *"Model Efficiencies, Pricing Power, and Shift Towards API/Enterprise"* — *"AI lab unit economics have improved greatly in '26 (vs. '25) driven by agentic workflows and enterprise becoming the 'must buy' products."*
- **⚠️ BUT THE COST THAT MATTERS IS PARKED BELOW THE LINE.** Barclays allocates only **~10% of total training cost to COGS** (the final production run); the remainder sits in R&D at **70 (Lab A) and 96 (Lab B) per $100 of revenue in '26.** ➜ **A "55% gross margin" lab is still deeply loss-making. Any bull argument on this page that cites frontier gross margin must carry the R&D training line beside it.**
- **🔴 THE GROSS-vs-NET ASYMMETRY, WHICH IS THE MOST ACTIONABLE ITEM HERE.** **Lab A recognises indirect API GROSS; Lab B recognises it NET of fees and "may exclude revenue recognition entirely when its strategic partner runs indirect API."** ➜ **Every Anthropic-vs-OpenAI ARR comparison on this page is comparing a gross-basis number to a net-basis one, in a direction that FLATTERS Lab A. Barclays: as indirect grows "it likely creates distortion between reported revenues, and hence can distort the narrative around relative AI lab progress."**
- **The pass-through to the clouds, sized:** *"around **$35-$40 of every $100 in AI Lab revenue flows to hyperscaler revenue** in '26, on which the hyperscalers generate nearly **$10-$20 in OI** (at high **~35%-45% operating margin**)."* By lab: **Lab A $35 at 34% margin; Lab B $41 at 47%** — the difference is the partner fee, *"but the actual profit on a per token basis is likely the same excluding these fees."*
- **The industry ladder ($bn, Barclays estimates):** AI-lab revenue **7 / 26 / 137 / 376 / 690** ('24/'25/'26E/'27E/'28E); year-end AI ARR **44 / 200 / 453 / 782**; inference cost **4 / 17 / 58 / 157 / 292** (settling at 42% of revenue); training cost **7 / 19 / 66 / 132 / 210** (96% → 30% of revenue); hyperscaler AI revenue **11 / 36 / 124 / 289 / 502**, i.e. **153% → 73% of AI-lab revenue.** *"Right now, just about every dollar of AI lab ARR is finding its way to the hyperscaler revenue line, because of the high mix of training relative to inference in '26."*
- **🔴 A DATED SHARE-SHIFT CALL:** *"Hyperscalers likely start to lose market share of AI lab training and inference starting in **'28** when backstopped AI infrastructure projects come online and become the first option for AI labs."*
- **Subscription is the structurally worse business:** *"Subscription products (like Claude Code or Codex) generally have LOWER inference margins than API as some users benefit from AI labs' willingness to SUBSIDIZE TOKEN COST to remain competitive and retain users"* — with usage caps *"reset from time to time (we have seen an increase in cap resets of late, likely in response to churn reduction efforts and model efficiency gains)."* ➜ **Cap resets as a churn instrument is a new, observable tokenmaxxing tell for this page.**

(Barclays "U.S. Internet: A Primer on AI Lab & AI Hyperscaler Unit Economics", Ross Sandler / Alex Hughes / Michael DiSanto / Alexander Kessinger, 2026-08-28.) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/US_Internet_A_Primer_on_AI_Lab_AI_Hyperscaler_Unit_Economics_US_Internet_A_Primer_on_AI_Lab_AI_Hyperscaler_Unit.html)

### 🔴 (2) THE EX-AWS BEDROCK PM: ENTERPRISES GO **90/10** THROUGH THE CLOUDS — AND BEDROCK IS *CHEAPER* THAN BUYING FROM THE LAB

An AlphaSense expert call (buy-side-led) with a former Product Manager Technical at AWS who bootstrapped Bedrock, and previously built agents on Google Vertex, ran PyTorch/AI-infra at Meta and spent a decade at Microsoft (Azure ML). ⚠️ **Single expert, left AWS in 2024; directional, not disclosure.**

- **🔴 ROUTING: *"Enterprises are, I would say, mostly **90/10** that they will go to Azure, Vertex, or Bedrock to get their API access and NOT go to OpenAI and Anthropic."*** Reasons: multi-geography support, and network stability *"like six or eight nines that Azure has, or AWS has spent years building."*
- **🔴 AND THE PRICE RUNS THE *WRONG* WAY vs THE RESALE-MARKUP ASSUMPTION:** *"AWS doesn't charge any premium. They get discounts from Anthropic, and that's how they're trying to make money… customers get **blanket commitment-based discounts** on everything they spend on cloud. And they get that discount if they go to Bedrock; they don't get it if they go to Anthropic today. So **it's actually cheaper to go to Bedrock than to run an API through Anthropic** for enterprises."* ➜ **⚠️ THIS SITS IN TENSION WITH BARCLAYS' SAME-WEEK MODEL, which carries "Indirect Hyperscaler Fees" at 20% of customer spend. Reconcilable if the 20% is a WHOLESALE SPREAD rather than a markup on list — but the two are not obviously the same number and this page should not treat them as one. Flagged in [`../_meta/reconciliation-2026-08-28.md`](../_meta/reconciliation-2026-08-28.md).**
- **Open-weights hosting is NOT free, and the rent is quantified:** hyperscalers pay large open-source publishers **10-30% revenue share** (*"15%-20%, 30% is what we have negotiated in the past, or what I have seen in public"*) — named: Minimax, GLM 5.2, Kimi, DeepSeek. Azure differs structurally: *"Azure also had an agreement with **Hugging Face**, so they do pay Hugging Face a blanket fee"*, giving Foundry a long tail at no marginal licence cost. ➜ **Corroborates and PRICES this page's standing "open weights are not free" finding.**
- **🔴 THE SUBSTITUTION SPREAD, FROM THE SELL SIDE OF IT:** *"the cost that these models are charging is **$0.50** [GLM-5.3]… versus **$1** that GPT-5.6 comes in… Anthropic… can charge for the same category of model, **$2 or $2.50**. So five times more than what GLM 5.3 gets you."* And **"GLM 5.3's claim is that they are as good or at least good enough like Opus 5 or GPT-5.6."**
- **BUT THE HYPERSCALER MARGIN ON OPEN WEIGHTS IS FINE:** *"the margins are not as bad as 10%. The margins are still healthy, like **50% margins, above 50% margins** in all of these"* — roughly **20% below frontier economics**, and *"that can be negotiated… nobody wants to spend that time and energy in negotiation because the overall number is not big yet."* Cost driver: hardware is optimised for only *"two or three models"*, so open weights burn more FLOPS per unit of work.
- **A STRATEGIC DISCLOSURE ON WHY THE CHANNEL DIVERSIFIED AT ALL:** *"before two quarters ago, Bedrock only had Anthropic as the largest, biggest lab… they were worried mostly that… they have no power to negotiate with them on pricing. So they were incentivizing large models [Llama, OpenAI]… to get a second player in so that we can have negotiation power with Anthropic."* Now *"with Grok, GPT, and Claude all available, I don't think there is much incentive now."* ➜ **The multi-model shelf on this page was built as a PROCUREMENT weapon, and it has already done its work.**
- **Demand, unquantified but stark:** *"inference demand from customers has gone **10X every half-year**, and that is underserved today."* And on the labs' own-infrastructure push: *"it's a **hedge strategy rather than real all-in**"* — on Anthropic becoming its own hyperscaler, *"I think it will be **2040**."*

(AlphaSense expert call, former Product Manager Technical at AWS (Bedrock), buy-side-led, 2026-08-27.) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/--_-_ExpertCall_document_dated_27-08-2026_1.html)

### 📚 BACKFILL — Morgan Stanley's agentic-commerce TAM (2026-07-19, ~6 weeks stale; supersedes nothing above)

MS's 99-page "Global eCommerce: The Agentic Era" is the demand-side counterpart to the token economics on this page: **>20% of eCommerce GMV materially agent-influenced within five years, ~8% with higher-level agent task execution and ~2% autonomous agent-to-agent**, driving a **~6% five-year TAM uplift (bear ~1.5% / bull ~15%)** on a **US$7tn 2030 eCommerce opportunity**. The tokenmaxxing-relevant finding is the cost test: **"incremental contribution profit covering LLM costs by ~2-3x outside our stress case"** — i.e. at current token prices, agentic commerce clears its own inference bill with room. Adoption anchors: **Amazon's Rufus at 300mn+ customers with users "60% more likely to complete a purchase"**; Alibaba Qwen ~140mn. Counterweight: **"GenAI platforms account for <0.5% of average eCommerce web traffic today."** (Morgan Stanley "Global eCommerce — The Agentic Era", Ruben / Nowak / Feather et al., 2026-07-19.) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/LATAM_20260719_2200.html)

📚 **AND THE ANCESTOR OF THAT NOTE IS NOW ON DISK — Morgan Stanley, "Internet: Agentic Shoppers Are Coming… Who Could Win or Lose?", published 2025-11-17 (Nathan Feather / Brian Nowak / Simeon Gutman et al.). ⚠️⚠️ TEN MONTHS STALE, recovered from a P: drive backlog, logged at its REAL publication date: it SUPERSEDES NOTHING on this page, displaces no block above, updates no current-state number and moved nothing to § Changelog.** **The 2025-vintage frame, for dating purposes only: agentic commerce as "the next substantial GenAI-enabled unlock", adding UP TO ~$115bn / ~6% TO 2030 E-COMMERCE SPEND; a "5 I's" retailer framework — Inventory, Infrastructure, Innovation, Incrementality, Income Statement — focused on incrementality and profitability (positive AMZN/WMT/M, negative LULU/FIVE); and within digital advertising, LEADING REACH PLATFORMS BECOME MORE VALUABLE while retail media and the open web are at risk (positive [[META]]/APP, mixed TTD).** ➤ **WHY IT IS WORTH THE SPACE, GIVEN IT CHANGES NOTHING: it is the ORIGIN of the framework this wiki has been debating for months — the July-2026 note above is its successor, and the [[SHOP]], [[AMZN]] and [[GOOG]] pages already argue its conclusions. Having the dated ancestor turns "the agentic-commerce debate" into a MEASURABLE ten-month track: the TAM uplift held (~6% then, ~6% in the July note), the adoption assumption was ~50% of e-commerce shoppers using agents by 2030 at ~25% of their spend, and the take-rate question MS raised then — current effective search take rates ~5-10x higher than OpenAI's early low-to-high-single-digit agentic commission — is STILL the open question in 2026. The debate has moved on traffic and on partnerships; it has NOT moved on monetisation.** ⚠️ **Every figure here is 2025 vintage and is recorded as such, not adopted. ⚠️ The PDF text extraction drops leading characters on tickers ("MZN"=AMZN, "OOGL"=GOOGL, "ETA"=META, "SFT"=MSFT); all tickers were decoded from surrounding prose before quoting.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/INTERNET_20251117_2110.html)
