**Source: [[CRM]] — Mike Spencer, Deputy CFO / EVP Finance, at the Deutsche Bank 2026 Technology Conference, 2026-08-27** (Bloomberg *INITIAL DRAFT* transcript — ⚠️ draft, ASR risk on names). Corroborating: **Atlassian (TEAM) IR hosted investor call, 2026-08-26** (⚠️ off-coverage, no wiki page); **UBS · Karl Keirstead** post-print callback, 2026-08-27. [CRM/DB](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Salesforce_Inc_Conf_Presentation_Call_2026827_DN000000003117875164pdf.html) · [TEAM IR](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_27_team_ir_meeting_aug_26.html) · [UBS](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_27_salesforce_crm_veeva_veev_crowdstrike_crwd_okta_ok.html)

- 🔴🔴 **THE DISCLOSURE, VERBATIM, AND IT IS THE MOST CONCRETE TOKEN-COST DATAPOINT THIS PAGE HAS: *"we roughly about six months ago, we **unleashed Claude in our R&D cycle**. **It's part of the reason we DIDN'T RAISE MARGIN GUIDANCE ON THE YEAR is because we're COVERING SOME OF THE TOKEN SPEND that we've got going.**"*** ➤ **Salesforce reaffirmed a 34% FY27 non-GAAP operating margin rather than raising it, and inference cost is the named reason. Until now this page has had to infer enterprise token burden from vendor pricing and index data; here it is, on the record, as a guidance decision.** ⚠️ **NOT SIZED. "Some of the token spend" is the entire disclosure — no dollars, no basis points. Do not model a number from it.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE COUNTER-STRATEGY IS THE REAL NEWS FOR THIS PAGE, BECAUSE IT IS DEMAND DESTRUCTION AT THE FRONTIER TIER, ANNOUNCED BY A BUYER: *"we're going into the zone of **PRESCRIPTIVE MODEL CHOICE FOR TASK AT HAND**. And what that really means is **you DON'T NEED TO USE THE LATEST AND GREATEST MODEL for every single task**. Of course it's applicable in some use cases. But **for the LARGE MAJORITY, you're totally fine with the SECOND OR THIRD-GENERATION MODEL** that might be out there. That also includes **OPTIMIZATION ACROSS DIFFERENT VENDORS**. So internally, we've got **[[OPENAI]], we've got Cursor, we've got Claude. We're starting to experiment with Grok. ALL OF THEM HAVE DIFFERENT COST STRUCTURE.**"*** ➤ **This is the single clearest articulation on this wiki of the mechanism the tokenmaxxing thesis depends on: aggregate token VOLUME rises while the mix rotates DOWN the capability ladder and ACROSS vendors on price. It is exactly consistent with this page's standing synthesis — *"volume up, price per token down, share rotating."***
- 🔴 **THE FOUR NAMED GROSS-MARGIN LEVERS, WHICH TOGETHER ARE A ROADMAP FOR HOW APPLICATION VENDORS DEFEND MARGIN AGAINST INFERENCE COST:** management expects AI supply cost to stay *"relatively **NEUTRAL**"* via **(1) incremental PREMIUM MONETIZATION to cover the AI cost structure · (2) NEW SKUs · (3) DETERMINISTIC WORKFLOW — *"not having to pay an LLM if we don't need to, avoid the cost"* · (4) MODEL SELECTION — *"us getting into the zone ultimately, and you can see where the train goes, of **US MAKING THE SELECTION FOR CUSTOMERS on which model they're going to actually use** for whatever scenario."*** ➤ 🔴 **LEVER (4) IS THE STRUCTURALLY IMPORTANT ONE AND IT IS NEW TO THIS PAGE: the application layer is positioning itself as the MODEL ROUTER. If that becomes the norm, the frontier labs lose direct pricing contact with the end workload — the router, not the lab, decides which model runs. Lever (3) is arguably worse for the labs: it removes the inference call entirely.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE SELL-SIDE INDEPENDENTLY NAMED THE SAME FORCE AS A PRODUCT RISK ON THE SAME DAY — WHICH IS WHAT MAKES THIS A THEME RATHER THAN ONE COMPANY'S COST PROBLEM: on Claudeforce (launched 24 hours earlier with Claude as the DEFAULT model), UBS's Keirstead wrote that *"against a backdrop of **many customers looking to harness NON-FRONTIER MODELS for non-coding use cases**, there's also a risk that I need to vet — that **Claudeforce ends up being perceived as simply TOO EXPENSIVE. Essentially OVERKILL by leveraging Claude models.**"*** ➤ **The vendor's internal cost discipline and the analyst's product risk are the same fact seen from two ends. Note the tension inside Salesforce itself: its PRODUCT strategy points customers toward Claude while its MARGIN strategy points them down the stack.**
- ✅ **CORROBORATION FROM A SECOND, INDEPENDENT ENTERPRISE VENDOR — AND THIS ONE IS SELLING TOKEN EFFICIENCY AS A FEATURE.** Atlassian's IR, asked which of its two teamwork-graph claims — ***"44% BETTER ACCURACY"* or *"48% LOWER TOKEN USAGE"*** — actually wins enterprise deals today: *"certainly in this current moment in time, **there's a LOT OF FOCUS ON TOKEN EFFICIENCY and the TOKEN MAXXING THEME is out there. And a lot of organizations are certainly MUCH MORE CONSCIOUS OF THEIR TOKEN SPEND.** You certainly read it in the news and in the zeitgeist… that's very RESONANT right now."* The CEO, after meeting *"60-some customers across a couple different countries"*, reported that token efficiency *"is the **NUMBER ONE THING** I think customers understand."* ⚠️ **Self-serving — Atlassian is selling context efficiency — but the 48% figure is a specific, published claim and the "number one thing customers understand" is a channel observation from ~60 accounts.**
- **A supporting usage datapoint from the same source, which cuts the other way and should be held alongside: Atlassian reports **MCP calls +400% q/q**, **Jira issues created via MCP +4x q/q**, **Confluence pages via MCP +4x q/q**, and **Rovo assisted actions +50% q/q**, with MCP-using customers growing ARR at **2x** the rate of non-users. [[CRM]] reported the same week that **agentic use via MCP and CLI calls "surged SIXFOLD, 6x"**.** ➤ **Volume is exploding through the agentic interface at the same moment buyers are actively engineering the cost per call down. Both halves of this page's thesis got evidence in the same 48 hours.**

## 📚 Recent signals (Aug 27 — **NuttyCLD, INDEPENDENT SUBSTACK AUTHOR, NOT A BROKER**) — ⚠⚠ **THIS IS A *FREE PREVIEW*: THE TEXT ON DISK IS TRUNCATED AND THE AUTHOR'S ACTUAL VALUE-MIGRATION CONCLUSION IS PAYWALLED. WHAT SURVIVES IS THE MECHANISM — AND IT IS THE BEST PHYSICAL ACCOUNT THIS PAGE HAS OF *WHY* THE KV CACHE, NOT THE WEIGHTS, IS BECOMING THE DOMINANT COST OF A TOKEN.**

**Source: NuttyCLD (Substack) — "An Investor's Handbook IV - AI Inference (1): How Heterogeneous Computing Is Redrawing the Architecture of AI Inference", 2026-08-27** (arrived as a forward from the daniel.grozdea research hub). [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/inference.html)

⚠⚠ **READ THE PROVENANCE BEFORE THE CONTENT, THREE WAYS. ① **INDEPENDENT AUTHOR, NOT A BROKER — NO RATING, NO PRICE TARGET, NO MODEL, NO ESTIMATES.** The author's own disclaimer, verbatim: *"This is an **analysis of industry and technology structure, NOT INVESTMENT ADVICE**. The author **may hold positions** in securities mentioned."* ② **THE ARTICLE IS A FREE PREVIEW AND IS CUT OFF MID-ARGUMENT.** The on-disk text ends at *"NVIDIA and Groq are the best place to start reading that shift… the answer becomes a framework for judging **where value is likely to accumulate next across GPUs, memory, networking, and software**"* — and then: *"**Subscribe to Nutty to unlock the rest.**"* **The investment conclusion — who captures the margin — IS WITHHELD. Nobody reading this block later should mistake the truncation for the author's full argument, and no company page was patched from it.** ③ It is filed to this theme page **only**.

- 🔴 **THE CORE REFRAME, AND IT IS THE USEFUL PART: *"Do NOT read the inference era as an era that shrinks the GPU's place. The point is that **inference is being DECOMPOSED**"*** — into **prefill** (reading the question), **decode** (writing the answer), **KV cache** (the memory of the conversation) and **agentic orchestration** (directing the work). *"The pieces have grown identities too distinct to keep filing under the single name ‘inference’… **Read the market as PHASE-SPECIFIC INFRASTRUCTURE, not as GPU vs ASIC.** Inference is not becoming an ASIC market. It is becoming a **heterogeneous systems market**."* And the punchline the whole essay builds to: *"Memory is no longer something attached to the accelerator. **The memory architecture DETERMINES WHAT THE ACCELERATOR IS.**"* (NuttyCLD "An Investor's Handbook IV - AI Inference", 2026-08-27, free preview — truncated)
- 🔴 **THE ARITHMETIC BEHIND "DECODE IS MEMORY-BOUND", WHICH THIS PAGE HAS ASSERTED FOR WEEKS WITHOUT SHOWING: time per token ≈ **bytes to read ÷ memory bandwidth**. On 2023's standard equipment — an **H100 at 3.35TB/s** with a **70B model in FP16** — *"about **140GB of weights must be read in full for every token**, which works out to about **42ms of pure memory reading**… the most expensive waiting room in the world."* Run the same division on 2026 hardware and **42ms falls to 1.6ms**: *"the newest GPUs carry **nearly SEVEN TIMES** that machine's bandwidth, and pressing the model into **FP4** cuts the bytes to read to **a quarter**. A **26x improvement in three years** — **though 4x of it belongs to the NUMBER FORMAT, not the hardware**."* ⚠️ **That last clause is the discipline item: roughly a quarter of the "cost curve" this page tracks is a precision change, which is a one-time step, not a recurring slope.**
- 🔴 **AND THEN THE BOTTLENECK MOVES — THE NUMBERS THAT MAKE THE KV CACHE THE COST DRIVER: on a **70B-class model (Llama 3.1 70B configuration)**, *"a single **128K-token context produces about 40GB** of notes. **One user's long conversation eats HALF the memory of an H100 (80GB)**."* Under production batching the weights problem largely disappears and the KV problem does not: *"the model weights get **read once and shared by the whole batch**, so the per-request cost of reading weights drops sharply. The problem is the KV cache. **Each request's conversation history is its own, so KV must be read separately for every request.** As batches grow and conversations lengthen, **KV reads, rather than weights, become the biggest cost**."* ✅ **DIRECT CORROBORATION of the 08-29 KV-cache block and the 08-20 Solidigm company-primary already on this page — and it supplies the per-request arithmetic neither of them had.**
- **THE CONDITIONALITY, WHICH IS THE MOST TRANSFERABLE IDEA IN THE PIECE: *"So ‘decode is memory-bound’ is **NOT a fixed property stamped on a chip. It is a CONDITIONAL STATE that appears and disappears with the model, the batch, the context, and the SLO**"* — an SLO being *"the response promise a service commits to keeping, say ‘first word within a second, token gap under 100ms’."* And the design maxim: *"A good architecture does not remove every jam. It just **moves the jam to the cheapest, most predictable place** it can afford."* ➤ **Practical consequence for this page: any single "cost per token" or "margin per token" figure is only valid at a stated batch size, context length and SLO. Most of the numbers circulating on this page state none of the three.**
- **THE COST-METRIC ARGUMENT, AND IT IS THIS THEME'S NEXT UNIT: *"an agent plans, searches, runs code, reads the result, and reasons again. **One task becomes HUNDREDS of inference calls.** A 200ms delay per step looks trivial until it repeats **300 times and becomes a minute**."* Price trail as given: a GPT-3.5-quality query fell from **$20 per million tokens (Nov 2022) to $0.07 (Oct 2024)**, and *"as of **August 2026**, the light model of OpenAI's newest 5.6 generation (**gpt-5.6-luna**) sells for **$0.20 per million INPUT tokens** — a far better model than GPT-3.5 of four years ago, at **one hundredth of the price**."* ⚠️ **UNIT DISCIPLINE: $0.20/M is INPUT-only, not blended and not output — do not net it against this page's output-price series or the 08-24 "OpenAI cut GPT-5.6 output pricing 33%" mark.** ➤ **Conclusion: *"In a world where tokens get this cheap, the yardstick of competition does not stop at cost per token. What users buy is not tokens but the **solved query, the finished code**. The metric the agent era's hardware will eventually be graded on is **COST PER COMPLETED TASK**."***
- **THE PRIOR ART, WHICH IS CHECKABLE AND PRE-DATES THE 2026 SILICON: two 2024 papers reached the same conclusion — **Splitwise** (University of Washington + [[MSFT]]) reported ***2.35x the throughput within the same power and cost budget***, and **DistServe** (Peking University) ***up to 7.4x per-GPU throughput while holding the same latency terms***, both by separating prefill and decode into different machine pools. The **Sarathi** line showed *"without splitting hardware at all, **scheduling alone** — chunking prefill finely and interleaving it between decode steps — can cut the interference dramatically."* ⚠️ **The author's own caveat, and it is the right one: *"‘The two phases are different’ and ‘you must have two kinds of chips’ are NOT the same sentence. Today’s inference software stacks, in fact, use both approaches together."***
- **THE COMMERCIAL EVIDENCE ON THE PAGE'S "WHO CAPTURES IT" QUESTION — reported facts, not the author's conclusion (which is paywalled): on **December 24, 2025** [[NVDA]] *"signed the largest deal in its history with **Groq** (**about $20 billion by press accounts**)"*; then on **August 24, 2026**, the opening day of Hot Chips, NVIDIA announced *"the **Groq 3 LPX rack had entered MASS PRODUCTION**, named the cloud provider **Nebius ([[NBIS]]) as its FIRST CUSTOMER**, and noted that **the cloud business of Groq, the very company that sold the technology, would be among the early buyers**."* NVIDIA's own framing on stage the next day, setting the LPX rack beside the Rubin GPU: *"**the GPU reads the question, the LPU writes the answer**… The ecosystem is heading toward **interlocking parts rather than mutually exclusive competition**."* ⚠️ **Prices, volumes and rack economics: NONE given. The $20bn is explicitly *"by press accounts."***
- ⚠️ **AND THE HONEST OPEN QUESTION THE AUTHOR LEAVES STANDING, WHICH IS THE RIGHT NOTE TO FILE THIS UNDER: *"**Separation has a price.** The moment you split the hardware, the **tens of gigabytes of KV cache** the prefill machine produced must move to the decode machine, the two pools must be load-balanced, and when one side dies it must be recovered. It is a **tug-of-war between the costs specialization saves and the costs coordination creates**. Whether inference's future really goes heterogeneous depends on how that tug-of-war ends, and **IT HAS NOT ENDED**. What we are watching now is its first large-scale experiment."* ➤ **So this source does NOT resolve the GPU-vs-ASIC-vs-memory value question for this page. It reframes it as a systems question and supplies the physics. Treat the framework as adopted, the destination as open, and the author's own answer as unread — because it is behind the paywall.**

## Recent signals (Aug 25 — Invest Like the Best EP488 / Sail Research) — 🔴 **THE 1,000× TITLE IS A WORKLOAD-SEGMENTATION THESIS, NOT A CURRENT PRICE CLAIM: MOVE AGENTS OFF THE HUMAN LATENCY LOOP, THEN MONETIZE CHEAPER CHIPS, FAILURE-PRONE SITES AND INTERRUPTIBLE POWER.**

- **Source:** Invest Like the Best EP488, Patrick O'Shaughnessy with Neil Movva, founder/CEO of Sail Research, "From Transistor to Token", 2026-08-25. ⚠️ **Interested-party founder testimony: Sail sells the low-cost inference architecture being advocated; forecasts and cost claims are not independently verified.**
- 🔴 **THE PRODUCT SHIFT:** Movva expects workloads to move from roughly **50/50 background vs real-time by end-2026 toward ~90/10 background long term**. For work running hours or days, **~10 tokens/sec may be acceptable where a waiting user would demand ~100 tokens/sec**; batching can therefore maximize throughput rather than latency. Deep research, autonomous cybersecurity testing and proactive personal agents are the named use cases.
- 🔴 **THE DEMAND CLAIM:** background agents remove the human-attention ceiling and can spend tokens without a guaranteed return. Movva says a trillion tokens costs **at least ~$5mn at current OpenAI pricing**, while some smaller-model implementations are approaching **tens of thousands of dollars**; he ultimately asks for **three to six orders of magnitude** of further cost reduction. ⚠️ **The YouTube headline compresses that ambition to "1,000x cheaper"; the interview itself gives a 1,000x-to-1,000,000x desired range, not an achieved reduction.**
- **THE ECONOMIC MECHANISM:** the service can tolerate uncontrolled tail latency and even low-availability infrastructure because failed work is rescheduled while the customer is asleep. That converts otherwise-discounted chips, distributed sites and intermittent power into tokens. The correct unit of value should migrate from token count toward completed outcomes as agents self-administer token budgets.
- **THE MODEL-LAYER CONSEQUENCE:** closed labs pay to stay roughly **three to six months ahead**, but Movva doubts enterprise deployments move fast enough to preserve that premium indefinitely; open-weight capabilities diffuse through public model outputs and AI-generated code even without deliberate scraping. ➜ **This corroborates the page's price/mix thesis: the model-layer premium can compress while aggregate inference volume expands.**
- **WHAT WOULD FALSIFY IT:** background-agent share fails to rise; low-latency interaction remains the dominant paid use case; retries and state migration erase the hardware/power discount; or outcome-based pricing cannot cover long-tail token consumption.

## Recent signals (Aug 18, /wiki-ingest) — **"OPEN WEIGHTS" ARE NOT FREE, AND AI MARGINAL COST IS A DISTRIBUTION, NOT ONE NUMBER**

**Ben Thompson splits inference into two businesses.** A simple consumer query can cost only marginally more than serving a web page; a test-time-scaled task that reasons for hours or days carries direct, rising marginal cost. Open weights remove the model-builder's R&D charge, not the cost of running inference, so the relevant comparison is all-in cost per completed task. That heterogeneity explains three downstream business-model moves: consumer AI gravitates to advertising; heavy enterprise users force usage pricing beyond a seat bundle; and routers/down-tiering become structural rather than tactical. It also sharpens the frontier-lab question: self-improvement can lower cost-to-serve and widen a lead, but only if revenue compounds before the capital curve runs out (Ben Thompson, *Invest Like the Best*, 2026-08-18).

The closed/open ratio went from ~10.3x to ~6.3x in one quarter. **That ratio is the cleanest single metric this page has for whether frontier labs can earn a return on the compute they are contracting.** See [[OPENAI]], [[ANTHROPIC]].**

### 🔴 THE SUPPLY-SIDE PROOF ARRIVED THE SAME WEEK, PRICED AT ~3% OF CLAUDE OPUS AND RUN ON CHINESE SILICON
**Z.ai released **GLM-5.3-Flash** (previously circulating anonymously on OpenRouter as *"Ox Alpha"*): a **320B-parameter MoE with 18B active**, natively multimodal, **1M-token context**, **MIT licence**, weights on Hugging Face, and ***"developed and run ENTIRELY ON CHINESE AI CHIPS."*** Priced **$0.15/M input · $0.50/M output · $0.03/M cached input** — *"among the cheapest on the market"* — and on Z.ai's own Code Bench *"performs **ON PAR WITH CLAUDE OPUS 4.8 ($5/$25 pricing)**."* Attributed to *"architectural enhancements and an optimised pre-training corpus that deliver **greater intelligence with LESS COMPUTE**"* (Rothschild & Co Redburn, 2026-08-27). ⚠️ *Vendor benchmark on the vendor's own harness; the parity claim is unverified. The pricing and the Chinese-silicon claim are the falsifiable parts.*
➤ **$0.50/M output against Opus at $25/M output is a 50x gap on list. Even discounting the benchmark heavily, that is the mechanism behind SIG's −40%.**

### 🔴 AND THE DEMAND SIDE HAS ALREADY REORGANISED AROUND CHEAPEST-TOKEN SOURCING
- ***"The expert highlights a **meaningful shift in how companies think about AI spend over the last six months, with the focus moving AWAY FROM TRAINING and fine-tuning their own models TOWARD FINDING THE CHEAPEST SOURCE OF TOKENS AT SCALE.** As bills from frontier [labs]…"*** — an expert interview on why neoclouds are expanding beyond compute to compete with hyperscalers ([[AMZN]], [[MSFT]], [[CRWV]], [[NBIS]]) (@AlphaSenseInc, corpus, 2026-08-26).
- 🔴 **AND THE WORKLOAD MIX ARGUMENT THAT DECIDES WHICH SILICON WINS: *"Neil thinks **90% of AI workloads will run in the BACKGROUND versus real time.** In that future, **LATENCY MATTERS LESS AND COST MATTERS MORE**, and he's configured his company around a unique way of serving tokens at the cheapest possible price. **'There's no bad chips. There's only bad pricing, and I will make any chip work at the right price.'**"*** (@patrick_oshag relaying an inference operator, corpus, 2026-08-26.) ➤ **If 90% of tokens are background, the marginal buyer optimises $/token and not time-to-first-token — which is bearish latency-optimised silicon (including [[NVDA]]'s Groq 3 LPX, positioned by management for *"ultra-low-latency interactive inference where speed is prioritised over throughput and cost per token"*) and bullish throughput-per-dollar ASICs and older-generation GPUs.**

### ✅ THE COUNTERWEIGHT — THE BUYER OF COMPUTE SAYS THE TOKENS ARE PROFITABLE, AND THE MIX JUST CROSSED INTO AGENTIC
- **[[NVDA]] management, Q2 FY27 call (2026-08-26): *"**frontier labs sales are skyrocketing, their margins are fantastic… they're generating PROFITABLE TOKENS… they're only limited by the amount of compute**."* Jensen's three-point framing: *"1) AI is now doing productive and useful work; 2) **AI is generating profitable tokens**; and 3) **if we had more compute, we could generate more profitable tokens**."***
- 🔴 **THE MIX CROSSOVER, DATED: *"the vast majority of AI is prompted by people, but I believe that **THIS LAST MONTH IT HAS CROSSED, and most AI are now AGENTIC**"*** (Jensen, via Vital Knowledge, 2026-08-26). **And the multiplier: *"the amount of compute necessary for an agent versus a human using it is probably **15 TO 100 TIMES**, depending on the type of problem"*** (logged from the same call).
➤ ⚠️ **THE TENSION THIS PAGE SHOULD HOLD OPEN: the compute vendor says token economics are healthy and volumes are exploding; the measured price series says the frontier premium is collapsing. **Both can be true if VOLUME growth outruns PRICE decline — which is precisely the bet inside NVDA's +70% FY28 guide and inside every $45bn-plus compute contract signed this month.** The falsifiable test is revenue per token at the labs, and no lab discloses it.**

### THE OTHER ITEMS FROM THE WINDOW
- ⚠️ **A CAPABILITY-THRESHOLD CLAIM THAT WOULD COMMODITISE THE FRONTIER IF RIGHT: *"'Sam Altman believes OpenAI will have an internal system that will qualify as AGI by the end of 2026.' **So, 10T-15T is all u need for AGI. This also means that a bunch of companies will have an AGI-LEVEL MODEL BY H1 2027 in the US & China**"* (@zephyr_z9, corpus, 2026-08-26, ~123k views). ➤ *An inference, not a datapoint — but it is the logical endpoint of the price series above: if the capability bar is a compute bar, capability is purchasable and only cost differentiates.*

That is the clearest available picture of what open-weight token economics actually earn — and it is the number to hold against every frontier-lab valuation on this wiki.**

See [china-export.md](china-export.md).**
